# List 12

## Unit 1

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>harsh</b></td><td>/hɑːrʃ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>严厉的</td><td>harsh criticism<br>严厉的批评</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The judge's harsh sentence surprised even the prosecutors in the courtroom.  法官严厉的判决连法庭上的检察官都感到意外。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 afflicting</td><td>/əˈflɪktɪŋ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>使痛苦的，折磨人的</td><td>an afflicting illness<br>折磨人的疾病</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The afflicting heat kept everyone indoors for three whole days.  折磨人的酷热让所有人在室内待了整整三天。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 agonizing</td><td>/ˈæɡənaɪzɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人痛苦的，煎熬的</td><td>an agonizing decision<br>痛苦的决定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 After an agonizing wait, the test results finally arrived.  经过痛苦的等待，化验结果终于来了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 cruel</td><td>/ˈkruːəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>残酷的，无情的</td><td>a cruel joke<br>残忍的玩笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It was cruel to leave the dog out in the storm.  把狗留在暴风雨里太残忍了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 excruciating</td><td>/ɪkˈskruːʃieɪtɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>极度痛苦的，难以忍受的</td><td>excruciating pain<br>极度的疼痛</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The excruciating pain in his back made walking impossible.  他背部剧烈的疼痛使他根本无法行走。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 galling</td><td>/ˈɡɔːlɪŋ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人恼怒的，屈辱的</td><td>a galling defeat<br>屈辱的失败</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It was galling to lose to a much weaker team.  输给弱得多的队伍让人非常恼火。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 grievous</td><td>/ˈɡriːvəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>严重的，令人痛苦的</td><td>a grievous mistake<br>严重的错误</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The report described grievous failures in patient care.  报告描述了病人护理中的严重失误。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 harrowing</td><td>/ˈheroʊɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人痛苦的，惨痛的</td><td>a harrowing experience<br>痛苦的经历</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She described her harrowing escape from the burning building.  她描述了自己从燃烧的大楼中惨痛的逃生经历。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 clement</td><td>/ˈklemənt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>宽容的，善良的；气候温和的</td><td>a clement judge<br>宽容的法官</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A clement judge gave the young offender a second chance.  一位宽容的法官给了这名年轻罪犯第二次机会。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 forbearing</td><td>/fɔrˈbɛrɪŋ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>宽容的，有耐心的</td><td>a forbearing teacher<br>宽容的老师</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His forbearing manner calmed even the rudest customers.  他宽容的态度连最无礼的顾客都能安抚。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 gentle</td><td>/ˈdʒentəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>温和的，轻柔的</td><td>a gentle voice<br>温柔的声音</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He gave the door a gentle push and stepped inside.  他轻轻推了一下门，走了进去。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 indulgent</td><td>/ɪnˈdʌldʒənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>（对己）放纵的，（对他人）纵容的</td><td>an indulgent parent<br>溺爱孩子的家长</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her indulgent grandparents let her eat whatever she wanted.  溺爱她的祖父母让她想吃什么就吃什么。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 lax</td><td>/læks/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>松懈的，不严格的；漫不经心的</td><td>lax security standards<br>松懈的安保标准</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Lax security standards at the warehouse allowed the theft to go unnoticed.  仓库松懈的安保标准使这起盗窃未被发觉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 lenient</td><td>/ˈliːniənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>宽大仁慈的</td><td>a lenient sentence<br>从轻的判决</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The judge handed down a lenient sentence because of the boy's age.  由于这个男孩年纪小，法官作出了从轻的判决。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 tolerant</td><td>/ˈtɑːlərənt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>宽容的，容忍的</td><td>tolerant of criticism<br>能容忍批评</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A good editor is tolerant of odd but honest writing.  好编辑对古怪却真诚的文字是宽容的。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>hasten</b></td><td>/ˈheɪsn/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>加速，促进；催促</td><td>hasten the decision<br>促使尽快决定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Warm weather hastened the melting of snow on the high mountain passes.  暖和的天气促进了高山山口积雪的融化。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 accelerate</td><td>/əkˈseləreɪt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>动词</td><td>加速，加快</td><td>accelerate the process<br>加快进程</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 New funding accelerated the development of the vaccine.  新的资金加快了疫苗的研发。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bundle</td><td>/ˈbʌndəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>匆忙送走，急送</td><td>bundle him into a car<br>把他匆匆塞进车</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The guards bundled the prisoner into a waiting van.  卫兵把犯人匆匆塞进等候的货车。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fast-track</td><td>/ˈfæst ˌtræk/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>加快，快速推进</td><td>fast-track the approval<br>加快审批</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The city decided to fast-track the new hospital project.  市政府决定加快推进新医院项目。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rush</td><td>/rʌʃ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>匆忙，急速行动；催促</td><td>rush to the airport<br>赶往机场</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They rushed to the airport and barely caught the flight.  他们赶往机场，勉强赶上了航班。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 speed up</td><td>/spiːd ˈʌp/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>加速，加快</td><td>speed up production<br>加快生产</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The factory sped up production to meet holiday demand.  工厂加快生产以满足假期需求。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 slow the progress of</td><td>/sloʊ ðə ˈprɑɡrɛs ʌv/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>减缓某事的进程</td><td>slow the progress of decay<br>减缓腐烂进程</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Cold storage can slow the progress of decay in fresh fruit.  冷藏可以减缓新鲜水果腐烂的进程。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 check</td><td>/tʃek/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使突然停止，阻止；检查，核对</td><td>check the spread of disease<br>阻止疾病蔓延</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Strict quarantine measures checked the spread of the disease.  严格的隔离措施阻止了疾病的蔓延。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 retard</td><td>/rɪˈtɑːrd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>减速，延迟</td><td>retard the growth<br>延缓生长</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Language barriers retarded the progress of the peace negotiations.  语言障碍阻碍了和平谈判的进展。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 brake</td><td>/breɪk/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>刹车</td><td>brake sharply<br>急刹车</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The driver braked hard when a deer crossed the road.  司机看到一只鹿穿过马路，猛地刹了车。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 decelerate</td><td>/dɪˈseləreɪt/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>减速，放慢</td><td>decelerate before the curve<br>转弯前减速</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The train decelerated smoothly as it entered the station.  列车进站时平稳地减了速。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 slow down</td><td>/sloʊ ˈdaʊn/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>减速，放慢</td><td>slow down the process<br>放慢进程</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Please slow down so that I can write all of this out.  请慢一点，我好把这些都写下来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>hasty</b></td><td>/ˈheɪsti/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>匆忙的，仓促的；轻率的</td><td>a hasty judgment<br>轻率的判断</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A hasty decision made in anger often costs far more later.  盛怒之下作出的轻率决定往往日后代价更大。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 cursory</td><td>/ˈkɜːrsəri/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>匆忙的，不注意细节的</td><td>a cursory glance<br>匆匆一瞥</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A cursory inspection missed the cracks running along the bridge supports.  一次匆忙的检查漏掉了桥墩上的裂缝。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 headlong</td><td>/ˈhedlɔːŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词、副词</td><td>鲁莽的，草率的</td><td>rush headlong into<br>贸然冲进…</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their headlong rush to publish left several errors in the final report.  他们急于发表的草率做法使最终报告留下了几处错误。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 precipitate</td><td>/prɪˈsɪpɪteɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词、及物动词</td><td>匆忙的；促使，导致</td><td>a precipitate departure<br>匆忙的离去</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His precipitate resignation surprised colleagues who expected a much longer transition.  他匆忙的辞职让原以为过渡期会长得多的同事大吃一惊。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rash</td><td>/ræʃ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>草率的，仓促的</td><td>a rash decision<br>草率的决定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He regretted his rash decision to sell the house so quickly.  他后悔自己如此草率地决定迅速卖掉房子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rushed</td><td>/ˈrʌʃt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>匆忙的，草率的</td><td>a rushed decision<br>草率的决定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The report felt rushed and full of careless mistakes.  这份报告显得草率，满是粗心的错误。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pell-mell</td><td>/ˌpɛl ˈmɛl/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词、副词</td><td>慌乱的，仓促混乱的</td><td>a pell-mell retreat<br>仓皇的撤退</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The crowd rushed pell-mell toward the only open exit.  人群慌乱地涌向唯一开着的出口。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 deliberate</td><td>/dɪˈlɪbərət/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>深思熟虑的，故意的</td><td>a deliberate attempt<br>故意的尝试</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The delay was deliberate, designed to weaken our position.  这次延误是故意的，目的是削弱我们的立场。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 well-considered</td><td>/ˌwɛl kənˈsɪdərd/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>考虑周全的</td><td>a well-considered plan<br>周全的计划</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her well-considered answer impressed everyone on the panel.  她考虑周全的回答让评审组都很佩服。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unhurried</td><td>/ʌnˈhɜːrid/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>从容的，不慌忙的</td><td>an unhurried pace<br>从容的步调</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He works in an unhurried way but never misses a deadline.  他做事从容，却从不误期。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 unrushed</del></td><td>/ʌnˈrʌʃt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不急促的，从容的</td><td>an unrushed conversation<br>不慌不忙的交谈</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We enjoyed a long, unrushed breakfast on the quiet terrace.  我们在安静的露台上享受了一顿悠长从容的早餐。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>haunt</b></td><td>/hɔːnt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>常去拜访；不断地想起，萦绕心头</td><td>haunt a favorite cafe<br>常去某咖啡馆</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 As a student he haunted the secondhand bookshops near the river.  学生时代，他常去河边的旧书店。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 affect</td><td>/əˈfekt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>假装，装作；影响</td><td>affect indifference<br>装作漠不关心</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She affected surprise, though she had known for weeks.  她装作惊讶，其实早已知情数周。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 habituate</td><td>/həˈbɪtʃuˌeɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使习惯于</td><td>habituate oneself to cold<br>使自己习惯寒冷</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Divers slowly habituate themselves to the pressure of deep water.  潜水员慢慢让自己习惯深水的压力。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 visit</td><td>/ˈvɪzɪt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词、不及物动词</td><td>拜访，常去</td><td>visit an old friend<br>拜访老友</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We visit my grandmother in the country every summer.  我们每年夏天都去乡下看望祖母。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hang at</td><td>/ˈhæŋ æt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>常泡在某处</td><td>hang at the corner store<br>常泡在街角店</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The teenagers hang at the diner every Friday night.  这些少年每个周五晚上都泡在那家小餐馆。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 resort to</td><td>/rɪˈzɔrt tu/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>常去；求助于</td><td>resort to violence<br>诉诸暴力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 When the talks failed they resorted to legal action.  谈判失败后他们诉诸法律行动。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 avoid</td><td>/əˈvɔɪd/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>避免，回避</td><td>avoid the issue<br>回避问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He carefully avoided answering the reporter's most difficult question.  他小心地避开了记者最难的那个问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 shun</td><td>/ʃʌn/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>避开，避免</td><td>shun public attention<br>避开公众关注</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The writer chose to shun public attention after winning the prize.  那位作家获奖后选择避开公众关注。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>hauteur</b></td><td>/hɔːˈtɜːr/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>傲慢，自大</td><td>an air of hauteur<br>傲慢的神态</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her hauteur offended the very people whose support she needed most.  她的傲慢恰恰得罪了她最需要支持的那些人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 bumptiousness</del></td><td>/ˈbʌmpʃəsnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>自负，傲慢</td><td>youthful bumptiousness<br>年轻人的自负</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His bumptiousness made him unpopular with the entire team.  他的自负让整个团队都不喜欢他。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 imperiousness</del></td><td>/ɪmˈpɪriəsnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>专横，霸道</td><td>the imperiousness of power<br>权力带来的专横</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His imperiousness in meetings silenced every junior colleague.  他在会上的专横让每个年轻同事都不敢开口。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 peremptoriness</del></td><td>/pəˈrɛmptərinəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>专横，独断</td><td>the peremptoriness of his order<br>他命令的专横</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The peremptoriness of the memo offended the whole department.  那份备忘录的专横语气得罪了整个部门。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pomposity</td><td>/pɑːmˈpɑːsəti/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>自大，浮夸</td><td>academic pomposity<br>学术上的浮夸</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The speech was ruined by pomposity and empty phrases.  演讲被浮夸和空话毁了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 presumptuousness</del></td><td>/prɪˈzʌmptʃuəsnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>冒失，放肆自大</td><td>sheer presumptuousness<br>十足的放肆</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Correcting his own mentor in front of the class showed remarkable presumptuousness.  在全班面前纠正自己的导师，显示出惊人的放肆。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pretentiousness</td><td>/prɪˈtɛnʃəsnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>自负，装腔作势</td><td>the pretentiousness of critics<br>批评家的做作</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The pretentiousness of his speech annoyed the whole audience.  他讲话的装腔作势让全场观众反感。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 superiority</td><td>/səˌpɪriˈɔːrəti/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>优越，优越感</td><td>an air of superiority<br>优越感的姿态</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His tone of superiority made the new staff uncomfortable.  他那种优越的语气让新员工很不舒服。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 humility</td><td>/hjuˈmɪlɪti/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>谦逊，谦卑</td><td>accept with humility<br>谦逊地接受</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He accepted the prize with genuine humility and short thanks.  他以真诚的谦逊和简短的致谢接受了奖项。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 humbleness</td><td>/ˈhʌmbəlnəs/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>谦卑，谦逊</td><td>quiet humbleness<br>沉静的谦卑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old monk's humbleness taught the students more than words.  老僧的谦卑教给学生的比言语更多。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 modesty</td><td>/ˈmɑːdəsti/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>谦虚，谦逊</td><td>false modesty<br>假谦虚</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her modesty about the discovery impressed the whole committee.  她对这一发现的谦逊让整个委员会印象深刻。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 unassumingness</del></td><td>/ˌʌnəˈsumɪŋnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>谦逊，不装架子</td><td>natural unassumingness<br>天然的谦逊</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her unassumingness made colleagues trust her almost immediately.  她的谦逊让同事几乎立刻就信任她。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 unpretentiousness</del></td><td>/ˌʌnprɪˈtɛnʃəsnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>谦逊，不张扬</td><td>charming unpretentiousness<br>可爱的谦逊</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 People trusted her because of her plain unpretentiousness.  人们信任她，因为她质朴不张扬。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>haven</b></td><td>/ˈheɪvn/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>避难所，安全的地方</td><td>a safe haven<br>安全的避难所</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old library became a quiet haven for students during exam week.  考试周里，那座旧图书馆成了学生们安静的避难所。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 asylum</td><td>/əˈsaɪləm/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>政治庇护；收容所，避难所</td><td>grant political asylum<br>给予政治庇护</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The family was granted political asylum after two years of waiting.  这家人在等待两年之后获得了政治庇护。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 harbor</td><td>/ˈhɑːrbər/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>藏匿，庇护；心怀（怨恨）</td><td>harbor a deep grudge<br>心怀深深怨恨</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The family was accused of harboring a fugitive for several months.  这家人被指控藏匿一名逃犯数月。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 refuge</td><td>/ˈrefjudʒ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>避难所；庇护，慰藉</td><td>take refuge from the storm<br>躲避风暴</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The travelers took refuge in a shepherd's stone hut.  旅人们在牧羊人的石屋里避难。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 retreat</td><td>/riˈtriːt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>避难所，隐居处</td><td>a mountain retreat<br>山间隐居处</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The cabin became his retreat from the noise of the city.  那间小屋成了他躲避城市喧闹的避难所。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sanctuary</td><td>/ˈsæŋktʃueri/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>庇护所；圣殿</td><td>seek sanctuary in a church<br>到教堂寻求庇护</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The refugees found sanctuary in a small church near the border.  难民们在边境附近的一座小教堂找到了庇护。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unsafe place</td><td>/ʌnˈseɪf pleɪs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>不安全的地方</td><td>an unsafe place to sleep<br>不安全的睡处</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old bridge is an unsafe place for children to play.  那座旧桥是孩子们玩耍的危险之地。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 dangerous place</td><td>/ˈdeɪndʒərəs pleɪs/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>危险的地方</td><td>a dangerous place at night<br>夜里危险的地方</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 That alley is a dangerous place after the shops close.  商店关门后那条巷子是个危险的地方。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>havoc</b></td><td>/ˈhævək/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>大混乱</td><td>wreak havoc on crops<br>对庄稼造成大破坏</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The flood wreaked havoc on roads and bridges throughout the valley.  洪水对整个山谷的道路和桥梁造成了大破坏。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 disarrangement</del></td><td>/ˌdɪsəˈreɪndʒmənt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>混乱，杂乱无序</td><td>the disarrangement of papers<br>文件的杂乱无序</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The disarrangement of the files suggested someone had searched the office.  文件的杂乱表明有人搜查过办公室。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 disarray</td><td>/ˌdɪsəˈreɪ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>混乱，无秩序</td><td>in complete disarray<br>一片混乱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The office was in complete disarray after the movers left boxes everywhere.  搬运工把箱子扔得到处都是，办公室一片混乱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 dishevelment</del></td><td>/dɪˈʃɛvəlmənt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>凌乱，蓬乱</td><td>the dishevelment of her hair<br>头发的凌乱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His dishevelment after the long flight was obvious to everyone.  长途飞行后他的凌乱样子人人都看得出。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 muddle</td><td>/ˈmʌdəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>使混乱，弄糟；混乱</td><td>muddle the facts<br>把事实弄乱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Too many footnotes muddled the argument of his short essay.  过多脚注把他短文的论证弄乱了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 muss</td><td>/mʌs/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>弄乱；混乱</td><td>muss up her hair<br>弄乱她的头发</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The wind mussed his carefully combed hair in seconds.  风几秒钟就吹乱了他精心梳理的头发。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 tumble</td><td>/ˈtʌmbəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>混乱，杂乱一堆</td><td>a tumble of clothes<br>一堆乱衣服</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The room was a tumble of boxes, books and old toys.  屋里是一堆乱七八糟的箱子、书和旧玩具。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 welter</td><td>/ˈweltər/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词、不及物动词</td><td>混乱，动乱</td><td>a welter of details<br>杂乱的一堆细节</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The report buried its main point in a welter of small details.  这份报告把要点埋在了杂乱的琐碎细节之中。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 order</td><td>/ˈɔːrdər/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>秩序，条理；命令</td><td>restore public order<br>恢复公共秩序</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Police worked all night to restore order in the city.  警察通宵工作以恢复城市的秩序。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 orderliness</td><td>/ˈɔːrdərlinəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>整齐，有序</td><td>the orderliness of the files<br>档案的整齐有序</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Visitors admired the orderliness of the small library.  参观者赞叹这座小图书馆的井然有序。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>headlong</b></td><td>/ˈhedlɔːŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词、副词</td><td>鲁莽的，草率的</td><td>rush headlong into<br>贸然冲进…</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their headlong rush to publish left several errors in the final report.  他们急于发表的草率做法使最终报告留下了几处错误。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 cursory</td><td>/ˈkɜːrsəri/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>匆忙的，不注意细节的</td><td>a cursory glance<br>匆匆一瞥</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A cursory inspection missed the cracks running along the bridge supports.  一次匆忙的检查漏掉了桥墩上的裂缝。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 overhasty</del></td><td>/ˌoʊvərˈheɪsti/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>过于匆忙的，草率的</td><td>an overhasty judgment<br>过于草率的判断</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their overhasty decision to expand nearly ruined the young company.  他们草率的扩张决定几乎毁了这家年轻公司。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 precipitate</td><td>/prɪˈsɪpɪteɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词、及物动词</td><td>匆忙的；促使，导致</td><td>a precipitate departure<br>匆忙的离去</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His precipitate resignation surprised colleagues who expected a much longer transition.  他匆忙的辞职让原以为过渡期会长得多的同事大吃一惊。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 precipitous</td><td>/prɪˈsɪpɪtəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>非常陡峭的；匆忙的</td><td>a precipitous decline<br>急剧的下滑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The path became precipitous near the summit, forcing us to use ropes.  靠近峰顶时山路变得非常陡峭，迫使我们用上绳索。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rash</td><td>/ræʃ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>草率的，仓促的</td><td>a rash decision<br>草率的决定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He regretted his rash decision to sell the house so quickly.  他后悔自己如此草率地决定迅速卖掉房子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pell-mell</td><td>/ˌpɛl ˈmɛl/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词、副词</td><td>慌乱的，仓促混乱的</td><td>a pell-mell retreat<br>仓皇的撤退</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The crowd rushed pell-mell toward the only open exit.  人群慌乱地涌向唯一开着的出口。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 helter-skelter</td><td>/ˌheltərˈskeltər/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词、副词</td><td>慌乱的，仓促的</td><td>run helter-skelter down the hill<br>慌乱地跑下山</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The children ran helter-skelter across the muddy yard.  孩子们慌慌张张地跑过泥泞的院子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 deliberate</td><td>/dɪˈlɪbərət/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>深思熟虑的，故意的</td><td>a deliberate attempt<br>故意的尝试</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The delay was deliberate, designed to weaken our position.  这次延误是故意的，目的是削弱我们的立场。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unhurried</td><td>/ʌnˈhɜːrid/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>从容的，不慌忙的</td><td>an unhurried pace<br>从容的步调</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He works in an unhurried way but never misses a deadline.  他做事从容，却从不误期。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 unrushed</del></td><td>/ʌnˈrʌʃt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不急促的，从容的</td><td>an unrushed conversation<br>不慌不忙的交谈</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We enjoyed a long, unrushed breakfast on the quiet terrace.  我们在安静的露台上享受了一顿悠长从容的早餐。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>hearken</b></td><td>/ˈhɑːrkən/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>倾听，关注</td><td>hearken to advice<br>听取建议</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Few leaders hearken to warnings until it is far too late.  很少有领导者在为时已晚之前听取警告。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 attend</td><td>/əˈtend/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>倾听，注意</td><td>attend to his words<br>留意他的话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Please attend carefully to what the doctor tells you.  请仔细听医生对你说的话。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 harken</td><td>/ˈhɑːrkən/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>倾听，留意</td><td>harken to the call<br>响应召唤</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The young priest urged them to harken to the ancient teachings.  这位年轻牧师劝他们倾听古老的教义。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 heed</td><td>/ˈhiːd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>注意，留心</td><td>heed the warning<br>留意警告</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Few sailors heeded the warning about the coming storm.  很少有水手留意关于风暴来临的警告。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mind</td><td>/ˈmaɪnd/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、不及物动词</td><td>留意，注意；介意</td><td>mind the step<br>留意台阶</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Please mind the gap between the train and the platform.  请注意列车与站台之间的空隙。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 ignore</td><td>/ˌɪɡˈnɔːr/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>忽视，不理</td><td>ignore the warning<br>忽视警告</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The captain ignored the warning and sailed into the storm.  船长忽视了警告，驶入了风暴之中。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>hearten</b></td><td>/ˈhɑːrtn/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>给予鼓励，鼓舞</td><td>hearten the troops<br>鼓舞部队士气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The crowd's cheering heartened the runners on the last steep hill.  观众的欢呼在最后一个陡坡上鼓舞了跑者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 embolden</td><td>/ɪmˈboʊldən/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>鼓励，使大胆</td><td>embolden the rebels<br>壮了叛乱者的胆</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Early success emboldened her to ask for a promotion.  初期的成功让她有胆量要求升职。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 inspire</td><td>/ɪnˈspaɪr/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>鼓舞，激励；引发</td><td>inspire confidence in others<br>让他人产生信心</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her speech inspired the students to work much harder.  她的演讲激励学生更加努力。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 inspirit</del></td><td>/ɪnˈspɪrɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>鼓舞，激励</td><td>inspirit the weary troops<br>鼓舞疲惫的部队</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her speech inspirited the exhausted volunteers to keep working through the night.  她的演讲鼓舞疲惫的志愿者继续通宵工作。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 buck up</td><td>/bʌk ˈʌp/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>振作起来，打起精神</td><td>buck up a discouraged friend<br>让沮丧的朋友振作</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A short walk and some coffee bucked him up nicely.  散散步再喝点咖啡，他精神好了很多。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 buoy up</td><td>/ˈbuːi ʌp/</td><td>★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>鼓舞，使振作</td><td>buoy up her spirits<br>鼓舞她的士气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Letters from home buoyed up the soldiers during the long winter.  家书在漫长的冬天鼓舞了士兵们。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 cheer up</td><td>/tʃɪr ˈʌp/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>使高兴起来</td><td>cheer up a sad friend<br>让难过的朋友开心</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We took her to the beach to cheer her up.  我们带她去海边让她开心起来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 daunt</td><td>/dɔːnt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使胆怯，吓倒</td><td>daunt the bravest soldiers<br>吓倒最勇敢的士兵</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The steep climb ahead did not daunt the experienced mountain guide.  前方陡峭的攀登丝毫没有吓倒这位经验丰富的山地导游。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 dismay</td><td>/dɪsˈmeɪ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使失去勇气；使不安，使焦虑</td><td>dismay his supporters<br>使支持者沮丧</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The abrupt policy reversal dismayed his supporters and delighted his rivals.  政策的突然反转让他的支持者沮丧，让对手高兴。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 discourage</td><td>/dɪsˈkɜːrɪdʒ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使沮丧，使气馁；劝阻</td><td>discourage him from trying<br>劝他不要尝试</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Early failure should not discourage a young researcher.  早期的失败不该让年轻研究者气馁。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 dishearten</td><td>/dɪsˈhɑːrtn/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使沮丧，使失去信心</td><td>disheartened by the news<br>因这消息而沮丧</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The string of early losses disheartened even the most loyal fans.  开局连输让最忠实的球迷也失去了信心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 dispirit</del></td><td>/dɪˈspɪrɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使沮丧，使气馁</td><td>dispirit the whole team<br>使全队士气低落</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The endless rain dispirited even the most cheerful campers.  连绵的阴雨让最开朗的营员也沮丧起来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 2

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>heartrending</b></td><td>/ˈhɑːrtˌrendɪŋ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人心碎的</td><td>a heartrending story<br>令人心碎的故事</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The documentary tells the heartrending story of a village losing its children.  这部纪录片讲述了一个村庄失去孩子的令人心碎的故事。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>hedonism</b></td><td>/ˈhiːdənɪzəm/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>享乐主义</td><td>a life of hedonism<br>享乐主义的生活</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics accused the novel of celebrating hedonism above every other value.  批评者指责这部小说把享乐主义置于其他一切价值之上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 carnality</td><td>/kɑrˈnæləti/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>肉欲，淫乐</td><td>sink into carnality<br>沉溺于肉欲</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The novel condemns the empty carnality of the very rich.  小说谴责了极富者空洞的肉欲生活。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 debauchery</td><td>/dəˈbɔːtʃəri/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>放荡，纵欲</td><td>a life of debauchery<br>放荡的生活</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He wasted his inheritance on years of reckless debauchery.  他把遗产浪费在多年放荡纵欲上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 sybaritism</del></td><td>/ˈsɪbərəˌtɪzəm/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>奢华享乐主义</td><td>the sybaritism of court life<br>宫廷的奢华享乐</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Ancient writers mocked the sybaritism of the wealthy merchants.  古代作家嘲讽富商们的奢华享乐。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 voluptuousness</td><td>/vəˈlʌptʃuəsnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>感官享乐，妖艳</td><td>the voluptuousness of the music<br>音乐的感官魅力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics praised the warm voluptuousness of her singing voice.  评论家赞赏她歌声中温暖的感官魅力。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 abstinence</td><td>/ˈæbstənəns/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>节制，禁欲</td><td>abstinence from alcohol<br>戒酒</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The doctor recommended total abstinence from sugar for a month.  医生建议一个月完全戒糖。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 asceticism</td><td>/əˈsɛtəˌsɪzəm/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>禁欲主义，苦行</td><td>a life of asceticism<br>苦行的生活</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The monk chose asceticism over the comforts of his family home.  这位僧人舍弃家中舒适，选择了苦行。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 sobriety</td><td>/səˈbraɪəti/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>清醒节制；严肃</td><td>a mood of sobriety<br>严肃的气氛</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He has kept his sobriety for more than ten years.  他已保持戒酒清醒十多年了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 temperance</td><td>/ˈtempərəns/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>节制，自我克制</td><td>temperance in all things<br>凡事节制</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old doctor preached temperance in eating and drinking.  这位老医生倡导饮食节制。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>hegemony</b></td><td>/hɪˈdʒeməni/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>霸权，统治权</td><td>regional hegemony<br>地区霸权</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The empire lost its naval hegemony within a single generation.  这个帝国在一代人之内就失去了海上霸权。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ascendancy</td><td>/əˈsendənsi/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>支配地位，优势</td><td>gain political ascendancy<br>取得政治优势</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The party gained ascendancy over its rivals after the election.  该党在选举后取得了压过对手的优势。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dominance</td><td>/ˈdɑːmənəns/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>支配，统治地位</td><td>market dominance<br>市场支配地位</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company's dominance in search has lasted twenty years.  这家公司在搜索领域的支配地位已持续二十年。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dominion</td><td>/dəˈmɪnjən/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>统治权；领地</td><td>hold dominion over land<br>统治土地</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The king held dominion over every valley in the north.  国王统治着北方的每一个山谷。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 predominance</td><td>/prɪˈdɑːmənəns/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>主导地位，优势</td><td>the predominance of English<br>英语的主导地位</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The predominance of small farms shapes the local economy.  小农场的主导地位塑造了当地经济。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 preeminence</td><td>/priˈemənəns/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>卓越，杰出地位</td><td>scientific preeminence<br>科学上的卓越</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her preeminence in the field was never seriously questioned.  她在该领域的卓越地位从未被真正质疑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 lack of authority</td><td>/læk əv əˈθɔrəti/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>缺乏权威</td><td>a lack of authority<br>缺乏权威</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His lack of authority made the students ignore every rule.  他缺乏权威，学生们把每条规矩都当空气。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>heinous</b></td><td>/ˈheɪnəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>可憎的，十恶不赦的</td><td>a heinous act<br>令人憎恶的行为</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The court described the crime as heinous and utterly without excuse.  法庭称这一罪行十恶不赦，毫无借口可言。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 commendable</td><td>/kəˈmendəbəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>值得赞扬的</td><td>commendable honesty<br>值得赞扬的诚实</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The rescue team showed commendable calm during the long night.  救援队在漫长的夜里表现出值得赞扬的镇定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>hew</b></td><td>/hjuː/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词、不及物动词</td><td>砍；劈；坚守（原则）</td><td>hew to the line<br>严守准则</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new mayor promised to hew closely to the party line.  新市长承诺严格遵循党的路线。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 adhere</td><td>/ədˈhɪr/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>服从，遵守</td><td>adhere to the rules<br>遵守规则</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 All staff must adhere to the safety procedures without exception.  全体员工必须无例外地遵守安全规程。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 cling</td><td>/klɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>紧贴；坚守（信念）</td><td>cling to tradition<br>坚守传统</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Older residents still cling to tradition despite all the changes.  尽管变化很多，年长的居民仍然坚守传统。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 conform</td><td>/kənˈfɔːrm/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、不及物动词</td><td>使协调；遵照，遵从</td><td>conform to the standard<br>符合标准</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 All new buildings must conform to strict safety codes.  所有新建筑都必须符合严格的安全规范。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>herald</b></td><td>/ˈherəld/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>预示，预兆</td><td>herald a new era<br>预示新时代来临</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The invention heralded a new era in communication.  这项发明预示着通信新时代的到来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 adumbrate</del></td><td>/ˈædəmbreɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>预示</td><td>adumbrate future trouble<br>预示日后的麻烦</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The early chapters adumbrate the tragedy that follows.  开头几章预示了随后的悲剧。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 forerun</del></td><td>/fɔːrˈrʌn/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>预示，先于…而来</td><td>forerun a change<br>预示一场变革</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The falling leaves forerun the arrival of a long winter.  落叶预示着漫长冬天的到来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 harbinger</td><td>/ˈhɑːrbɪndʒər/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>预兆，先兆；预示者</td><td>a harbinger of spring<br>春天的先兆</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The first robin is often seen as a harbinger of spring.  第一只知更鸟常被视为春天的预兆。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 prefigure</td><td>/priːˈfɪɡjər/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>预示，预先显示</td><td>prefigure later events<br>预示后来的事件</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His early sketches prefigure the style of his mature paintings.  他早期的素描预示了他成熟画作的风格。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>heresy</b></td><td>/ˈherəsi/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>异教，和普遍观点相悖的说法</td><td>accused of heresy<br>被指为异端</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Suggesting that the old tradition might be wrong was treated as heresy.  暗示这一古老传统可能有错，被视为异端邪说。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dissent</td><td>/dɪˈsent/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>不及物动词、名词</td><td>持异议，不同意；反对正统</td><td>voice one's dissent<br>表达异议</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Two judges dissented from the majority opinion of the court.  两位法官对法院的多数意见持异议。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dissidence</td><td>/ˈdɪsədəns/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>异议，不同意</td><td>political dissidence<br>政治异见</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The regime punished even mild dissidence among its writers.  该政权连作家中温和的异见也要惩罚。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 heterodoxy</td><td>/ˈhetərədɑːksi/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>异端邪说；非正统观点</td><td>charged with heterodoxy<br>被指为异端</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The bishop condemned her writings as dangerous heterodoxy.  主教谴责她的著作是危险的异端邪说。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 nonconformity</td><td>/ˌnɑːnkənˈfɔːrməti/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>不墨守成规，不遵从</td><td>a spirit of nonconformity<br>不循规蹈矩的精神</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her nonconformity made her unpopular with the school administration.  她的不合常规使她不受校方欢迎。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 dogma</td><td>/ˈdɑːɡmə/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>教条，信条</td><td>religious dogma<br>宗教教条</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She refused to accept the party's dogma without question.  她拒绝毫不质疑地接受该党的教条。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 conformity</td><td>/kənˈfɔːrməti/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>遵从，一致，顺从</td><td>conformity to social norms<br>遵从社会规范</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company demands strict conformity to its dress code.  公司要求严格遵守其着装规范。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 orthodoxy</td><td>/ˈɔːrθəˌdɑːksi/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>正统观念，传统信仰</td><td>challenge economic orthodoxy<br>挑战经济正统</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her paper challenges the orthodoxy of modern economics.  她的论文挑战了现代经济学的正统观念。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>heretical</b></td><td>/həˈretɪkl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>异教的，异端邪说的</td><td>a heretical opinion<br>异端的观点</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His heretical views cost him his teaching post at the seminary.  他的异端观点使他失去了神学院的教职。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 heterodox</td><td>/ˈhetərədɑːks/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>非正统的，异端的</td><td>heterodox opinions<br>非正统的观点</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her heterodox economic theories were ignored for decades before gaining respect.  她非正统的经济理论被忽视数十年后才获得尊重。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 nonconformist</td><td>/ˌnɑːnkənˈfɔːrmɪst/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>不墨守成规的人</td><td>a lifelong nonconformist<br>终身的叛逆者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He was a nonconformist who questioned every rule at school.  他是个不循规蹈矩的人，质疑学校的每条规定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unconventional</td><td>/ˌʌnkənˈvenʃənəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>非传统的，不合常规的</td><td>an unconventional method<br>非常规的方法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her unconventional teaching style made the class come alive.  她非传统的教学风格让课堂活了起来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unorthodox</td><td>/ʌnˈɔːrθədɑːks/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>非正统的，异端的</td><td>an unorthodox approach<br>非正统的做法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The coach used unorthodox drills that somehow worked very well.  教练用了非正统的训练，效果却出奇地好。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 conforming</td><td>/kənˈfɔːrmɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>遵从的，顺应的</td><td>conforming to the rules<br>遵守规则</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Only designs conforming to the code will be approved.  只有符合规范的设计才会获批。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 conformist</td><td>/kənˈfɔːrmɪst/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>墨守成规者，随大流者</td><td>a cautious conformist<br>谨慎的随大流者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 In that office every conformist was rewarded and every rebel ignored.  在那个办公室，随大流者受奖，反叛者被无视。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 conventional</td><td>/kənˈvenʃənəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>传统的，常规的</td><td>conventional wisdom<br>传统观念</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They chose a conventional design to please the older members.  他们选了常规设计以取悦年长的成员。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 orthodox</td><td>/ˈɔːrθədɑːks/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>传统的</td><td>orthodox medical treatment<br>传统医疗手段</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Many doctors still prefer orthodox treatments to these unproven herbal remedies.  许多医生仍偏爱传统疗法，而非这些未经验证的草药。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>hermetic</b></td><td>/hərˈmetɪk/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>深奥的；密封的</td><td>a hermetic style<br>晦涩的风格</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The poet's hermetic style baffles most readers.  这位诗人晦涩的风格令多数读者困惑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 abstruse</td><td>/əbˈstruːs/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>难以理解的</td><td>abstruse theories<br>深奥的理论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His lecture was too abstruse for first-year students.  他的讲座对一年级学生来说太晦涩。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 arcane</td><td>/ɑːrˈkeɪn/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>深奥的，难以理解的</td><td>arcane rules<br>晦涩难懂的规则</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The tax code is full of arcane rules nobody understands.  税法充满无人理解的晦涩规定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 esoteric</td><td>/ˌesəˈterɪk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>深奥难懂的</td><td>esoteric knowledge<br>深奥的知识</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She writes about esoteric topics in medieval music.  她写中世纪音乐中深奥的题目。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 recondite</td><td>/ˈrekəndaɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>深奥的，难解的</td><td>recondite arguments<br>深奥的论证</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The lecture grew so recondite that half the audience quietly left.  讲座变得如此深奥，一半听众悄悄离场。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 easily comprehended</td><td>/ˈizəli ˌkɑmprɪˈhɛndəd/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>容易理解的</td><td>easily comprehended instructions<br>易懂的说明</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The manual is written in easily comprehended everyday language.  这本手册用容易理解的日常语言写成。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 shallow</td><td>/ˈʃæloʊ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>浅薄的，肤浅的；浅的</td><td>shallow water<br>浅水</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His arguments are shallow and easy to refute.  他的论点浅薄，很容易反驳。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 superficial</td><td>/ˌsuːpərˈfɪʃl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>表面的，肤浅的</td><td>a superficial understanding<br>肤浅的理解</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She has only a superficial knowledge of the subject.  她对这门学科只有肤浅的了解。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>hagiographic</b></td><td>/ˌhæɡiəˈɡræfɪk/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>捧为圣人一般的，过分谄媚的</td><td>a hagiographic biography<br>圣徒传式的传记</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The biography is so hagiographic that it mentions no flaws at all.  这本传记谄媚得如同圣徒传，完全不提任何缺点。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 adulatory</del></td><td>/ˈædʒələˌtɔri/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>谄媚的，过分吹捧的</td><td>an adulatory review<br>吹捧的评论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The magazine ran an adulatory profile of the young director.  杂志刊登了一篇吹捧那位年轻导演的报道。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 flattering</td><td>/ˈflætərɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>奉承的；美化的</td><td>a flattering description<br>奉承美化的描述</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The portrait was flattering but hardly looked like him.  这幅画像很美化，却几乎不像他。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 gushy</td><td>/ˈɡʌʃi/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>感情流露过度的，肉麻的</td><td>a gushy thank-you note<br>肉麻的感谢便条</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her gushy introduction embarrassed the guest speaker.  她过度热情的介绍让客座讲者很不好意思。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ingratiating</td><td>/ɪnˈɡreɪʃieɪtɪŋ/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>讨人喜欢的；逢迎的，意在奉承的</td><td>an ingratiating smile<br>讨好的微笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The salesman's ingratiating manner made me suspect he was hiding something important.  这位销售员逢迎的态度让我怀疑他隐瞒了重要的事。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 oily</td><td>/ˈɔɪli/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>油滑的，谄媚的；油质的</td><td>an oily salesman<br>油嘴滑舌的推销员</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His oily compliments made everyone in the room uncomfortable.  他油滑的奉承让屋里每个人都不舒服。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 oleaginous</td><td>/ˌoʊliˈædʒənəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>油滑谄媚的</td><td>an oleaginous salesman<br>油嘴滑舌的销售</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The oleaginous host praised every guest far too warmly.  那位油滑的主持人对每位嘉宾都夸得过火。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 soapy</td><td>/ˈsoʊpi/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>谄媚的；含皂的</td><td>a soapy compliment<br>谄媚的赞美</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His soapy manner with the boss embarrassed his colleagues.  他对老板谄媚的样子让同事们难堪。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 toady</td><td>/ˈtoʊdi/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>马屁精；拍马屁</td><td>toady to the powerful<br>对权贵拍马屁</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Junior officials toady to the powerful hoping for quick promotion.  下级官员对权贵拍马屁，希望快速升迁。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unctuous</td><td>/ˈʌŋktʃuəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>油嘴滑舌的，谄媚的</td><td>an unctuous compliment<br>油腻的奉承</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The unctuous salesman praised every choice we made in his store.  那个油嘴滑舌的销售员对我们在他店里的每个选择都大加赞美。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 artless</td><td>/ˈɑrtləs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>缺乏技巧的，笨拙的</td><td>an artless imitation<br>拙劣的模仿</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The painting was an artless copy of a famous portrait.  这幅画是对一幅名作的拙劣临摹。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 earnest</td><td>/ˈɜːrnɪst/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>严肃认真的</td><td>an earnest student<br>认真的学生</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 An earnest student stayed behind to ask about the summer reading list.  一名认真的学生留下来询问暑期阅读书目。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 genuine</td><td>/ˈdʒenjəwən/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>真正的，真实的；真诚的</td><td>genuine leather<br>真皮</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Experts confirmed that the signature on the letter was genuine.  专家确认信上的签名是真的。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 heartfelt</td><td>/ˈhɑːrtfelt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>真心实意的</td><td>heartfelt thanks<br>真诚的感谢</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He offered heartfelt thanks to everyone who had supported his family.  他向所有支持过他家人的人表达了真心的感谢。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 ingenuous</td><td>/ɪnˈdʒenjuəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>天真淳朴的</td><td>an ingenuous young student<br>天真淳朴的年轻学生</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His ingenuous questions about the city amused the veteran taxi driver greatly.  他关于这座城市的天真提问让老出租车司机大为好笑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 sincere</td><td>/sɪnˈsɪr/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>真诚的，诚挚的</td><td>a sincere apology<br>真诚的道歉</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her sincere apology repaired a friendship damaged for years.  她真诚的道歉修复了受损多年的友谊。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unfeigned</td><td>/ʌnˈfeɪnd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>真诚的，不做作的</td><td>unfeigned delight<br>真心的喜悦</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her unfeigned joy at the news warmed the whole room.  她听到消息时真心的喜悦温暖了整个屋子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 3

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>heterodox</b></td><td>/ˈhetərədɑːks/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>非正统的，异端的</td><td>heterodox opinions<br>非正统的观点</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her heterodox economic theories were ignored for decades before gaining respect.  她非正统的经济理论被忽视数十年后才获得尊重。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dissenting</td><td>/dɪˈsentɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>持异议的，不同意的</td><td>a dissenting opinion<br>异议意见</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Two judges wrote dissenting opinions on the final ruling.  两位法官就终审判决写了异议意见。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 out-there</td><td>/ˌaʊt ˈðɛr/</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>非常古怪的，离谱的</td><td>an out-there idea<br>离谱的想法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His out-there theories amused the students but worried the dean.  他离谱的理论逗乐了学生，却让院长忧心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unconventional</td><td>/ˌʌnkənˈvenʃənəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>非传统的，不合常规的</td><td>an unconventional method<br>非常规的方法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her unconventional teaching style made the class come alive.  她非传统的教学风格让课堂活了起来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unorthodox</td><td>/ʌnˈɔːrθədɑːks/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>非正统的，异端的</td><td>an unorthodox approach<br>非正统的做法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The coach used unorthodox drills that somehow worked very well.  教练用了非正统的训练，效果却出奇地好。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 conforming</td><td>/kənˈfɔːrmɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>遵从的，顺应的</td><td>conforming to the rules<br>遵守规则</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Only designs conforming to the code will be approved.  只有符合规范的设计才会获批。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 conventional</td><td>/kənˈvenʃənəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>传统的，常规的</td><td>conventional wisdom<br>传统观念</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They chose a conventional design to please the older members.  他们选了常规设计以取悦年长的成员。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 orthodox</td><td>/ˈɔːrθədɑːks/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>传统的</td><td>orthodox medical treatment<br>传统医疗手段</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Many doctors still prefer orthodox treatments to these unproven herbal remedies.  许多医生仍偏爱传统疗法，而非这些未经验证的草药。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 regular</td><td>/ˈreɡjələr/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>规律的，通常的</td><td>on a regular basis<br>定期地</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He goes swimming on a regular basis every week.  他每周都定期去游泳。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 routine</td><td>/ruˈtiːn/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>例行的；常规程序</td><td>a routine check<br>例行检查</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The problem was discovered during a routine inspection last week.  这个问题是上周例行检查时发现的。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>hidebound</b></td><td>/ˈhaɪdbaʊnd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>死板的，极度保守的</td><td>hidebound traditions<br>僵化的传统</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The hidebound innkeeper flatly refused to install internet for guests.  这位死板的旅店老板断然拒绝为客人装网络。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 archconservative</del></td><td>/ˌɑrtʃkənˈsɜrvətɪv/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>极端保守的（人）</td><td>an archconservative senator<br>极端保守的参议员</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The archconservative judge opposed every proposed change to the law.  这位极端保守的法官反对法律的每项修改提议。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 brassbound</del></td><td>/ˈbræsˌbaʊnd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>顽固守旧的</td><td>a brassbound tradition<br>僵化的传统</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The brassbound committee refused to consider any new idea.  这个顽固守旧的委员会拒绝考虑任何新想法。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 die-hard</td><td>/ˈdaɪ hɑrd/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>顽固不化的；死硬派</td><td>a die-hard supporter<br>一个死忠支持者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Only a few die-hard fans stayed until the final whistle.  只有几个死忠球迷留到终场哨响。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 old-fashioned</td><td>/ˌoʊldˈfæʃənd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>守旧的，过时的</td><td>old-fashioned manners<br>守旧的礼节</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His old-fashioned views on marriage puzzled his own children.  他守旧的婚姻观让自己的孩子困惑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 standpat</del></td><td>/ˈstændˌpæt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>守旧的，不肯变通的</td><td>a standpat attitude<br>守旧的态度</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His standpat views blocked every reform the town really needed.  他守旧的观点阻碍了小镇真正需要的每项改革。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ultraconservative</td><td>/ˌʌltrəkənˈsɜːrvətɪv/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>极端保守的；极端保守者</td><td>an ultraconservative faction<br>极端保守的派别</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The party's ultraconservative wing blocked every proposed reform.  该党极端保守的一翼阻挠了每项改革提议。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 broad-minded</td><td>/ˌbrɔd ˈmaɪndəd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>思想开明的，宽容的</td><td>a broad-minded editor<br>开明的编辑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her broad-minded parents let her study art abroad.  她开明的父母让她出国学艺术。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 large-minded</td><td>/ˌlɑrdʒ ˈmaɪndəd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>宽宏大量的，开明的</td><td>a large-minded leader<br>宽宏的领导者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Only a large-minded judge could hear such a case fairly.  只有宽宏开明的法官才能公正审理这样的案子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 liberal</td><td>/ˈlɪbərəl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>思想前卫的；慷慨的，大方的</td><td>liberal social views<br>前卫的社会观念</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His liberal social views often surprised the older members of the club.  他前卫的社会观念常常让俱乐部里年长的成员感到意外。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 nonconservative</del></td><td>/ˌnɑnkənˈsɜrvətɪv/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>非保守的，开放的</td><td>a nonconservative outlook<br>非保守的观念</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Younger voters favored the nonconservative candidate by wide margins.  年轻选民以巨大优势支持这位非保守派候选人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 nonconventional</del></td><td>/ˌnɑnkənˈvɛnʃənəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>非常规的，不合传统的</td><td>nonconventional energy sources<br>非常规能源</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The city invested in nonconventional energy such as tidal power.  该市投资了潮汐能等非常规能源。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 nonorthodox</del></td><td>/ˌnɑnˈɔrθəˌdɑks/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>非正统的</td><td>a nonorthodox reading<br>非正统的解读</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She offered a nonorthodox reading of the ancient poem.  她对这首古诗提出了非正统的解读。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 nontraditional</td><td>/ˌnɑːntrəˈdɪʃənəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>非传统的，不循常规的</td><td>a nontraditional approach<br>非传统的做法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The school welcomes nontraditional students who return after working.  这所学校欢迎工作后返校的非传统学生。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 open-minded</td><td>/ˈoʊpən ˈmaɪndɪd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>思想开明的</td><td>an open-minded editor<br>思想开明的编辑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A good reviewer stays open-minded about unfamiliar musical styles.  好的评论者对陌生的音乐风格保持开明。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 progressive</td><td>/prəˈɡresɪv/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>进步的，开明的</td><td>progressive social policies<br>进步的社会政策</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The school follows progressive methods that welcome student questions.  这所学校采用鼓励学生提问的进步方法。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>hideous</b></td><td>/ˈhɪdiəs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>非常丑陋的</td><td>a hideous mask<br>丑陋可怕的面具</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They tore down the hideous concrete tower that blocked the view.  他们拆掉了那座挡住视野的丑陋混凝土塔楼。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 homely</td><td>/ˈhoʊmli/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>其貌不扬的，朴素简单的</td><td>a homely face<br>其貌不扬的脸</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She had a homely but kind face that put strangers at ease.  她有一张其貌不扬却和善的脸，让陌生人感到自在。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ill-favored</td><td>/ɪl ˈfeɪvərd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>相貌丑陋的</td><td>an ill-favored face<br>丑陋的面容</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The ill-favored old man was surprisingly gentle with small children.  这位相貌丑陋的老人对小孩子出奇地温和。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 monstrous</td><td>/ˈmɑːnstrəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>巨大的；骇人的</td><td>a monstrous wave<br>巨浪</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A monstrous wave rose above the tiny fishing boat.  一个巨浪在小渔船上方涌起。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 uncomely</del></td><td>/ʌnˈkʌmli/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不好看的，不雅观的</td><td>an uncomely dress<br>不好看的裙子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She wore an uncomely dress that hid her real charm.  她穿了件不好看的裙子，掩住了她真正的魅力。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unsightly</td><td>/ʌnˈsaɪtli/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>难看的，刺眼的</td><td>unsightly stains<br>难看的污渍</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Unsightly stains covered the wall behind the old kitchen sink.  难看的污渍覆盖了旧厨房水槽后的墙面。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 pulchritudinous</del></td><td>/ˌpʌlkrəˈtudənəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>美丽的，貌美的</td><td>a pulchritudinous face<br>美丽的面容</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The poet described his pulchritudinous neighbor in eight long stanzas.  诗人用八段长诗描写他貌美的邻居。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 aesthetic</td><td>/esˈθetɪk/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>审美的，美的</td><td>aesthetic appeal<br>审美上的吸引力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The building's aesthetic appeal outweighs its many practical problems.  这栋楼的美感胜过它的诸多实用缺陷。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 attractive</td><td>/əˈtræktɪv/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有吸引力的，迷人的</td><td>an attractive offer<br>有吸引力的提议</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company made him an attractive offer he could not refuse.  公司给了他一个无法拒绝的诱人条件。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 comely</td><td>/ˈkʌmli/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>秀丽的，好看的</td><td>a comely young woman<br>秀丽的年轻女子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A comely young woman greeted us politely at the gate.  一位秀丽的年轻女子在门口礼貌地迎接我们。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 gorgeous</td><td>/ˈɡɔːrdʒəs/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>极美的，绚丽的</td><td>a gorgeous sunset<br>绚丽的落日</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We watched a gorgeous sunset from the hotel balcony.  我们在酒店阳台上看了一场绚丽的落日。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 handsome</td><td>/ˈhænsəm/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>英俊的，好看的</td><td>a handsome young man<br>英俊的年轻人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He was a handsome man with unusually kind eyes.  他是个英俊的男人，眼神格外和善。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 knockout</td><td>/ˈnɑːˌkaʊt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>击倒昏迷；击倒获胜</td><td>win by a knockout<br>以击倒对手获胜</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The champion ended the fight with a knockout in round two.  冠军在第二回合以击倒结束了比赛。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 ravishing</td><td>/ˈrævɪʃɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>极美的，令人陶醉的</td><td>a ravishing beauty<br>绝色的美人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She looked absolutely ravishing in her long silver evening gown.  她穿着银色晚礼长裙，美得令人陶醉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 seemly</td><td>/ˈsiːmli/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>得体的，合宜的</td><td>seemly conduct in public<br>公共场合得体的举止</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The teacher expects seemly conduct from her students in public places.  老师期望学生在公共场所举止得体。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 stunning</td><td>/ˈstʌnɪŋ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人震惊的；极漂亮的</td><td>a stunning upset<br>令人震惊的逆转</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The underdog scored a stunning victory in the final round.  弱队在最后一轮取得令人震惊的胜利。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 taking</td><td>/ˈteɪkɪŋ/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>迷人的，讨人喜欢的</td><td>a taking manner<br>讨人喜欢的举止</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The child had a taking manner that won everyone over.  这孩子讨人喜欢的举止赢得了所有人的心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 well-favored</td><td>/wel ˈfeɪvərd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>相貌好看的</td><td>a well-favored young man<br>相貌好看的年轻人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He was a well-favored young man with easy country manners.  他是个相貌好看、举止随和的乡下青年。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 affinity</td><td>/əˈfɪnəti/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>喜好，倾向；相似，密切关系</td><td>an affinity for music<br>对音乐的爱好</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She has a natural affinity for languages and picks them up quickly.  她天生对语言有亲近感，学得很快。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>hie</b></td><td>/haɪ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>急忙前往，快走</td><td>hie to the market<br>匆匆前往集市</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The messenger hied to the palace with news of the great victory.  信使带着大胜的消息匆匆赶往王宫。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bustle</td><td>/ˈbʌsl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、不及物动词</td><td>忙乱，喧闹；（快速地）行走，奔忙</td><td>the bustle of the street<br>街上的喧闹</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 I love the bustle of the market on Saturday mornings.  我喜欢周六早晨市场上的喧闹。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dash</td><td>/ˈdæʃ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>不及物动词、名词</td><td>猛冲，疾奔</td><td>dash across the street<br>快速冲过街道</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She dashed across the street to catch the departing bus.  她冲过街道去赶那辆即将开走的公交车。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hustle</td><td>/ˈhʌsəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>催促；奋力行进</td><td>hustle through the crowd<br>在人群中挤行</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She hustled the children into the car before the rain.  她在下雨前把孩子们匆匆赶上车。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 scoot</td><td>/ˈskuːt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>疾走，快速离开</td><td>scoot down the hall<br>沿走廊疾走</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The cat scooted under the bed as soon as we arrived.  我们一到，猫就飞快钻到床底下。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 scurry</td><td>/ˈskɜːri/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>急匆匆地跑</td><td>scurry for cover<br>急忙找掩护</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Mice scurried across the floor when the light came on.  灯一亮，老鼠们急匆匆跑过地板。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 scuttle</td><td>/ˈskʌtəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>急匆匆地跑</td><td>scuttle across the floor<br>在地上疾跑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Crabs scuttled sideways over the wet rocks at low tide.  退潮时螃蟹在湿岩石上横着疾跑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 shoot</td><td>/ˈʃuːt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>射出，发射；迅速射出（光线等）</td><td>shoot a glance at<br>朝…投去一瞥</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The lighthouse shot a beam of light across the dark water.  灯塔把一束光射过漆黑的水面。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 trot</td><td>/ˈtrɑːt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>不及物动词、名词</td><td>快步走，疾走</td><td>trot along the path<br>沿小路快步走</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The pony trotted happily beside the fence for a while.  小马在栅栏边欢快地快步走了一阵。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 dawdle</td><td>/ˈdɔːdl/</td><td>☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>拖拖拉拉，磨蹭</td><td>dawdle on the way<br>路上拖拖拉拉</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She dawdled over her homework until it was nearly midnight.  她做作业拖拖拉拉，一直到近午夜。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 crawl</td><td>/ˈkrɔːl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>不及物动词、名词</td><td>缓慢行进；爬行</td><td>crawl along the highway<br>在公路上缓慢行进</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Traffic crawled along the flooded highway for nearly three hours.  车流在被淹的公路上缓慢行进了近三个小时。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 creep</td><td>/kriːp/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>不及物动词、名词</td><td>缓慢地行进</td><td>creep along slowly<br>缓慢地爬行前进</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Traffic crept forward only a few meters at a time all morning.  整个上午车流每次只缓慢前进几米。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 poke</td><td>/ˈpoʊk/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>缓慢行进，闲荡</td><td>poke along the road<br>沿路慢慢晃</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old truck poked along the highway at thirty miles an hour.  那辆旧卡车以每小时三十英里在公路上慢慢晃。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>humbug</b></td><td>/ˈhʌmbʌɡ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>骗人的话；骗子；欺骗</td><td>an outright humbug<br>十足的骗子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Most of his grand promises turned out to be pure humbug.  他那些宏大的承诺大多不过是骗人的空话。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 charlatan</td><td>/ˈʃɑːrlətən/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>骗子，装懂的人</td><td>expose a charlatan<br>揭穿骗子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The journalist exposed the charlatan selling fake medicine.  记者揭穿了那个卖假药的骗子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fake</td><td>/ˈfeɪk/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词、动词</td><td>假的，伪造的；伪造品</td><td>a fake diploma<br>假文凭</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The gallery unknowingly displayed a fake for almost ten years.  这家画廊不知情地展出了一件赝品近十年。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fraud</td><td>/ˈfrɔːd/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>骗子；欺诈行为</td><td>expose a fraud<br>揭露骗局</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The so-called healer turned out to be an outright fraud.  那位所谓的治疗师原来是个彻头彻尾的骗子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hoaxer</td><td>/ˈhoʊksər/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>骗子，行骗恶作剧者</td><td>an anonymous hoaxer<br>匿名的行骗者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A hoaxer sent fake fossils to museums for nearly a decade.  一个骗子近十年间向博物馆寄送假化石。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 imposter</td><td>/ɪmˈpɑːstər/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>冒充者，骗子</td><td>unmask an imposter<br>揭穿冒充者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The imposter had used a dead man's name for twelve years.  这个冒充者用一个死者的名字生活了十二年。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mountebank</td><td>/ˈmaʊntɪbæŋk/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>骗子，江湖郎中</td><td>a smooth-talking mountebank<br>口若悬河的骗子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The town square once drew mountebanks selling miracle cures.  镇中心广场曾吸引卖神奇药方的江湖骗子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 phony</td><td>/ˈfoʊni/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>假的，冒充的；骗子</td><td>a phony accent<br>假装的口音</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He used a phony name when renting the small apartment.  他租那间小公寓时用了假名。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pretender</td><td>/prɪˈtendər/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>冒充者，觊觎者</td><td>a pretender to the throne<br>觊觎王位者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Three pretenders claimed the crown within a single year.  一年之内有三个觊觎者宣称拥有王位。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 quack</td><td>/kwæk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>骗子医生，江湖郎中</td><td>a medical quack<br>冒牌医生</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The medical quack sold colored water as a cure for every illness.  那个江湖郎中把有色的水当作万病灵药出售。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 quacksalver</del></td><td>/ˈkwækˌsælvər/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>江湖郎中，骗子</td><td>an old quacksalver<br>老江湖郎中</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The village quacksalver sold colored water as powerful medicine.  村里的江湖郎中把有色的水当灵药卖。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ringer</td><td>/ˈrɪŋər/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>冒名顶替者</td><td>bring in a ringer<br>找人冒名顶替</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The team lost when the other side used a ringer.  对方用了冒名顶替的选手，我们队因此输了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sham</td><td>/ʃæm/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词、形容词</td><td>掩饰，假装；虚假的</td><td>a sham marriage<br>假婚姻</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The couple admitted their marriage was a sham arranged for the visa.  这对夫妻承认他们的婚姻是为签证安排的假婚姻。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>hilarious</b></td><td>/hɪˈleriəs/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>非常好笑的</td><td>a hilarious joke<br>一个极好笑的玩笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His story about the runaway goat was absolutely hilarious.  他讲的那只逃跑山羊的故事实在太好笑了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hysterical</td><td>/hɪˈsterɪkəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>情绪失控的，歇斯底里的</td><td>become hysterical with grief<br>因悲伤而失控</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The child grew hysterical when she lost sight of her mother.  孩子看不见母亲时变得歇斯底里。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ludicrous</td><td>/ˈluːdɪkrəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>荒唐的，可笑的</td><td>a ludicrous idea<br>可笑的想法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The price they asked was simply ludicrous.  他们的要价简直可笑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ridiculous</td><td>/rɪˈdɪkjələs/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>极其可笑的，滑稽的</td><td>look absolutely ridiculous<br>看上去滑稽极了</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The clown's ridiculous hat made the children laugh out loud.  小丑那顶滑稽的帽子逗得孩子们大笑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 screaming</td><td>/ˈskriːmɪŋ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>极其滑稽的；尖叫的</td><td>a screaming comedy<br>滑稽至极的喜剧</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The show's screaming final scene had the audience laughing helplessly.  这出戏滑稽至极的最后一幕让观众笑得停不下来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 sidesplitting</del></td><td>/ˈsaɪdˌsplɪtɪŋ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人捧腹的</td><td>a sidesplitting joke<br>令人捧腹的笑话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His sidesplitting stories kept the whole table laughing all evening.  他令人捧腹的故事让整桌人笑了一晚上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 uproarious</td><td>/ʌpˈrɔriəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>非常滑稽的；喧闹的</td><td>uproarious laughter<br>哄堂大笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The comedian's last joke drew uproarious laughter from the crowd.  喜剧演员最后一个笑话引来观众的哄堂大笑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 humorless</td><td>/ˈhjuːmərləs/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>缺乏幽默感的，严肃的</td><td>a humorless manager<br>毫无幽默感的经理</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her humorless boss never laughed at any office joke.  她那毫无幽默感的老板从不为办公室笑话发笑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 lame</td><td>/ˈleɪm/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无趣的，蹩脚的</td><td>a lame joke<br>蹩脚的笑话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His lame joke got nothing but an awkward silence from everyone.  他那个蹩脚的笑话只换来大家尴尬的沉默。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 unamusing</del></td><td>/ˌʌnəˈmjuːzɪŋ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不好笑的，无趣的</td><td>an unamusing skit<br>一个不好笑的小品</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The comedian's unamusing routine emptied half the room.  这位喜剧演员不好笑的段子让半屋人都走了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 uncomic</del></td><td>/ʌnˈkɑːmɪk/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不滑稽的，不好笑的</td><td>an uncomic performance<br>毫不滑稽的表演</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His uncomic delivery made the funny script fall flat.  他毫不滑稽的表达让搞笑剧本变得平淡。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unfunny</td><td>/ʌnˈfʌni/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不好笑的，无趣的</td><td>an unfunny joke<br>一个不好笑的玩笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The audience sat silent through his unfunny opening joke.  观众在他不好笑的开场玩笑中沉默不语。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>histrionic</b></td><td>/ˌhɪstriˈɑːnɪk/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>夸张做作的</td><td>histrionic gestures<br>夸张做作的手势</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her histrionic response to the small mistake embarrassed the whole team.  她对这个小错误夸张做作的反应让全队难堪。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dramatic</td><td>/drəˈmætɪk/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>戏剧性的；夸张的；显著的</td><td>a dramatic improvement<br>显著的改善</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her dramatic gestures made the simple announcement seem like a tragedy.  她夸张的手势让这个简单的通知显得像一场悲剧。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 melodramatic</td><td>/ˌmelədrəˈmætɪk/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>夸张做作的，煽情的</td><td>a melodramatic reaction<br>夸张的反应</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her melodramatic sighs made the argument seem almost funny.  她夸张的叹气让这场争吵显得有点好笑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 operatic</td><td>/ˌɑːpəˈrætɪk/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>歌剧般夸张的</td><td>an operatic display of grief<br>歌剧般的悲伤</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He made an operatic scene over a simple parking ticket.  他为一张普通罚单闹得像演歌剧。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 stagy</del></td><td>/ˈsteɪdʒi/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>做作的，戏剧化的</td><td>a stagy performance<br>做作的表演</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His stagy bow at the door annoyed the other guests.  他在门口做作的鞠躬让其他客人反感。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 theatrical</td><td>/θiˈætrɪkl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>做作的，夸张的，矫揉造作的</td><td>a theatrical gesture<br>夸张的姿态</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He raised his arms in a theatrical gesture of despair.  他张开双臂，做出夸张的绝望姿态。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 affected</td><td>/əˈfektɪd/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>装出来的，做作的</td><td>an affected accent<br>装出来的口音</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She spoke with an affected accent that fooled nobody present.  她用装出来的口音说话，没骗过在场任何人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 undramatic</td><td>/ˌʌndrəˈmætɪk/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>平淡的，不戏剧化的</td><td>an undramatic ending<br>平淡的结局</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The trial had a quiet, undramatic ending that pleased nobody.  审判以平淡无波的方式收场，谁都不满意。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>hive</b></td><td>/haɪv/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>忙碌之地；储备，积累</td><td>a hive of activity<br>热闹忙碌之地</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 By dawn the kitchen was a hive of noisy and cheerful activity.  黎明时，厨房已成了喧闹欢快的忙碌之地。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>hoard</b></td><td>/hɔːrd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>贮藏，秘藏</td><td>hoard food and water<br>贮藏食物和水</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Squirrels hoard nuts in hollow trees long before the first frost.  松鼠早在初霜之前就把坚果贮藏在树洞里。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 cache</td><td>/kæʃ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>囤货，藏货</td><td>a cache of weapons<br>一批藏匿的武器</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Police discovered a cache of weapons beneath the barn floor.  警方在谷仓地板下发现了一批藏匿的武器。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 stash</td><td>/ˈstæʃ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>藏匿，私藏；藏匿物</td><td>stash the money away<br>把钱藏起来</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She stashed the letters in a box under her bed.  她把那些信藏在床下的盒子里。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 stockpile</td><td>/ˈstɑːkˌpaɪl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>储备，大量囤积</td><td>a stockpile of grain<br>储备的大批粮食</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The country stockpiled fuel before the long winter arrived.  这个国家在漫长冬季到来前囤积燃料。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 store</td><td>/ˈstɔːr/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>储存，贮藏；储备物</td><td>store food for winter<br>为冬天储存食物</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Squirrels store nuts in hollow trees every autumn.  松鼠每年秋天把坚果藏在树洞里。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 squirrel</td><td>/ˈskwɜːrəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>贮藏，把东西藏起来</td><td>squirrel away money<br>把钱藏起来</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She squirrels away a little cash from every paycheck.  她从每份薪水里存下一点现金。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 lavish</td><td>/ˈlævɪʃ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词、及物动词</td><td>奢侈的，大量的，无节制的；挥霍，浪费</td><td>a lavish banquet<br>奢华的宴会</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They hosted a lavish banquet for three hundred guests in the garden.  他们在花园里为三百位客人举办了一场奢华的宴会。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>hoary</b></td><td>/ˈhɔːri/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>极老的</td><td>a hoary old joke<br>老掉牙的笑话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He told the same hoary anecdote at every family dinner.  他每次家庭聚餐都讲同一个老掉牙的轶事。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 aged</td><td>/ˈeɪdʒɪd/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>年老的；陈年的</td><td>an aged farmer<br>一位年老的农民</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The aged oak still shades the whole courtyard in summer.  这棵老橡树夏天仍能荫蔽整个庭院。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 antique</td><td>/ænˈtiːk/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>古老的，古董的；古董</td><td>an antique wooden chair<br>一把古董木椅</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She collects antique clocks that were made in nineteenth-century France.  她收集在十九世纪法国制造的古董钟。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dateless</td><td>/ˈdeɪtləs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>古老得无从考证的</td><td>dateless folk songs<br>年代不明的民歌</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 These dateless legends were told long before anyone wrote them down.  这些年代不明的传说早在有人记录之前就流传着。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 immemorial</td><td>/ˌɪməˈmɔːriəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>远古的，久远的</td><td>from time immemorial<br>自远古以来</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Farmers here have shared the same well from time immemorial.  自远古以来，这里的农民一直共用同一口井。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 antediluvian</td><td>/ˌæntidɪˈluːviən/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>非常古老的；过时的</td><td>antediluvian attitudes<br>过时的观念</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His antediluvian views on marriage shocked the younger guests.  他对婚姻的陈腐观念让年轻的客人们大为震惊。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 modern</td><td>/ˈmɑːdərn/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>现代的，新式的</td><td>modern architecture<br>现代建筑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Modern phones can do far more than early computers could.  现代手机的功能远超早期的计算机。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 new</td><td>/ˈnuː/</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>新的，最近出现的</td><td>a brand new car<br>一辆全新的汽车</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They moved into a new apartment near the river.  他们搬进了河边一套新公寓。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 recent</td><td>/ˈriːsənt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>最近的，近来的</td><td>in recent years<br>近几年来</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Recent studies suggest that short naps improve memory.  最近的研究表明小睡能改善记忆。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 4

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>hoax</b></td><td>/hoʊks/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>欺骗</td><td>an elaborate hoax<br>精心设计的骗局</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The photograph turned out to be an elaborate hoax created by students.  那张照片原来是学生们精心设计的骗局。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bamboozle</td><td>/bæmˈbuzəl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>欺骗，蒙骗</td><td>bamboozle the customers<br>欺骗顾客</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The salesman bamboozled them into buying a worthless extended warranty.  推销员骗他们买了毫无价值的延保。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 beguile</td><td>/bɪˈɡaɪl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>欺骗，诱骗；使着迷</td><td>beguile the audience<br>令观众着迷</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The magician could beguile an audience for a full hour.  这位魔术师能让观众着迷整整一小时。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bluff</td><td>/blʌf/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>虚张声势地骗人，唬人</td><td>bluff your way through<br>靠虚张声势混过去</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He tried to bluff his way through the whole interview.  他试图靠虚张声势混过整场面试。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 con</td><td>/ˈkɑːn/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>欺骗，骗取；骗局</td><td>con artist<br>骗子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The swindler conned elderly victims out of their life savings.  那个骗子骗走了老年受害者的毕生积蓄。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 cozen</td><td>/ˈkʌzn/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>诱骗</td><td>cozen him into agreeing<br>诱骗他同意</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The swindler cozened elderly widows out of their modest life savings.  那个骗子诱骗年老的寡妇们交出微薄的积蓄。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 delude</td><td>/dɪˈluːd/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>欺骗，使误信</td><td>delude oneself<br>自欺欺人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Do not delude yourself into thinking the danger has passed.  别欺骗自己，以为危险已经过去。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dupe</td><td>/duːp/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>欺骗</td><td>dupe trusting investors<br>欺骗轻信的投资者</td></tr>
