<tr><td colspan="7">例 The scheme duped trusting investors out of nearly a million dollars.  这个骗局欺骗轻信的投资者，骗走近百万美元。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fake out</td><td>/feɪk ˈaʊt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>欺骗，蒙骗，晃过</td><td>fake out the defender<br>晃过防守球员</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The guard faked out two defenders and scored quite easily.  这名后卫晃过两名防守者，轻松得分。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 gull</td><td>/ɡʌl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>欺骗</td><td>gull the unwary<br>欺骗粗心的人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The swindler gulled dozens of elderly investors out of their savings.  骗子欺骗了数十位老年投资者，骗走他们的积蓄。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hoodwink</td><td>/ˈhʊdwɪŋk/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>欺骗</td><td>hoodwink the voters<br>欺骗选民</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company hoodwinked customers with promises it could never possibly keep.  这家公司用根本无法兑现的承诺欺骗顾客。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 take in</td><td>/teɪk ˈɪn/</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>欺骗；收留；领会</td><td>be taken in by<br>被…欺骗</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Don't be taken in by his charming manner and generous promises.  别被他迷人的举止和大方的承诺骗了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>hodgepodge</b></td><td>/ˈhɑːdʒpɑːdʒ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>大杂烩</td><td>a hodgepodge of styles<br>风格的大杂烩</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The house is a hodgepodge of every style he admired.  这房子是他欣赏过的各种风格的大杂烩。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 agglomeration</td><td>/əˌɡlɑːməˈreɪʃən/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>聚集物，杂乱一堆</td><td>an agglomeration of shops<br>商铺的聚集</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The district is an agglomeration of tiny workshops and cafes.  这个区是小作坊和咖啡馆的聚集地。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 assortment</td><td>/əˈsɔːrtmənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>各式各样的一批</td><td>an assortment of tools<br>各式工具</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The box held an odd assortment of buttons and coins.  盒子里装着一堆杂七杂八的纽扣和硬币。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 collage</td><td>/kəˈlɑːʒ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>拼贴画，混合物</td><td>a collage of photographs<br>照片拼贴</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her film is a collage of interviews and old home movies.  她的影片是访谈与旧家庭录像的拼贴。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 medley</td><td>/ˈmedli/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>大杂烩，混合物</td><td>a medley of flavors<br>各种风味的混合</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The buffet table offered a medley of flavors from six different countries.  自助餐台上供应着来自六个不同国家的各式风味。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mishmash</td><td>/ˈmɪʃˌmæʃ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>大杂烩，混杂物</td><td>a mishmash of ideas<br>想法的大杂烩</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The report was a mishmash of statistics and personal opinion.  这份报告是数据与个人意见的大杂烩。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pastiche</td><td>/pæˈstiːʃ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>（带嘲讽的）模仿；大杂烩</td><td>a clever pastiche<br>巧妙的模仿之作</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The novel is a clever pastiche of Victorian detective fiction.  这部小说是对维多利亚时代侦探小说的巧妙模仿。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 potpourri</td><td>/ˌpoʊpʊˈriː/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>混合物，大杂烩</td><td>a potpourri of songs<br>歌曲的大杂烩</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The concert offered a potpourri of jazz, folk and blues.  音乐会呈现了爵士、民谣与蓝调的大杂烩。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>homage</b></td><td>/ˈhɑːmɪdʒ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>尊敬，敬意</td><td>pay homage to<br>向…致敬</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Thousands came to pay homage to the poet on her ninetieth birthday.  数千人在诗人九十岁生日那天前来致敬。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 commendation</td><td>/ˌkɑːmənˈdeɪʃən/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>表扬，嘉奖</td><td>receive a commendation for bravery<br>因勇敢获得嘉奖</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The officer received a commendation for rescuing the trapped driver.  这名警官因救出被困司机而获嘉奖。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 eulogy</td><td>/ˈjuːlədʒi/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>悼词；颂词，赞辞</td><td>deliver a moving eulogy<br>致感人的悼词</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She delivered a moving eulogy at her grandfather's funeral.  她在祖父的葬礼上致了感人的悼词。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hymn</td><td>/hɪm/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>赞歌，赞美诗</td><td>sing a hymn<br>唱赞美诗</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The whole congregation stood up to sing a hymn of thanksgiving.  全体教众站起身来唱一首感恩的赞美诗。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 paean</td><td>/ˈpiːən/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>欢乐颂，赞扬</td><td>a paean to freedom<br>对自由的赞歌</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The review read like a paean to the young violinist and her talent.  那篇评论读来像是对这位年轻小提琴手及其才华的赞歌。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 panegyric</td><td>/ˌpænəˈdʒɪrɪk/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>赞颂之词，颂文</td><td>deliver a long panegyric<br>发表一篇长颂词</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The retirement dinner ended with a long panegyric to the old professor.  退休晚宴以对这位老教授的长篇颂词结束。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 salutation</td><td>/ˌsæljuˈteɪʃn/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>致敬，问候</td><td>a salutation to the winners<br>向获胜者致敬</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The crowd offered a warm salutation to the returning champions.  人群向归来的冠军们热情致敬。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 tribute</td><td>/ˈtrɪbjuːt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>称颂，颂词</td><td>pay tribute to her work<br>对她的工作表示称颂</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Colleagues paid warm tribute to her forty years of patient teaching.  同事们对她四十年的耐心教学致以热情的颂扬。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 dithyramb</del></td><td>/ˈdɪθəræm/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>热情洋溢的赞歌</td><td>a dithyramb to spring<br>献给春天的赞歌</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His review was less an essay than a dithyramb to the singer.  他的评论不像文章，更像献给歌手的狂热赞歌。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 disrespect</td><td>/ˌdɪsrɪˈspekt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>轻蔑，不敬；不尊重</td><td>show disrespect for elders<br>对长辈不敬</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Interrupting her that way showed real disrespect.  那样打断她实在很不尊重人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>homely</b></td><td>/ˈhoʊmli/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>其貌不扬的，朴素简单的</td><td>a homely face<br>其貌不扬的脸</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She had a homely but kind face that put strangers at ease.  她有一张其貌不扬却和善的脸，让陌生人感到自在。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hideous</td><td>/ˈhɪdiəs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>非常丑陋的</td><td>a hideous mask<br>丑陋可怕的面具</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They tore down the hideous concrete tower that blocked the view.  他们拆掉了那座挡住视野的丑陋混凝土塔楼。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unappealing</td><td>/ˌʌnəˈpiːlɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>没有吸引力的，不讨喜的</td><td>an unappealing dish<br>不讨喜的菜</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The cold gray soup looked completely unappealing to all of us.  那碗冷灰色的汤在我们看来毫无食欲。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unattractive</td><td>/ˌʌnəˈtræktɪv/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>没有吸引力的，难看的</td><td>an unattractive offer<br>没有吸引力的报价</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The job was well paid but otherwise thoroughly unattractive.  这份工作薪水不错，但其他方面毫无吸引力。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 uncomely</del></td><td>/ʌnˈkʌmli/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不好看的，不雅观的</td><td>an uncomely dress<br>不好看的裙子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She wore an uncomely dress that hid her real charm.  她穿了件不好看的裙子，掩住了她真正的魅力。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unsightly</td><td>/ʌnˈsaɪtli/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>难看的，刺眼的</td><td>unsightly stains<br>难看的污渍</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Unsightly stains covered the wall behind the old kitchen sink.  难看的污渍覆盖了旧厨房水槽后的墙面。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 pulchritudinous</del></td><td>/ˌpʌlkrəˈtudənəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>美丽的，貌美的</td><td>a pulchritudinous face<br>美丽的面容</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The poet described his pulchritudinous neighbor in eight long stanzas.  诗人用八段长诗描写他貌美的邻居。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 aesthetic</td><td>/esˈθetɪk/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>审美的，美的</td><td>aesthetic appeal<br>审美上的吸引力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The building's aesthetic appeal outweighs its many practical problems.  这栋楼的美感胜过它的诸多实用缺陷。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 attractive</td><td>/əˈtræktɪv/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有吸引力的，迷人的</td><td>an attractive offer<br>有吸引力的提议</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company made him an attractive offer he could not refuse.  公司给了他一个无法拒绝的诱人条件。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 beautiful</td><td>/ˈbjuːtəfəl/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>美丽的，美好的</td><td>a beautiful view<br>美丽的景色</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 From the hill we had a beautiful view of the whole bay.  从山上我们看到整个海湾的美丽景色。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 comely</td><td>/ˈkʌmli/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>秀丽的，好看的</td><td>a comely young woman<br>秀丽的年轻女子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A comely young woman greeted us politely at the gate.  一位秀丽的年轻女子在门口礼貌地迎接我们。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 gorgeous</td><td>/ˈɡɔːrdʒəs/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>极美的，绚丽的</td><td>a gorgeous sunset<br>绚丽的落日</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We watched a gorgeous sunset from the hotel balcony.  我们在酒店阳台上看了一场绚丽的落日。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 handsome</td><td>/ˈhænsəm/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>英俊的，好看的</td><td>a handsome young man<br>英俊的年轻人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He was a handsome man with unusually kind eyes.  他是个英俊的男人，眼神格外和善。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 knockout</td><td>/ˈnɑːˌkaʊt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>击倒昏迷；击倒获胜</td><td>win by a knockout<br>以击倒对手获胜</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The champion ended the fight with a knockout in round two.  冠军在第二回合以击倒结束了比赛。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 lovely</td><td>/ˈlʌvli/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>可爱的，美丽的</td><td>a lovely garden<br>美丽的花园</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We spent a lovely afternoon by the quiet lake.  我们在安静的湖边度过了一个美好的下午。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 pretty</td><td>/ˈprɪti/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>漂亮的，好看的</td><td>a pretty dress<br>漂亮的裙子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She wore a pretty blue dress to the party.  她穿了一条漂亮的蓝裙子去派对。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 seemly</td><td>/ˈsiːmli/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>得体的，合宜的</td><td>seemly conduct in public<br>公共场合得体的举止</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The teacher expects seemly conduct from her students in public places.  老师期望学生在公共场所举止得体。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 sightly</td><td>/ˈsaɪtli/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>好看的，悦目的</td><td>a sightly garden<br>悦目的花园</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They turned the empty lot into a sightly little garden.  他们把空地改成了一个悦目的小花园。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 stunning</td><td>/ˈstʌnɪŋ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人震惊的；极漂亮的</td><td>a stunning upset<br>令人震惊的逆转</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The underdog scored a stunning victory in the final round.  弱队在最后一轮取得令人震惊的胜利。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 taking</td><td>/ˈteɪkɪŋ/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>迷人的，讨人喜欢的</td><td>a taking manner<br>讨人喜欢的举止</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The child had a taking manner that won everyone over.  这孩子讨人喜欢的举止赢得了所有人的心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 well-favored</td><td>/wel ˈfeɪvərd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>相貌好看的</td><td>a well-favored young man<br>相貌好看的年轻人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He was a well-favored young man with easy country manners.  他是个相貌好看、举止随和的乡下青年。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>homily</b></td><td>/ˈhɑːməli/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>冗长乏味的道德讲演或训诫</td><td>deliver a homily<br>作一番训诫</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We had to sit through another homily about punctuality and respect.  我们只得又听了一通关于守时与尊重的冗长训诫。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 banality</td><td>/bəˈnæləti/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>平庸；陈腐的话</td><td>the banality of his speech<br>他讲话的平庸乏味</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The film offered nothing but banalities about growing up.  这部电影只提供了些关于成长的陈腐说法。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bromide</td><td>/ˈbroʊmaɪd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>陈词滥调；镇静剂</td><td>a tired old bromide<br>老掉牙的陈词</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His speech ended with a tired old bromide about hard work.  他的演讲以一句关于努力工作的陈腐套话作结。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 chestnut</td><td>/ˈtʃesnʌt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>老掉牙的话；栗子</td><td>an old chestnut<br>老掉牙的说法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He repeated that old chestnut about hard work and luck.  他又重复了那句关于努力与运气的老话。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 cliché</del></td><td>/kliːˈʃeɪ/</td><td>☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>陈词滥调，老套的说法</td><td>an overused cliché<br>用滥的陈词</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Saying that time heals everything has become a tired cliché.  说时间治愈一切已成为老套的陈词滥调。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 groaner</del></td><td>/ˈɡroʊnər/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>让人无语的烂笑话</td><td>an old groaner<br>老套的烂笑话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His pun was such a groaner that everyone just sighed.  他的双关语烂得让所有人只能叹气。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 platitude</td><td>/ˈplætɪtuːd/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>陈词滥调</td><td>utter a platitude<br>说一句陈词滥调</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The speech offered nothing but platitudes about hard work and dreams.  这篇演讲除了关于努力与梦想的陈词滥调外别无他物。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 shibboleth</td><td>/ˈʃɪbəˌleθ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>陈腐的口号；标志性用语</td><td>an outworn shibboleth<br>过时的老口号</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Free trade became a shibboleth repeated by every candidate.  自由贸易成了每位候选人都挂在嘴上的口号。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>homogenize</b></td><td>/həˈmɑːdʒənaɪz/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使统一化</td><td>homogenize the curriculum<br>使课程统一化</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 National testing tends to homogenize teaching across very different schools.  全国统考往往使差异很大的学校的教学趋于统一。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 formalize</td><td>/ˈfɔːrməˌlaɪz/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使正式化，使规范化</td><td>formalize the agreement<br>使协议正式化</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The two firms decided to formalize their loose partnership.  两家公司决定把松散的合作正式化。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 normalize</td><td>/ˈnɔːrməˌlaɪz/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使正常化，使标准化</td><td>normalize relations<br>使关系正常化</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The treaty helped normalize relations between the two countries.  这项条约帮助两国关系正常化。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 regularize</td><td>/ˈrɛɡjələˌraɪz/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使规范化，使正常化</td><td>regularize the procedure<br>使程序规范化</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new law regularized the status of thousands of workers.  新法规使数千名工人的身份合规化。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>hone</b></td><td>/hoʊn/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>提升，改进</td><td>hone your skills<br>磨练你的技能</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She spent the summer honing her interviewing skills at a local paper.  她整个夏天都在一家本地报社磨练采访技能。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ameliorate</td><td>/əˈmiːliəreɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>改善，改进</td><td>ameliorate the situation<br>改善局面</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new subsidy did little to ameliorate rural poverty.  新补贴对改善农村贫困几乎没有作用。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 cultivate</td><td>/ˈkʌltɪveɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>提升，加强；种植，培养</td><td>cultivate a friendship<br>培养一段友谊</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She cultivated a wide network of contacts during her years abroad.  她在国外的岁月里培养了广泛的人际关系网。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 improve</td><td>/ɪmˈpruːv/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>改善，提高</td><td>improve the quality of<br>提高…的质量</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Regular practice quickly improved her pronunciation and her confidence.  经常练习很快改善了她的发音和自信。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 meliorate</del></td><td>/ˈmiliəˌreɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>改善，使变好</td><td>meliorate living conditions<br>改善生活条件</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Simple drainage work meliorated conditions in the crowded district.  简单的排水工程改善了拥挤街区的状况。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 perfect</td><td>/ˈpɜːrfɪkt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词、及物动词</td><td>完美的；使完善</td><td>a perfect score<br>满分成绩</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He spent years trying to perfect his technique on the cello.  他花了数年时间钻研以完善自己的大提琴技巧。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 polish</td><td>/ˈpɑːlɪʃ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>擦亮，磨光；润色</td><td>polish the wooden floor<br>把木地板擦亮</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She polished her essay carefully before sending it in.  她仔细润色文章后才交上去。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 refine</td><td>/rɪˈfaɪn/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>提纯，精炼；改进</td><td>refine crude oil<br>精炼原油</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The plant refines crude oil into gasoline and other useful products.  这家工厂把原油提炼成汽油和其他有用的产品。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 aggravate</td><td>/ˈæɡrəveɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>加重，恶化；激怒</td><td>aggravate the situation<br>使局势恶化</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Running on the injury will only aggravate it.  带着伤跑步只会让伤势加重。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 deteriorate</td><td>/dɪˈtɪriəreɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>恶化，变坏</td><td>deteriorate rapidly<br>迅速恶化</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her health deteriorated sharply during the cold winter.  在寒冷的冬天里她的健康急剧恶化。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 worsen</td><td>/ˈwɜːrsən/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>使变坏，恶化</td><td>worsen over time<br>随时间恶化</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her cough worsened after a week outdoors in the cold.  在寒冷的户外待了一周后，她的咳嗽加重了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>hoodwink</b></td><td>/ˈhʊdwɪŋk/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>欺骗</td><td>hoodwink the voters<br>欺骗选民</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company hoodwinked customers with promises it could never possibly keep.  这家公司用根本无法兑现的承诺欺骗顾客。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 beguile</td><td>/bɪˈɡaɪl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>欺骗，诱骗；使着迷</td><td>beguile the audience<br>令观众着迷</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The magician could beguile an audience for a full hour.  这位魔术师能让观众着迷整整一小时。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 con</td><td>/ˈkɑːn/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>欺骗，骗取；骗局</td><td>con artist<br>骗子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The swindler conned elderly victims out of their life savings.  那个骗子骗走了老年受害者的毕生积蓄。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 delude</td><td>/dɪˈluːd/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>欺骗，使误信</td><td>delude oneself<br>自欺欺人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Do not delude yourself into thinking the danger has passed.  别欺骗自己，以为危险已经过去。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dupe</td><td>/duːp/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>欺骗</td><td>dupe trusting investors<br>欺骗轻信的投资者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The scheme duped trusting investors out of nearly a million dollars.  这个骗局欺骗轻信的投资者，骗走近百万美元。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fool</td><td>/ˈfuːl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>傻瓜，蠢人；愚弄</td><td>make a fool of oneself<br>让自己出丑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He felt like a fool after believing the obvious lie.  信了那个明显的谎言后，他觉得自己像个傻瓜。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hoax</td><td>/hoʊks/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>欺骗</td><td>an elaborate hoax<br>精心设计的骗局</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The photograph turned out to be an elaborate hoax created by students.  那张照片原来是学生们精心设计的骗局。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 humbug</td><td>/ˈhʌmbʌɡ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>骗人的话；骗子；欺骗</td><td>an outright humbug<br>十足的骗子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Most of his grand promises turned out to be pure humbug.  他那些宏大的承诺大多不过是骗人的空话。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 disabuse</td><td>/ˌdɪsəˈbjuːz/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>打消错误念头，纠正</td><td>disabuse him of the notion<br>打消他的错误念头</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It took hours to disabuse him of the notion that the test was simple.  花了好几个小时才打消他认为考试很简单的错误念头。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del><b>hortative</b></del></td><td>/ˈhɔːrtətɪv/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>鼓励的</td><td>a hortative speech<br>激励性的演讲</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The coach ended with a hortative shout that lifted every player.  教练最后一声激励的呼喊振奋了每位球员。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>heartfelt</b></td><td>/ˈhɑːrtfelt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>真心实意的</td><td>heartfelt thanks<br>真诚的感谢</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He offered heartfelt thanks to everyone who had supported his family.  他向所有支持过他家人的人表达了真心的感谢。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sincere</td><td>/sɪnˈsɪr/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>真诚的，诚挚的</td><td>a sincere apology<br>真诚的道歉</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her sincere apology repaired a friendship damaged for years.  她真诚的道歉修复了受损多年的友谊。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unfeigned</td><td>/ʌnˈfeɪnd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>真诚的，不做作的</td><td>unfeigned delight<br>真心的喜悦</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her unfeigned joy at the news warmed the whole room.  她听到消息时真心的喜悦温暖了整个屋子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 affected</td><td>/əˈfektɪd/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>装出来的，做作的</td><td>an affected accent<br>装出来的口音</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She spoke with an affected accent that fooled nobody present.  她用装出来的口音说话，没骗过在场任何人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 artificial</td><td>/ˌɑːrtəˈfɪʃəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>人造的；虚假的</td><td>artificial flowers<br>人造花</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The lobby was decorated with artificial flowers that never wilted.  大厅用永不枯萎的人造花装饰。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 false</td><td>/ˈfɔːls/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>虚假的，不真诚的</td><td>a false smile<br>虚假的笑容</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His false praise fooled nobody at the small dinner table.  他虚假的赞美在那张小餐桌上骗不了任何人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 feigned</td><td>/ˈfeɪnd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>假装的，伪装的</td><td>feigned surprise<br>假装的惊讶</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He listened with feigned interest while checking his phone.  他一边看手机，一边假装感兴趣地听着。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 insincere</td><td>/ˌɪnsɪnˈsɪr/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不诚实的，虚伪的</td><td>an insincere apology<br>虚伪的道歉</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His insincere apology only made the situation considerably worse.  他虚伪的道歉只让情况更糟。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 5

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>hubris</b></td><td>/ˈhjuːbrɪs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>狂妄自大</td><td>punished for his hubris<br>因狂妄而受罚</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His hubris led him to ignore every warning from his own advisers.  他的狂妄自大使他无视自己顾问的所有警告。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 humility</td><td>/hjuˈmɪlɪti/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>谦逊，谦卑</td><td>accept with humility<br>谦逊地接受</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He accepted the prize with genuine humility and short thanks.  他以真诚的谦逊和简短的致谢接受了奖项。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>humble</b></td><td>/ˈhʌmbl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>卑微的，地位低下的；谦逊的</td><td>humble origins<br>卑微的出身</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He rose from humble origins to lead the company.  他出身卑微，最终领导了这家公司。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 modest</td><td>/ˈmɑːdəst/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>谦虚的；不大的，适度的</td><td>modest about her success<br>对成功很谦虚</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He stayed modest about his award and thanked his team.  他对自己的奖项很谦虚，还感谢了团队。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unassuming</td><td>/ˌʌnəˈsuːmɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>谦逊的，不张扬的</td><td>quiet and unassuming<br>安静谦逊</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The famous author was surprisingly unassuming in person.  这位著名作家本人出乎意料地谦逊。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unpretentious</td><td>/ˌʌnprɪˈtenʃəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不炫耀的，朴实低调的</td><td>an unpretentious restaurant<br>朴实的小餐馆</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They live in a small, unpretentious house near the river.  他们住在河边一间朴实的小房子里。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 demure</td><td>/dɪˈmjʊr/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>端庄的，娴静的</td><td>a demure smile<br>娴静的微笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She gave a demure nod and said nothing more.  她端庄地点了点头，没再多说。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 down-to-earth</td><td>/ˌdaʊn tu ˈɜrθ/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>务实的，不摆架子的</td><td>a down-to-earth manner<br>务实的作风</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Despite his fame he remained a down-to-earth neighbor.  尽管成名，他仍是个不摆架子的邻居。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 base</td><td>/ˈbeɪs/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>卑劣的，低下的</td><td>base motives<br>卑劣的动机</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He acted from base motives of greed and envy.  他出于贪婪和嫉妒的卑劣动机行事。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 menial</td><td>/ˈmiːniəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>卑贱的，低下的</td><td>menial tasks<br>低贱的杂活</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She took menial jobs to pay for night school.  她做低微的工作来支付夜校学费。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 servile</td><td>/ˈsɜːrvl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>卑屈的，奴性的</td><td>servile flattery<br>奴性的谄媚</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His servile manner toward the boss annoyed everyone.  他对老板卑躬屈膝的态度让大家反感。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 slavish</td><td>/ˈsleɪvɪʃ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>盲从的，奴性的</td><td>slavish imitation<br>盲目的模仿</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The design is a slavish imitation of an older model.  这个设计是对旧款的盲目模仿。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 demureness</del></td><td>/dɪˈmjʊrnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>端庄，娴静</td><td>her quiet demureness<br>她安静的端庄</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her demureness at dinner hid a very sharp wit.  她餐桌上的娴静掩藏着极机敏的才思。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 humbleness</td><td>/ˈhʌmbəlnəs/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>谦卑，谦逊</td><td>quiet humbleness<br>沉静的谦卑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old monk's humbleness taught the students more than words.  老僧的谦卑教给学生的比言语更多。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 lowliness</del></td><td>/ˈloʊlinəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>谦卑，地位卑微</td><td>the lowliness of his birth<br>他出身的卑微</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He spoke of the lowliness of his early years without shame.  他毫无羞愧地谈起早年的卑微。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 meekness</td><td>/ˈmiknəs/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>温顺，谦和</td><td>answer with meekness<br>以温顺回应</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her meekness should not be mistaken for weakness.  她的温顺不应被误当成软弱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 modesty</td><td>/ˈmɑːdəsti/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>谦虚，谦逊</td><td>false modesty<br>假谦虚</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her modesty about the discovery impressed the whole committee.  她对这一发现的谦逊让整个委员会印象深刻。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 arrogant</td><td>/ˈærəɡənt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>傲慢的，自大的</td><td>an arrogant manner<br>傲慢的举止</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His arrogant remarks cost him the respect of his colleagues.  他傲慢的言论使他失去了同事的尊重。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 bumptious</td><td>/ˈbʌmpʃəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>专横傲慢的，自以为是的</td><td>a bumptious young man<br>自以为是的年轻人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her bumptious manner annoyed the older members of the committee.  她自以为是的态度让委员会里年长的成员很反感。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 conceited</td><td>/kənˈsiːtɪd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>自负的，骄傲自满的</td><td>a conceited young actor<br>自负的年轻演员</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He grew conceited after winning a single small prize.  他只赢了一个小奖就变得自负起来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 egotistic</td><td>/ˌiːɡəˈtɪstɪk/</td><td>☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>自我中心的，自负的</td><td>an egotistic remark<br>自负的言论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The director was too egotistic to accept advice from anyone.  这位主管太自负，不肯接受任何人的建议。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 haughty</td><td>/ˈhɔːti/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>傲慢的，目中无人的</td><td>a haughty manner<br>傲慢的举止</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She gave the waiter a haughty look and said nothing.  她傲慢地看了服务员一眼，什么也没说。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 high-and-mighty</td><td>/ˌhaɪ ənd ˈmaɪti/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>傲慢自大的</td><td>a high-and-mighty tone<br>傲慢的语气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She dislikes his high-and-mighty way of correcting everyone.  她讨厌他那种傲慢地纠正每个人的方式。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 imperious</td><td>/ɪmˈpɪriəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>傲慢的，专横的</td><td>an imperious tone<br>专横的语气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her imperious manner made junior colleagues afraid to disagree.  她专横的态度让年轻同事不敢有异议。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 lordly</td><td>/ˈlɔːrdli/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>傲慢的；高贵的</td><td>a lordly manner<br>傲慢的举止</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He waved the waiter away with a lordly gesture.  他以傲慢的手势把侍者挥退。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 overweening</td><td>/ˌoʊvərˈwiːnɪŋ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>傲慢的，专横的</td><td>overweening pride<br>过度的骄傲</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His overweening confidence led him to ignore every warning from the engineers.  他过度的自信使他忽视了工程师的每一次警告。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 peremptory</td><td>/pəˈremptəri/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不容置疑的；专横傲慢的</td><td>a peremptory order<br>不容置疑的命令</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His peremptory tone left no room for questions or discussion.  他傲慢的语气不给任何提问或讨论的余地。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 pompous</td><td>/ˈpɑːmpəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>浮夸的；傲慢的</td><td>a pompous speech<br>浮夸的演讲</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The pompous speech bored everyone in the crowded town hall.  那场浮夸的演讲让拥挤的市政厅里每个人都厌烦。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 presuming</td><td>/prɪˈzuːmɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>自以为是的，冒失放肆的</td><td>a presuming stranger<br>冒失的陌生人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It was presuming of him to offer advice uninvited.  他未经邀请就给建议，实在冒失。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 presumptuous</td><td>/prɪˈzʌmptʃuəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>冒失的，放肆的</td><td>presumptuous of you<br>你太放肆了</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It seems presumptuous to criticize a book you have not read.  批评一本没读过的书，似乎太放肆了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 pretentious</td><td>/prɪˈtenʃəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>浮夸的，自命不凡的</td><td>pretentious prose<br>浮夸的文风</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His pretentious speech annoyed almost everyone in the audience.  他浮夸的演讲让几乎所有听众反感。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 self-assertive</td><td>/ˌsɛlf əˈsɜːrtɪv/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>坚持自我主张的，自信果断的</td><td>a self-assertive manner<br>自信果断的作风</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She learned to be more self-assertive in meetings.  她学会了在会议上更敢于表达主张。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 supercilious</td><td>/ˌsuːpərˈsɪliəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>高傲的，傲慢的</td><td>a supercilious smile<br>傲慢的微笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A supercilious smile crossed his face when he heard my question.  听到我的问题，他脸上掠过傲慢的微笑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 superior</td><td>/suːˈpɪriər/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>更好的，占优势的；上级</td><td>superior to the rest<br>优于其余各方</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 This model is clearly superior to the older version.  这个型号明显优于旧版。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 toplofty</del></td><td>/ˈtɑpˌlɔfti/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>自大的，趾高气扬的</td><td>a toplofty attitude<br>趾高气扬的态度</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His toplofty remarks about the village offended everyone there.  他对那个村子趾高气扬的评论得罪了当地所有人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 uppish</del></td><td>/ˈʌpɪʃ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>自命不凡的，傲慢的</td><td>an uppish clerk<br>傲慢的店员</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The uppish clerk barely looked at the waiting customers.  那个傲慢的店员几乎不看等候的顾客。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 uppity</td><td>/ˈʌpəti/</td><td>★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>自大的，不知本分的</td><td>an uppity attitude<br>自大的态度</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The manager called her uppity for asking about the budget.  经理因她询问预算而说她不知本分。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 arrogance</td><td>/ˈærəɡəns/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>傲慢，自大</td><td>sheer arrogance<br>十足的傲慢</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His arrogance made it impossible for colleagues to offer honest advice.  他的傲慢让同事们无法提出诚实的建议。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 assumption</td><td>/əˈsʌmpʃən/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>自大，傲慢；假设</td><td>an air of assumption<br>自大的神气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His quiet assumption of authority annoyed the older members.  他不声不响地自居权威，惹恼了年长成员。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 bumptiousness</del></td><td>/ˈbʌmpʃəsnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>自负，傲慢</td><td>youthful bumptiousness<br>年轻人的自负</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His bumptiousness made him unpopular with the entire team.  他的自负让整个团队都不喜欢他。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 hauteur</td><td>/hɔːˈtɜːr/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>傲慢，自大</td><td>an air of hauteur<br>傲慢的神态</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her hauteur offended the very people whose support she needed most.  她的傲慢恰恰得罪了她最需要支持的那些人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 pomposity</td><td>/pɑːmˈpɑːsəti/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>自大，浮夸</td><td>academic pomposity<br>学术上的浮夸</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The speech was ruined by pomposity and empty phrases.  演讲被浮夸和空话毁了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 presumption</td><td>/prɪˈzʌmpʃən/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>冒失无礼；假定</td><td>forgive my presumption<br>请原谅我的冒失</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It was sheer presumption to speak for the whole committee.  代表整个委员会发言纯属冒失无礼。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 superciliousness</del></td><td>/ˌsupərˈsɪliəsnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>傲慢，目中无人</td><td>chilly superciliousness<br>冷冰冰的傲慢</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The critic's superciliousness annoyed even his most loyal readers.  这位评论家的傲慢连最忠实的读者都反感。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>hedge</b></td><td>/hedʒ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>树篱，篱笆；用篱笆围住</td><td>a neatly trimmed hedge<br>修剪整齐的树篱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A neatly trimmed hedge separated the garden from the road.  一道修剪整齐的树篱把花园与马路隔开。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fudge</td><td>/ˈfʌdʒ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>含糊其词，回避；软糖</td><td>fudge the issue<br>回避问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The spokesman fudged the question about layoffs for twenty painful minutes.  发言人就裁员问题含糊其词了痛苦的二十分钟。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 equivocate</td><td>/ɪˈkwɪvəkeɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>（带有欺骗目的地）模棱两可地说，说谎话</td><td>equivocate under questioning<br>受问时含糊其辞</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She equivocated under questioning about the true state of her finances.  在被问及财务真实状况时，她含糊其辞。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 waffle</td><td>/ˈwɑːfl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>含糊其辞，支吾</td><td>waffle about the plan<br>就计划含糊其辞</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The candidate waffled about the tax plan for a full ten minutes.  这位候选人就税收方案含糊其辞地扯了整整十分钟。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 weasel</td><td>/ˈwiːzəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>耍滑逃避（责任）；黄鼠狼</td><td>weasel out of a promise<br>耍滑推掉承诺</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He tried to weasel out of paying his share.  他试图耍滑不付自己那份钱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>humor</b></td><td>/ˈhjuːmər/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>迁就；顺应；纵容</td><td>humor a small child<br>顺着小孩的心意</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She humored her grandfather by listening to the same story again.  她顺着祖父的心意，又听了一遍同样的故事。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 cater</td><td>/ˈkeɪtər/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>迎合，满足；承办酒席</td><td>cater to popular taste<br>迎合大众口味</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 This magazine caters to readers who love old racing cars.  这本杂志迎合喜爱老赛车的读者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 gratify</td><td>/ˈɡrætɪfaɪ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使满足，使高兴</td><td>gratify a desire<br>满足某种欲望</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It gratified her to see her students succeed.  看到学生成功让她很欣慰。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 indulge</td><td>/ɪnˈdʌldʒ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>纵容，迁就</td><td>indulge his every whim<br>纵容他的任性</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Grandparents often indulge children in ways parents will not.  祖辈常以父母不会的方式纵容孩子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>hurricane</b></td><td>/ˈhɜːrəkeɪn/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>飓风般的事物，引起动荡的事物</td><td>a hurricane of protest<br>抗议的风暴</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A hurricane of protest followed the sudden announcement of the new tax.  新税突然宣布之后，随即掀起一场抗议的风暴。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 disturbance</td><td>/dɪˈstɜːrbəns/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>骚乱，扰乱</td><td>cause a disturbance<br>引起骚乱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Police were called after a disturbance broke out in the bar.  酒吧里发生骚乱后警察被叫来了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 furor</td><td>/ˈfjʊrɔːr/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>喧闹，狂怒；激动，狂热</td><td>cause a public furor<br>引起公众哗然</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The painting caused a public furor when it was first shown.  这幅画首次展出时引起公众哗然。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pandemonium</td><td>/ˌpændɪˈmoʊniəm/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>喧嚣，一片混乱</td><td>pandemonium broke out<br>顿时一片混乱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Pandemonium broke out when the final goal was scored.  最后一个进球时全场一片沸腾混乱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 tumult</td><td>/ˈtuːmʌlt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>骚动，暴动</td><td>the tumult of the crowd<br>人群的骚动</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The tumult in the square grew louder as more protesters arrived.  随着更多抗议者到来，广场上的骚动越来越大。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 turmoil</td><td>/ˈtɜːrmɔɪl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>混乱，动荡，骚动</td><td>in political turmoil<br>处于政治动荡中</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The country remained in political turmoil for nearly a decade.  这个国家在近十年里一直处于政治动荡之中。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 uproar</td><td>/ˈʌˌprɔːr/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>喧嚣，骚动</td><td>cause an uproar<br>引起轩然大波</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new tax plan caused an uproar among small business owners.  新税收方案在小企业主中引起轩然大波。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>husband</b></td><td>/ˈhʌzbənd/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>节俭，勤俭持家</td><td>husband his resources<br>节省他的资源</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The village learned to husband its water carefully through the dry months.  村民学会了在旱季小心节省用水。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 budget</td><td>/ˈbʌdʒɪt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>编制预算，谋划；预算</td><td>budget for the trip<br>为旅行做预算</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We budgeted carefully for the trip and still ran short.  我们为旅行仔细做了预算，钱还是不够。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 conserve</td><td>/kənˈsɜːrv/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>节约，保存，保护</td><td>conserve energy<br>节约能源</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We must conserve water during the long summer drought.  漫长的夏季干旱期间我们必须节约用水。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 economize</td><td>/ɪˈkɑːnəˌmaɪz/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>节约，节省开支</td><td>economize on fuel<br>节省燃料</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The family had to economize sharply after he lost work.  他失业后一家人不得不大幅节省。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 dissipate</td><td>/ˈdɪsɪpeɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词、不及物动词</td><td>驱散，消散；浪费</td><td>dissipate the fog<br>驱散雾气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The morning mist dissipated as soon as the sun rose.  太阳一升起，晨雾就消散了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 lavish</td><td>/ˈlævɪʃ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词、及物动词</td><td>奢侈的，大量的，无节制的；挥霍，浪费</td><td>a lavish banquet<br>奢华的宴会</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They hosted a lavish banquet for three hundred guests in the garden.  他们在花园里为三百位客人举办了一场奢华的宴会。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 prodigalize</del></td><td>/ˈprɑdəɡəˌlaɪz/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>挥霍，浪费</td><td>prodigalize the family fortune<br>挥霍家产</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He prodigalized his inheritance on cars and long holidays.  他把遗产挥霍在汽车和长假上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 squander</td><td>/ˈskwɑːndər/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>浪费</td><td>squander an opportunity<br>浪费机会</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Do not squander this opportunity to study abroad for a year.  不要浪费这次出国留学一年的机会。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 waste</td><td>/ˈweɪst/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词、名词、动词</td><td>荒芜的；浪费；废物</td><td>waste land<br>荒地</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Nothing grows on this waste ground beside the old factory.  旧工厂旁的这片荒地上什么也长不出来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>husk</b></td><td>/hʌsk/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>剥去</td><td>husk the dried corn<br>剥去干玉米壳</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The children helped husk the corn on the back porch all afternoon.  孩子们整个下午都在后廊帮着剥玉米壳。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bark</td><td>/ˈbɑːrk/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>树皮；剥去树皮</td><td>strip the bark off<br>剥去树皮</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Workers stripped the bark from the fallen pine trunk.  工人们把倒下的松树干上的树皮剥掉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 flay</td><td>/ˈfleɪ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>严厉批评；剥皮</td><td>flay the officials<br>严厉批评官员</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The editorial flayed the officials for ignoring repeated safety warnings.  社论严厉批评官员无视再三的安全警告。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hull</td><td>/ˈhʌl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>外壳；去壳</td><td>hull the strawberries<br>给草莓去萼</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She hulled the strawberries while the water came to a boil.  她在水烧开的时候给草莓去萼。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 shell</td><td>/ˈʃel/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>去壳，剥壳</td><td>shell the peas<br>剥豌豆</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We sat on the porch and shelled peas all afternoon.  我们整个下午坐在门廊上剥豌豆。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 shuck</td><td>/ˈʃʌk/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>剥去外皮，去壳</td><td>shuck the corn<br>剥玉米皮</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The cook shucked a dozen oysters in under two minutes.  厨师不到两分钟就开了一打牡蛎。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 skin</td><td>/ˈskɪn/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>剥皮，去皮</td><td>skin a rabbit<br>给兔子剥皮</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He learned to skin and clean a fish as a boy.  他小时候就学会了给鱼剥皮清理。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>husky</b></td><td>/ˈhʌski/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>（尤指声音）沙哑的，粗糙的</td><td>a husky voice<br>沙哑的声音</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 After the long concert his voice was husky and barely audible.  漫长的演唱会后，他的声音沙哑，几乎听不见。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 coarse</td><td>/kɔːrs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>粗糙的，表面不平整的；粗俗的</td><td>coarse language<br>粗俗的言语</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The workers used coarse language that shocked the visiting students.  工人们说的粗话让来访的学生们大吃一惊。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 grating</td><td>/ˈɡreɪtɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>刺耳的，令人不适的</td><td>a grating voice<br>刺耳的嗓音</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The grating noise of the saw filled the workshop.  锯子刺耳的声音充满了作坊。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 gravelly</td><td>/ˈɡrævəli/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>声音低沉粗糙的</td><td>a gravelly voice<br>粗哑的嗓音</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old singer's gravelly voice suited the sad song perfectly.  老歌手粗哑的嗓音与这首悲伤的歌极为契合。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 harsh</td><td>/hɑːrʃ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>严厉的</td><td>harsh criticism<br>严厉的批评</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The judge's harsh sentence surprised even the prosecutors in the courtroom.  法官严厉的判决连法庭上的检察官都感到意外。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rasping</td><td>/ˈræspɪŋ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>刺耳的，沙哑的</td><td>a rasping cough<br>沙哑的咳嗽</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A rasping whisper came from behind the heavy curtain.  厚窗帘后传来一声沙哑的低语。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rusty</td><td>/ˈrʌsti/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>声音生涩沙哑的</td><td>a rusty voice<br>生涩的嗓音</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 After years of silence his voice sounded rusty and thin.  多年不语后，他的声音听起来生涩而单薄。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 scratchy</td><td>/ˈskrætʃi/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>沙沙作响的，刺耳的</td><td>a scratchy recording<br>沙沙响的录音</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The scratchy old record still filled the room with warmth.  那张沙沙作响的老唱片仍让房间充满温暖。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 throaty</td><td>/ˈθroʊti/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>喉音重的，低哑的</td><td>a throaty laugh<br>低哑的笑声</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She answered with a throaty laugh that filled the hallway.  她以一阵低哑的笑声作答，笑声充满走廊。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 mellifluous</td><td>/meˈlɪfluəs/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>（声音）甜美流畅的</td><td>a mellifluous voice<br>甜美悦耳的嗓音</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The narrator's mellifluous voice made even the dullest chapters pleasant to listen to.  叙述者优美悦耳的嗓音让最枯燥的章节也变得动听。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>hybrid</b></td><td>/ˈhaɪbrɪd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>杂交品种，混合品种</td><td>a hardy hybrid<br>耐寒的杂交种</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 This rose is a hybrid bred for cold northern gardens.  这种玫瑰是为寒冷北方花园培育的杂交品种。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 amalgam</td><td>/əˈmælɡəm/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>混合物；汞合金</td><td>an amalgam of styles<br>多种风格的混合</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her music is an amalgam of jazz and traditional folk song.  她的音乐是爵士与传统民歌的混合。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bastard</td><td>/ˈbæstərd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>杂种，劣质混合物</td><td>a bastard form<br>混杂的变体</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Scholars call this script a bastard of two older alphabets.  学者称这种文字是两种更古老字母的混杂产物。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 compound</td><td>/kəmˈpaʊnd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>混合，合成；使恶化</td><td>compound a medicine<br>配制药剂</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The pharmacist compounded an ointment from three simple ingredients.  药剂师用三种简单原料配制出一种药膏。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mixture</td><td>/ˈmɪkstʃər/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>混合物，混合体</td><td>a mixture of styles<br>风格的混合</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The soup is a mixture of beans, corn and smoked meat.  这汤是豆子、玉米和熏肉的混合。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mule</td><td>/ˈmjuːl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>骡子；杂交动物</td><td>stubborn as a mule<br>像骡子般固执</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The farmer led a tired mule up the steep path.  农夫牵着一头疲惫的骡子走上陡坡。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 crossbred</td><td>/ˈkrɔsˌbrɛd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>杂交的，杂种的</td><td>a crossbred calf<br>杂交的小牛</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The crossbred sheep survive the mountain winters much better.  杂交的绵羊更能耐住山区的冬天。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mongrel</td><td>/ˈmʌŋɡrəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>杂种的；杂种狗</td><td>a cheerful mongrel dog<br>快乐的杂种狗</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A cheerful mongrel dog followed us along the beach for nearly a mile.  一只快乐的杂种狗沿着海滩跟了我们将近一英里。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 purebred</td><td>/ˈpjʊrbred/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>纯种的，纯血统的</td><td>a purebred horse<br>纯种的马</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her purebred collie won every prize at the county show.  她的纯种柯利犬在县展上赢得所有奖项。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>hymn</b></td><td>/hɪm/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>赞歌，赞美诗</td><td>sing a hymn<br>唱赞美诗</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The whole congregation stood up to sing a hymn of thanksgiving.  全体教众站起身来唱一首感恩的赞美诗。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 eulogy</td><td>/ˈjuːlədʒi/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>悼词；颂词，赞辞</td><td>deliver a moving eulogy<br>致感人的悼词</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She delivered a moving eulogy at her grandfather's funeral.  她在祖父的葬礼上致了感人的悼词。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 homage</td><td>/ˈhɑːmɪdʒ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>尊敬，敬意</td><td>pay homage to<br>向…致敬</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Thousands came to pay homage to the poet on her ninetieth birthday.  数千人在诗人九十岁生日那天前来致敬。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ode</td><td>/ˈoʊd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>颂歌，颂诗</td><td>an ode to autumn<br>一首秋之颂歌</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The poet wrote an ode celebrating the river of his childhood.  诗人写了一首颂诗，赞美童年的那条河。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 paean</td><td>/ˈpiːən/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>欢乐颂，赞扬</td><td>a paean to freedom<br>对自由的赞歌</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The review read like a paean to the young violinist and her talent.  那篇评论读来像是对这位年轻小提琴手及其才华的赞歌。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 panegyric</td><td>/ˌpænəˈdʒɪrɪk/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>赞颂之词，颂文</td><td>deliver a long panegyric<br>发表一篇长颂词</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The retirement dinner ended with a long panegyric to the old professor.  退休晚宴以对这位老教授的长篇颂词结束。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 psalm</td><td>/ˈsɑːm/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>圣歌，赞美诗</td><td>read a psalm aloud<br>朗读一首圣歌</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old man read a psalm before every evening meal.  老人每顿晚餐前都读一首圣歌。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 salutation</td><td>/ˌsæljuˈteɪʃn/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>致敬，问候</td><td>a salutation to the winners<br>向获胜者致敬</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The crowd offered a warm salutation to the returning champions.  人群向归来的冠军们热情致敬。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 tribute</td><td>/ˈtrɪbjuːt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>称颂，颂词</td><td>pay tribute to her work<br>对她的工作表示称颂</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Colleagues paid warm tribute to her forty years of patient teaching.  同事们对她四十年的耐心教学致以热情的颂扬。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bless</td><td>/ˈbles/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>赞美，祝福</td><td>bless the harvest<br>为收成祝福</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The priest blessed the fields at the start of spring.  神父在春天开始时为田地祝福。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 carol</td><td>/ˈkærəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>欢快颂唱，歌颂；颂歌</td><td>carol his praises<br>歌颂他的功绩</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The children caroled cheerfully outside every door on the street.  孩子们在街上每户门前欢快地颂唱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 celebrate</td><td>/ˈseləˌbreɪt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>赞颂，歌颂</td><td>celebrate her courage<br>赞颂她的勇气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The poem celebrates the quiet courage of ordinary farmers.  这首诗赞颂普通农人的沉静勇气。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 emblazon</del></td><td>/emˈbleɪzən/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>赞美，颂扬；用纹章装饰</td><td>emblazon his name<br>颂扬他的名字</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old chronicle emblazons the deeds of the founding kings.  古老的编年史颂扬开国君王的功业。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 exalt</td><td>/ɪɡˈzɔːlt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>赞扬；提升（地位）</td><td>exalt to the skies<br>推崇到极点</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics exalt her early novels as masterpieces of the era.  评论家将她早期的小说赞为那个时代的杰作。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 extol</td><td>/ɪkˈstoʊl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>赞美；吹捧</td><td>extol his virtues<br>赞美他的品德</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Neighbors still extol his kindness during the long winter flood.  邻居们至今仍赞美他在漫长冬季洪水期间的善举。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 glorify</td><td>/ˈɡlɔːrəˌfaɪ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>赞美，美化</td><td>glorify war and violence<br>美化战争与暴力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics said the film glorified war instead of questioning it.  批评者说这部电影美化战争而非质疑它。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 laud</td><td>/ˈlɔːd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>赞美，称赞</td><td>laud his courage<br>赞扬他的勇气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics lauded the young pianist for her fearless interpretation.  评论家赞扬这位年轻钢琴家大胆的演绎。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 magnify</td><td>/ˈmæɡnəˌfaɪ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>放大；夸大</td><td>magnify the problem<br>夸大问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The lens magnifies the specimen four hundred times.  这个镜头把样本放大四百倍。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 resound</td><td>/rɪˈzaʊnd/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>回响；被赞颂</td><td>resound with praise<br>充满赞颂之声</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The hall resounded with applause for the young violinist.  大厅回响着对这位年轻小提琴手的掌声。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 dirge</td><td>/ˈdɜːrdʒ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>哀歌，葬礼曲</td><td>a slow funeral dirge<br>缓慢的葬礼哀歌</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The band played a mournful dirge as the coffin passed.  棺木经过时，乐队奏起哀伤的挽歌。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 elegy</td><td>/ˈelədʒi/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>哀歌（诗），挽歌（诗）</td><td>write an elegy for him<br>为他写一首挽歌</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He wrote a short elegy for the friend who died in the flood.  他为在洪水中丧生的朋友写了一首短短的挽歌。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 6

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>hyperbole</b></td><td>/haɪˈpɜːrbəli/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>夸张</td><td>obvious hyperbole<br>明显的夸张</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His claim of a million loyal fans is obvious hyperbole.  他声称拥有百万忠实粉丝，这显然是夸张之辞。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 coloring</td><td>/ˈkʌlərɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>夸张渲染；着色</td><td>add some coloring<br>添些夸张渲染</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His account had enough coloring to make the fight sound heroic.  他的叙述加了足够渲染，把打斗说得很英勇。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 embellishment</td><td>/emˈbelɪʃmənt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>夸张修饰，渲染</td><td>harmless embellishment<br>无害的渲染</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He tells the story with a little embellishment each time.  他每次讲这故事都添一点渲染。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 embroidering</td><td>/emˈbrɔɪdərɪŋ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>添枝加叶，夸大</td><td>embroidering of the facts<br>对事实的夸大</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her embroidering of the details made the tale hard to believe.  她对细节的添枝加叶让故事变得难以相信。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 exaggeration</td><td>/ɪɡˌzædʒəˈreɪʃən/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>夸张，夸大</td><td>a slight exaggeration<br>略有夸张</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 To call the film a masterpiece is an exaggeration.  把这部电影称为杰作是一种夸张。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 magnification</td><td>/ˌmæɡnəfəˈkeɪʃən/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>夸大，放大</td><td>magnification of the danger<br>对危险的夸大</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The report's magnification of small risks alarmed the whole town.  报告对小风险的夸大惊动了全镇。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 overstatement</td><td>/ˈoʊvərˌsteɪtmənt/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>夸大陈述，言过其实</td><td>a slight overstatement<br>略微的夸大</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Calling it the best film ever is a clear overstatement.  说它是史上最佳影片显然言过其实。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 understatement</td><td>/ˈʌndərˌsteɪtmənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>保守的说法，轻描淡写</td><td>a masterpiece of understatement<br>轻描淡写的典范</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Saying the room was messy is a serious understatement.  说这房间乱，实在是太保守了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>hypnotic</b></td><td>/hɪpˈnɑːtɪk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>催眠的；迷人的；催眠药</td><td>a hypnotic rhythm<br>催眠般的节奏</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The drummer kept a hypnotic rhythm that held everyone completely still.  鼓手保持着一种催眠般的节奏，让所有人一动不动。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 drowsy</td><td>/ˈdraʊzi/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>昏昏欲睡的，困倦的</td><td>feel drowsy after lunch<br>午饭后犯困</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The warm room made everyone drowsy during the long lecture.  温暖的房间让所有人在长课上昏昏欲睡。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 narcotic</td><td>/nɑrˈkɑːtɪk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>催眠的；麻醉的</td><td>a narcotic effect<br>催眠的效果</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The steady rain had an almost narcotic effect on us.  连绵的雨对我们几乎有催眠的效果。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 opiate</td><td>/ˈoʊpiət/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>催眠药；麻醉剂</td><td>an opiate for the mind<br>心灵的麻醉剂</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He called cheap television an opiate for tired workers.  他把廉价电视称作疲惫工人的麻醉剂。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sleepy</td><td>/ˈsliːpi/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>困倦的；令人昏睡的</td><td>a sleepy little town<br>昏昏欲睡的小镇</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The children grew sleepy long before the movie ended.  电影还没结束孩子们就困了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 slumberous</del></td><td>/ˈslʌmbərəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>昏昏欲睡的，催眠的</td><td>a slumberous afternoon<br>令人困倦的午后</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The slumberous heat of noon emptied every street in the village.  正午令人昏睡的暑气让村里每条街都空了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 soporific</td><td>/ˌsɑːpəˈrɪfɪk/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>催眠的；安眠药</td><td>a mild soporific<br>温和的安眠药</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Alcohol acts as a mild soporific, but it ruins deep sleep.  酒精是一种温和的催眠物，但会破坏深度睡眠。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 stimulating</td><td>/ˈstɪmjəˌleɪtɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>激励的，令人振奋的</td><td>a stimulating discussion<br>一场激发思考的讨论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The seminar was the most stimulating class of the term.  这场研讨会是本学期最能激发思考的课。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>hypocritical</b></td><td>/ˌhɪpəˈkrɪtɪkl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>虚伪的</td><td>hypocritical behavior<br>虚伪的行为</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It is hypocritical to demand honesty while hiding your own mistakes.  一边要求诚实一边隐瞒自己的错误，这很虚伪。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 artificial</td><td>/ˌɑːrtəˈfɪʃəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>人造的；虚假的</td><td>artificial flowers<br>人造花</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The lobby was decorated with artificial flowers that never wilted.  大厅用永不枯萎的人造花装饰。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 backhanded</td><td>/ˌbækˈhændɪd/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>含讽刺的；间接的</td><td>a backhanded compliment<br>表面赞美实为讽刺</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He paid her a backhanded compliment about her surprising promotion.  他就她意外的升职说了句表面赞美实为讽刺的话。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 feigned</td><td>/ˈfeɪnd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>假装的，伪装的</td><td>feigned surprise<br>假装的惊讶</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He listened with feigned interest while checking his phone.  他一边看手机，一边假装感兴趣地听着。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mealy-mouthed</td><td>/ˈmiːli ˈmaʊðd/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>说话拐弯抹角的</td><td>a mealy-mouthed apology<br>含糊其词的道歉</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Voters distrust mealy-mouthed politicians who never give a straight answer.  选民不信任那些从不直接回答、说话含糊的政客。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 phony</td><td>/ˈfoʊni/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>假的，冒充的；骗子</td><td>a phony accent<br>假装的口音</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He used a phony name when renting the small apartment.  他租那间小公寓时用了假名。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pretended</td><td>/priˈtendɪd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>假装的，虚伪的</td><td>pretended concern<br>假装的关心</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her pretended surprise did not convince anyone in the room.  她假装的惊讶没能说服屋里任何人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unctuous</td><td>/ˈʌŋktʃuəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>油嘴滑舌的，谄媚的</td><td>an unctuous compliment<br>油腻的奉承</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The unctuous salesman praised every choice we made in his store.  那个油嘴滑舌的销售员对我们在他店里的每个选择都大加赞美。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 artless</td><td>/ˈɑrtləs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>缺乏技巧的，笨拙的</td><td>an artless imitation<br>拙劣的模仿</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The painting was an artless copy of a famous portrait.  这幅画是对一幅名作的拙劣临摹。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 candid</td><td>/ˈkændəd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>坦率的，直言的</td><td>a candid assessment<br>坦率的评价</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She gave a candid assessment of the team's weaknesses.  她对团队的弱点作了坦率的评价。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 genuine</td><td>/ˈdʒenjəwən/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>真正的，真实的；真诚的</td><td>genuine leather<br>真皮</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Experts confirmed that the signature on the letter was genuine.  专家确认信上的签名是真的。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 honest</td><td>/ˈɑːnəst/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>诚实的，正直的</td><td>an honest answer<br>一个诚实的回答</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Give me an honest answer, even if it disappoints me.  给我一个诚实的答复，即使会让我失望。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 sincere</td><td>/sɪnˈsɪr/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>真诚的，诚挚的</td><td>a sincere apology<br>真诚的道歉</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her sincere apology repaired a friendship damaged for years.  她真诚的道歉修复了受损多年的友谊。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 undesigning</del></td><td>/ˌʌndɪˈzaɪnɪŋ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无心机的，坦率的</td><td>an undesigning honest remark<br>毫无心机的诚实话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His undesigning honesty won friends wherever he happened to travel.  他毫无心机的诚实让他无论走到哪里都能交到朋友。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unfeigned</td><td>/ʌnˈfeɪnd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>真诚的，不做作的</td><td>unfeigned delight<br>真心的喜悦</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her unfeigned joy at the news warmed the whole room.  她听到消息时真心的喜悦温暖了整个屋子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>iconoclast</b></td><td>/aɪˈkɑːnəklæst/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>特立独行的人</td><td>a fearless iconoclast<br>无畏的反传统者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 As a lifelong iconoclast, she questioned every rule the institute cherished.  作为一生特立独行的人，她质疑该研究所珍视的每条规则。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bohemian</td><td>/boʊˈhiːmiən/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>特立独行之人</td><td>a bohemian lifestyle<br>放浪不羁的生活</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The young painter led a bohemian lifestyle in a crowded attic studio.  这位年轻画家在拥挤的阁楼画室里过着放浪不羁的生活。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 deviant</td><td>/ˈdiːviənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>离常规的人；异常的</td><td>labeled a deviant<br>被视为异类</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Society once treated any deviant thinker as a genuine danger.  社会曾把任何异类思想者视为真正的危险。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 heretic</td><td>/ˈherətɪk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>异端者，异教徒</td><td>burned as a heretic<br>作为异端被烧死</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 In his own time the scientist was treated as a heretic.  在他那个时代，这位科学家被当作异端。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 maverick</td><td>/ˈmævərɪk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>特立独行之人</td><td>a political maverick<br>政坛的独行者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 As a political maverick, she voted against her own party on almost every bill.  作为政坛的独行者，她几乎在每项法案上都投票反对本党。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 non-conformer</del></td><td>/ˌnɑn kənˈfɔrmər/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>不循规蹈矩的人</td><td>a proud non-conformer<br>自豪的不从众者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Every class has one non-conformer who questions the teacher openly.  每个班都有一个公开质疑老师的不从众者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 conformer</del></td><td>/kənˈfɔrmər/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>顺从者，随大流者</td><td>a quiet conformer<br>安静的顺从者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The office rewarded the conformer and punished the original thinker.  办公室奖励顺从者，惩罚有独立想法的人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 conformist</td><td>/kənˈfɔːrmɪst/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>墨守成规者，随大流者</td><td>a cautious conformist<br>谨慎的随大流者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 In that office every conformist was rewarded and every rebel ignored.  在那个办公室，随大流者受奖，反叛者被无视。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del><b>idolatrize</b></del></td><td>/aɪˈdɑːlətraɪz/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>（通常盲目）崇拜</td><td>idolatrize a pop star<br>盲目崇拜明星</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Teenagers often idolatrize singers they have never actually met.  青少年常盲目崇拜从未见过的歌手。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 adore</td><td>/əˈdɔːr/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>热爱，极喜爱；崇拜</td><td>absolutely adore someone<br>非常喜爱某人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The children adore their new puppy.  孩子们非常喜爱他们的新小狗。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 adulate</del></td><td>/ˈædʒəleɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>极度谄媚，拍马屁</td><td>adulate a celebrity<br>对名人极力吹捧</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Reporters adulated the young singer after her first hit.  记者们在这位年轻歌手第一首热门歌后对她大加吹捧。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 canonize</td><td>/ˈkænənaɪz/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使神圣，封为圣徒</td><td>canonize a saint<br>封为圣徒</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The church voted to canonize the nun a century after her death.  教会在这位修女去世百年后决定将她封为圣徒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 deify</td><td>/ˈdeɪɪfaɪ/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>神化，奉若神明</td><td>deify a leader<br>神化领袖</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The regime tried to deify its founder in every textbook.  该政权试图在每本教科书里神化其创始人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dote</td><td>/ˈdoʊt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>溺爱，宠爱</td><td>dote on her grandson<br>溺爱她的孙子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She dotes on her grandson and refuses him nothing.  她溺爱孙子，对他从不拒绝。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 worship</td><td>/ˈwɜːrʃəp/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>崇拜，敬奉；礼拜</td><td>worship ancient gods<br>敬奉古代神灵</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They gathered at dawn to worship in the small chapel.  他们黎明时聚在小教堂做礼拜。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 abhor</td><td>/əbˈhɔːr/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>极度厌恶，深恶痛绝</td><td>abhor violence<br>痛恨暴力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She abhors any form of cruelty to animals.  她极度厌恶任何形式的虐待动物行为。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 abominate</del></td><td>/əˈbɑːmɪneɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>痛恨，厌恶</td><td>abominate cruelty<br>憎恶残忍</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He abominated the hypocrisy he saw all around him.  他痛恨周围随处可见的虚伪。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 detest</td><td>/dɪˈtest/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>厌恶，憎恨</td><td>detest hypocrisy<br>厌恶虚伪</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 I detest waiting in long lines on hot days.  我讨厌在炎热的日子里排长队。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 dislike</td><td>/dɪsˈlaɪk/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>不喜欢，厌恶</td><td>dislike crowded places<br>讨厌拥挤的地方</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He dislikes crowded places and avoids the subway during rush hour.  他讨厌拥挤的地方，高峰时段避开地铁。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 hate</td><td>/ˈheɪt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>憎恨，厌恶</td><td>hate being lied to<br>讨厌被人骗</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She hates being lied to, even about very small things.  她讨厌被骗，哪怕是很小的事。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 loathe</td><td>/loʊð/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>厌恶，讨厌</td><td>loathe the sight of<br>一见就厌恶</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He loathes the smell of boiled cabbage.  他讨厌煮卷心菜的味道。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>idyll</b></td><td>/ˈaɪdl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>无忧无虑的生活</td><td>a rural idyll<br>田园式的悠闲</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their summer by the lake was a brief idyll they never forgot.  他们在湖边的那个夏天是一段难忘的无忧无虑的时光。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 frisk</td><td>/ˈfrɪsk/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>欢跃，嬉戏</td><td>frisk in the grass<br>在草地上嬉戏</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The lambs frisked in the meadow all through the warm morning.  羊羔们整个温暖的早晨都在草地上欢跃。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 frolic</td><td>/ˈfrɑːlɪk/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>嬉戏，欢闹</td><td>frolic in the snow<br>在雪中嬉戏</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The puppies frolicked in the garden all afternoon.  小狗们整个下午都在花园里嬉戏。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 gambol</td><td>/ˈɡæmbl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>欢跳，雀跃</td><td>gambol in the meadow<br>在草地上欢跳</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Young lambs gamboled in the meadow all through the warm afternoon.  小羊羔整个温暖的下午都在草地上欢跳。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 revel</td><td>/ˈrevl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>不及物动词、名词</td><td>享乐，陶醉</td><td>revel in praise<br>陶醉于赞美</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Young actors often revel in the applause of a packed opening night.  年轻演员常陶醉于首演满座时的掌声。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 lark</td><td>/ˈlɑːrk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、不及物动词</td><td>嬉戏，玩闹</td><td>just for a lark<br>只是为了玩闹</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They climbed the fence for a lark and got caught.  他们为了好玩翻过栅栏，结果被抓住了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>ignite</b></td><td>/ɪɡˈnaɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>点燃，使燃烧；激起（感情）</td><td>ignite public outrage<br>激起公众愤怒</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The verdict ignited public outrage across the entire country within a few hours.  判决在几小时内就激起了全国的公众愤怒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 arouse</td><td>/əˈraʊz/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>唤起，激起；唤醒</td><td>arouse public suspicion<br>引起公众怀疑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The missing files aroused suspicion among the auditors.  丢失的文件引起了审计人员的怀疑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 incite</td><td>/ɪnˈsaɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>煽动，激起</td><td>incite a riot<br>煽动暴乱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He was charged with inciting a riot outside the county courthouse.  他被指控在县法院外煽动暴乱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 instigate</td><td>/ˈɪnstɪɡeɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>煽动，激起</td><td>instigate a rebellion<br>煽动叛乱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Several officers were accused of helping to instigate the rebellion in the province.  几名军官被指控参与煽动该省的叛乱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pique</td><td>/piːk/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>激起（兴趣）；使生气</td><td>pique her curiosity<br>激起她的好奇心</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The strange title piqued her curiosity, so she borrowed the book.  奇怪的书名激起了她的好奇心，于是她借走了那本书。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 spark</td><td>/ˈspɑːrk/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>引发，激起</td><td>spark a heated debate<br>引发激烈争论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 One careless remark sparked a long argument at the table.  一句不慎的话在餐桌上引发了长久的争论。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 stimulate</td><td>/ˈstɪmjəˌleɪt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>刺激，激发；促进</td><td>stimulate economic growth<br>促进经济增长</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Warm rain stimulated the seeds to sprout within a week.  温暖的雨水刺激种子一周内发芽。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 stir</td><td>/ˈstɜːr/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>激起，煽动；搅拌</td><td>stir up anger<br>激起怒火</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The article stirred up anger among local residents.  这篇文章激起了当地居民的愤怒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 appease</td><td>/əˈpiːz/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>安抚，平息；满足</td><td>appease the angry crowd<br>安抚愤怒的人群</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company offered refunds to appease its angry customers.  公司提供退款以安抚愤怒的顾客。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 assuage</td><td>/əˈsweɪdʒ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>缓和，减轻；平息</td><td>assuage his fears<br>减轻他的恐惧</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The mayor's speech did little to assuage public anger.  市长的演讲几乎没能平息公众的怒火。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 calm</td><td>/ˈkɑːm/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词、动词、名词</td><td>平静的；使镇静</td><td>calm down quickly<br>很快平静下来</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She spoke in a calm voice until the children settled.  她用平静的声音说话，直到孩子们安静下来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 conciliate</td><td>/kənˈsɪlieɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>安抚，平息；调解</td><td>conciliate angry customers<br>安抚愤怒的顾客</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The manager tried to conciliate the angry customers with refunds.  经理试图用退款来安抚愤怒的顾客。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 mollify</td><td>/ˈmɑːlɪfaɪ/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>平息，抚慰，缓和</td><td>mollify an angry customer<br>安抚愤怒的顾客</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A sincere apology from the manager mollified the furious client.  经理诚挚的道歉安抚了这位愤怒的客户。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 pacify</td><td>/ˈpæsɪfaɪ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使平静，安慰</td><td>pacify a crying baby<br>安抚哭闹的婴儿</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The nurse sang softly to pacify the frightened child before the injection.  打针前，护士轻声唱歌安抚受惊的孩子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 placate</td><td>/ˈpleɪkeɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>（通过让步以）平息抚慰</td><td>placate the critics<br>平息批评者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company offered full refunds to placate its angriest customers.  公司提供全额退款以平息最愤怒的客户。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 propitiate</td><td>/prəˈpɪʃieɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>抚慰，劝解</td><td>propitiate the angry gods<br>抚慰愤怒的神灵</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Villagers offered rice and flowers to propitiate the mountain spirits.  村民献上米和花来抚慰山神。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 soothe</td><td>/suːð/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>安慰，抚慰；减轻</td><td>soothe a crying baby<br>安抚哭闹的婴儿</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Soft music helped soothe the restless patient through the long night.  轻柔的音乐帮助安抚了焦躁的病人度过漫长的夜晚。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>ignominy</b></td><td>/ˈɪɡnəmɪni/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>耻辱</td><td>the ignominy of defeat<br>失败的耻辱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He could not bear the ignominy of being dismissed so publicly.  他无法承受如此公开地被解职的耻辱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 discredit</td><td>/dɪsˈkredɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>使丧失名誉；怀疑</td><td>discredit a witness<br>质疑证人可信度</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The lawyer tried to discredit the witness with old records.  律师试图用旧档案质疑证人的可信度。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 disesteem</del></td><td>/ˌdɪsɪˈstiːm/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>轻视，不尊重</td><td>hold in disesteem<br>予以轻视</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics held the popular novelist in open disesteem.  批评家公开轻视这位通俗小说家。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dishonor</td><td>/dɪsˈɑːnər/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>使丢脸，玷污；不光彩</td><td>dishonor his memory<br>玷污他的名声</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Lying under oath would dishonor everything he had defended.  作伪证会玷污他一生所维护的一切。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 disrepute</td><td>/ˌdɪsrɪˈpjuːt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>声名扫地，坏名声</td><td>fall into disrepute<br>声名扫地</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The scandal brought the whole institution into disrepute.  这场丑闻让整个机构声名扫地。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 infamy</td><td>/ˈɪnfəmi/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>恶名，臭名；恶行</td><td>a day of infamy<br>耻辱的一天</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His name lives on in infamy after the scandal.  丑闻之后他的名字以恶名流传下来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 obloquy</del></td><td>/ˈɑːbləkwi/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>谩骂，诽谤</td><td>suffer public obloquy<br>遭受公众谩骂</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The former minister suffered public obloquy for years after the scandal broke.  丑闻爆发后，这位前部长多年遭受公众谩骂。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 odium</td><td>/ˈoʊdiəm/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>憎恶，讨厌</td><td>public odium<br>公众的憎恶</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Public odium followed the executives long after the company had been dissolved.  公司解散很久之后，公众的憎恶仍伴随着这些高管。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 opprobrium</td><td>/əˈproʊbriəm/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>公开谴责，耻辱</td><td>public opprobrium<br>公众的谴责</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The mayor faced weeks of opprobrium after the decision.  市长在那个决定之后面对数周的谴责。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 reproach</td><td>/rɪˈproʊtʃ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>批评，责备</td><td>reproach oneself for<br>因某事自责</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She reproached him for never returning her calls.  她责备他从不回她的电话。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 shame</td><td>/ˈʃeɪm/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>使羞愧，羞辱；羞耻</td><td>shame him into silence<br>让他羞愧得沉默</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The teacher never tried to shame students in front of the class.  这位老师从不在全班面前羞辱学生。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 glory</td><td>/ˈɡlɔːri/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>感到自豪，得意；荣耀</td><td>glory in her success<br>为她的成功而得意</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He gloried in the praise his research finally received.  他为研究终于获得的赞誉而洋洋自得。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 honor</td><td>/ˈɑːnər/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>尊敬，给以荣誉；荣誉</td><td>honor the veterans<br>表彰老兵</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The city honored the veterans with a quiet morning ceremony.  该市用一场安静的晨间仪式表彰老兵。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>illiteracy</b></td><td>/ɪˈlɪtərəsi/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>文盲</td><td>widespread adult illiteracy<br>普遍的成人文盲</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The government launched a campaign to reduce adult illiteracy in rural areas.  政府发起了一场运动，以减少农村地区的成人文盲。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ignorance</td><td>/ˈɪɡnərəns/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>无知，愚昧</td><td>out of sheer ignorance<br>纯属无知</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His ignorance of basic history embarrassed him during the interview.  他对基本历史的无知让他在面试中很难堪。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 learning</td><td>/ˈlɜːrnɪŋ/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>学识，学问</td><td>a man of learning<br>有学问的人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old librarian was a woman of deep learning.  那位老图书馆员是个学识深厚的女性。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 literacy</td><td>/ˈlɪtərəsi/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>读写能力，识字</td><td>adult literacy programs<br>成人识字项目</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The village raised literacy sharply within a single decade.  这个村子在十年内大幅提高了识字率。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>illuminati</b></td><td>/ɪˌluːmɪˈnɑːti/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>智者</td><td>the illuminati of the age<br>当代的智者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The salon gathered the illuminati of eighteenth-century Paris.  这个沙龙聚集了十八世纪巴黎的智者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 clerisy</del></td><td>/ˈklɛrəsi/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>知识阶层，文人群体</td><td>the local clerisy<br>当地的知识阶层</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The city's clerisy gathered every Sunday in one small bookshop.  城里的知识阶层每周日聚在一家小书店。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 intellectual</td><td>/ˌɪntəˈlektʃuəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>知识分子；智力的</td><td>a public intellectual<br>公共知识分子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She became a leading intellectual in the reform movement.  她成为改革运动中的主要知识分子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 intelligentsia</td><td>/ˌɪnteləˈdʒentsiə/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>知识分子阶层</td><td>the urban intelligentsia<br>城市知识阶层</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The reform was debated hotly among the country's intelligentsia.  这项改革在该国知识分子中引发热议。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
