<tr><td>↳近 literati</td><td>/ˌlɪtəˈrɑːti/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>文人，文学界人士</td><td>the London literati<br>伦敦文人圈</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The novel was praised by the literati but ignored by readers.  这部小说受文人推崇，却被读者忽视。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 fool</td><td>/ˈfuːl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>傻瓜，蠢人；愚弄</td><td>make a fool of oneself<br>让自己出丑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He felt like a fool after believing the obvious lie.  信了那个明显的谎言后，他觉得自己像个傻瓜。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 dolt</td><td>/doʊlt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>笨蛋</td><td>call him a dolt<br>骂他是笨蛋</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Do not call him a dolt simply because he asked an obvious question.  别只因为他问了个显而易见的问题就骂他笨蛋。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 dullard</td><td>/ˈdʌlərd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>笨蛋</td><td>a hopeless dullard<br>无可救药的笨蛋</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The teacher wrongly treated the quiet boy as a hopeless dullard.  老师错把这个安静的男孩当成无可救药的笨蛋。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 idiot</td><td>/ˈɪdiət/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>白痴，蠢人</td><td>act like an idiot<br>表现得像蠢人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Only an idiot would drive that fast in fog.  只有蠢人才会在雾中开那么快。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 simpleton</td><td>/ˈsɪmpltən/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>（缺乏常识的）笨蛋</td><td>treat him as a simpleton<br>把他当傻子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They treated the new clerk as a simpleton until he corrected their figures.  他们把新来的职员当傻子，直到他改正了他们的数字。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 7

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>illuminate</b></td><td>/ɪˈluːmɪneɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>阐明</td><td>illuminate the problem<br>阐明问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her lecture illuminated a problem that had puzzled economists for years.  她的讲座阐明了一个困扰经济学家多年的问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 clarify</td><td>/ˈklerɪfaɪ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>阐明，澄清</td><td>clarify the rules<br>澄清规则</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The dean clarified the rules after weeks of confused complaints.  在数周混乱的投诉后，院长澄清了规则。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 clear</td><td>/ˈklɪr/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>明确的，确凿的；清楚的</td><td>a clear majority<br>明显的多数</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The party won by a clear majority of votes.  该党以明显多数票获胜。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 construe</td><td>/kənˈstruː/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>解释，理解为</td><td>construe the remark as praise<br>把那句话理解为赞扬</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Do not construe my silence as agreement with the plan.  别把我的沉默理解为赞同这个计划。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 demonstrate</td><td>/ˈdemənstreɪt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>（通过证据）证明，表明</td><td>demonstrate the theory clearly<br>清楚地证明该理论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The experiment demonstrated the theory clearly to every skeptical observer.  实验向每位持疑的观察者清楚证明了该理论。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 demystify</td><td>/diˈmɪstəˌfaɪ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使浅显易懂，阐明</td><td>demystify the process<br>阐明这一过程</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The book demystifies statistics for readers who fear numbers.  这本书为怕数字的读者把统计学讲得浅显易懂。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 elucidate</td><td>/iˈluːsɪdeɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>阐明</td><td>elucidate the problem<br>阐明问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The footnotes elucidate the problem far better than the chapter itself does.  脚注比正文本身更好地阐明了这个问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 explicate</td><td>/ˈeksplɪkeɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>解释，说明</td><td>explicate a difficult text<br>解说一段难懂的文本</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The teacher patiently explicated the most difficult passage in the poem.  老师耐心地解说了这首诗中最难的一段。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 expound</td><td>/ɪkˈspaʊnd/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>动词</td><td>详细阐述，解释</td><td>expound on his theory<br>详细阐述他的理论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The professor expounded her views on urban design for an hour.  教授用一个小时详细阐述了她的城市设计观点。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 illustrate</td><td>/ˈɪləsˌtreɪt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>说明，举例说明</td><td>illustrate the point<br>说明这一点</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 These figures illustrate how quickly the population has grown.  这些数字说明人口增长得多么迅速。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 interpret</td><td>/ɪnˈtɜːrprɪt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>解释，阐明</td><td>interpret the results<br>解释结果</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A good guide can interpret each painting in one minute.  好的导览员能在一分钟内解说每幅画。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 obscure</td><td>/əbˈskjʊr/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>含义模糊的；平凡的，不知名的</td><td>an obscure poet<br>不知名的诗人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He remained an obscure poet until a critic rediscovered his work decades later.  他一直是个不知名的诗人，直到几十年后一位批评家重新发现他的作品。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>illusory</b></td><td>/ɪˈluːsəri/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>幻觉的，虚幻的</td><td>an illusory advantage<br>虚幻的优势</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The advantage proved illusory once the competitors lowered their own prices.  竞争者一降价，这一优势就被证明是虚幻的。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 chimerical</td><td>/kɪˈmerəkəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>空想的，荒诞不经的</td><td>a chimerical plan<br>空想的计划</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their plan to colonize Mars by 2030 seems chimerical.  他们2030年殖民火星的计划显得荒诞不经。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fanciful</td><td>/ˈfænsɪfl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不切实际的，荒谬的</td><td>a fanciful explanation<br>荒谬的解释</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her fanciful account of the accident convinced no one at all.  她对事故荒谬不切实际的描述完全没能说服任何人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fantastic</td><td>/fænˈtæstɪk/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>荒诞的，异想天开的</td><td>a fantastic tale<br>荒诞的故事</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His fantastic story about talking wolves amused the children.  他关于会说话的狼的荒诞故事逗乐了孩子们。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fictional</td><td>/ˈfɪkʃənəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>虚构的，小说中的</td><td>a fictional character<br>虚构的人物</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Sherlock Holmes is a fictional character loved by millions.  夏洛克·福尔摩斯是深受数百万人喜爱的虚构人物。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fictitious</td><td>/fɪkˈtɪʃəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>虚构的</td><td>a fictitious name<br>虚构的名字</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He checked into the hotel under a fictitious name.  他用一个虚构的名字入住了旅馆。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 imaginary</td><td>/ɪˈmædʒəneri/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>想象的，虚构的</td><td>an imaginary friend<br>想象出的朋友</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The boy blamed every mess on an imaginary friend.  男孩把每次弄乱都推给一个想象出的朋友。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 suppositious</del></td><td>/ˌsʌpəˈzɪʃəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>假设的，虚构的</td><td>a suppositious case<br>假设的情形</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The lawyer built her argument on a purely suppositious example.  律师用一个纯属假设的例子构建论点。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unreal</td><td>/ənˈriːl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>虚幻的，不真实的</td><td>an unreal silence<br>不真实的寂静</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The empty city felt unreal in the early morning light.  清晨的光线里，空城显得不真实。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 factual</td><td>/ˈfæktʃuəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>真实的，基于事实的</td><td>a factual account<br>真实的叙述</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The report gives a factual account of the whole accident.  这份报告对整起事故做了真实的叙述。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>imbibe</b></td><td>/ɪmˈbaɪb/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>动词</td><td>喝水，摄取水分</td><td>imbibe cool water<br>喝下凉水</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The hikers imbibed cool spring water before climbing the last ridge.  徒步者在攀登最后一道山脊前喝了清凉的泉水。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 drink</td><td>/ˈdrɪŋk/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>喝，饮用</td><td>drink plenty of water<br>多喝水</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 You should drink more water during this hot dry week.  这个干热的一周里你该多喝水。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 guzzle</td><td>/ˈɡʌzl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>动词</td><td>狂饮</td><td>guzzle a can of beer<br>狂饮一罐啤酒</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He guzzled two bottles of soda before the game even started.  比赛还没开始他就狂饮了两瓶汽水。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hoist</td><td>/ˈhɔɪst/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>举杯饮酒；吊起</td><td>hoist a few drinks<br>喝上几杯</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The sailors hoisted a glass together after the long voyage.  水手们在漫长航行后一起举杯。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 quaff</td><td>/kwæf/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>痛饮；大口喝</td><td>quaff a mug of ale<br>大口喝下一杯麦酒</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The thirsty travelers quaffed mugs of ale at the roadside inn.  口渴的旅人在路边客栈痛饮大杯麦酒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sip</td><td>/ˈsɪp/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>小口喝，抿</td><td>sip hot tea slowly<br>慢慢抿热茶</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She sipped her tea and watched the rain through the window.  她抿着茶，透过窗子看雨。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sponge</td><td>/ˈspʌndʒ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>吸收水分</td><td>sponge up the spill<br>吸掉溢出的水</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Dry moss sponges up water and holds it for weeks.  干苔藓吸收水分并能保存数周。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 urinate</td><td>/ˈjɜːrəˌneɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>排尿，小便</td><td>urinate after drinking water<br>喝水后排尿</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Patients are asked to urinate before the scan begins.  病人被要求在扫描开始前排尿。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>imbroglio</b></td><td>/ɪmˈbroʊlioʊ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>困境，复杂的局面</td><td>a political imbroglio<br>政治上的乱局</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The minister resigned in the middle of a diplomatic imbroglio.  部长在一场外交乱局之中辞职了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 complexity</td><td>/kəmˈpleksəti/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>复杂，复杂性</td><td>the complexity of the case<br>案件的复杂性</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Nobody expected the complexity of the new tax rules.  谁都没料到新税规如此复杂。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 complication</td><td>/ˌkɑːmpləˈkeɪʃən/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>复杂情况，麻烦</td><td>an unexpected complication<br>意外的麻烦</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A small complication delayed the whole project by two months.  一个小麻烦把整个项目推迟了两个月。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 embarrassment</td><td>/ɪmˈberəsmənt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>窘境，尴尬</td><td>a source of embarrassment<br>尴尬的来源</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The missing file became a real embarrassment for the department.  丢失的文件成了部门真正的尴尬。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 entanglement</td><td>/enˈtæŋɡəlmənt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>纠缠，牵连</td><td>a legal entanglement<br>法律上的纠缠</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He spent years escaping a costly legal entanglement.  他花了数年才摆脱一场昂贵的法律纠缠。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 involvement</td><td>/ɪnˈvɑːlvmənt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>牵连，卷入</td><td>deep involvement in the scandal<br>深度卷入丑闻</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His involvement in the dispute cost him several old friends.  他卷入这场争端，失去了几个老朋友。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 misunderstanding</td><td>/ˌmɪsəndərˈstændɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>误解，误会</td><td>a simple misunderstanding<br>一场简单误会</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The whole quarrel grew from one small misunderstanding.  整场争吵源于一个小小的误会。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 quandary</td><td>/ˈkwɑːndəri/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>困惑，窘境</td><td>in a real quandary<br>陷入真正的困境</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The offer of two jobs left him in a real quandary.  两份工作的邀约让他陷入真正的困境。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>imitation</b></td><td>/ˌɪmɪˈteɪʃn/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>仿制品</td><td>a cheap imitation<br>廉价的仿制品</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The bag was a cheap imitation sold openly at the night market.  那只包是夜市上公开出售的廉价仿制品。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 clone</td><td>/ˈkloʊn/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>复制品，翻版</td><td>a cheap clone<br>廉价的翻版</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new phone is basically a clone of last year's model.  这款新手机基本上是去年型号的翻版。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 copy</td><td>/ˈkɑːpi/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>复制品，仿制品；抄写</td><td>a cheap copy<br>廉价仿制品</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The painting on the wall is only a cheap copy.  墙上那幅画只是廉价的仿制品。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dupe</td><td>/duːp/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>欺骗</td><td>dupe trusting investors<br>欺骗轻信的投资者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The scheme duped trusting investors out of nearly a million dollars.  这个骗局欺骗轻信的投资者，骗走近百万美元。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 duplication</td><td>/ˌduːpləˈkeɪʃən/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>复制品，重复</td><td>an exact duplication<br>精确的复制</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The painting is a faithful duplication of a lost original.  这幅画是失传原作的忠实复制。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 facsimile</td><td>/fækˈsɪməli/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>复制品，摹本</td><td>a perfect facsimile<br>完美的摹本</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The museum shows a facsimile while the original stays hidden.  博物馆展出摹本，原件则藏而不露。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mock</td><td>/ˈmɑːk/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词、动词</td><td>假的，模拟的；嘲笑</td><td>a mock trial<br>模拟审判</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Students held a mock election to learn how voting works.  学生举行模拟选举以了解投票如何运作。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 reduplication</td><td>/riˌdupləˈkeɪʃən/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>复制，重复</td><td>endless reduplication of forms<br>表格的无尽重复</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The office suffers from constant reduplication of the same paperwork.  这个办公室饱受同样文书不断重复之苦。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 replica</td><td>/ˈreplɪkə/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>复制品，仿制品</td><td>a full-size replica<br>原尺寸的复制品</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Visitors climb a wooden replica of the ancient tower.  游客可以爬上那座古塔的木制复制品。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 replication</td><td>/ˌrepləˈkeɪʃən/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>复制品；复制</td><td>a faithful replication<br>忠实的复制品</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The museum displays a careful replication of the ancient bronze vase.  博物馆展出了那只古代青铜花瓶的精细复制品。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 reproduction</td><td>/ˌriːprəˈdʌkʃən/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>复制品；复制</td><td>a cheap reproduction<br>廉价的复制品</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He hung a reproduction of the famous painting above his desk.  他在书桌上方挂了那幅名画的复制品。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 archetype</td><td>/ˈɑːrkɪtaɪp/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>原型，典型</td><td>the archetype of a hero<br>英雄的典型</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Sherlock Holmes remains the archetype of the brilliant private detective.  夏洛克·福尔摩斯仍是天才私家侦探的原型。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 original</td><td>/əˈrɪdʒənl/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>创新的；最初的，最早的</td><td>an original idea<br>有创意的想法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her proposal was genuinely original, unlike the tired ideas we usually hear.  她的提案确实新颖，不像我们常听到的那些陈旧想法。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 prototype</td><td>/ˈproʊtətaɪp/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>原型</td><td>a working prototype<br>可运行的原型</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Engineers tested a working prototype of the electric bus last spring.  工程师去年春天测试了这款电动公交车的可用原型。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>immaculate</b></td><td>/ɪˈmækjələt/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>洁净无瑕的；完美无缺的</td><td>an immaculate record<br>毫无瑕疵的记录</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 After thirty years of service his record remained absolutely immaculate.  服务三十年后，他的记录依然毫无瑕疵。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 absolute</td><td>/ˈæbsəluːt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>专制的；无限的；纯净不掺杂的；确凿的</td><td>absolute power<br>绝对的权力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The king held absolute power over his subjects.  国王对臣民拥有绝对权力。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 faultless</td><td>/ˈfɔːltləs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无瑕的，完美的</td><td>a faultless performance<br>无懈可击的表演</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her faultless pronunciation impressed everyone in the room.  她无瑕的发音让房间里的每个人都印象深刻。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 flawless</td><td>/ˈflɔːləs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无瑕的，完美无缺的</td><td>a flawless diamond<br>毫无瑕疵的钻石</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her flawless pronunciation surprised everyone in the language class.  她毫无瑕疵的发音令语言课上所有人惊讶。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 impeccable</td><td>/ɪmˈpekəbl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无瑕的，无可挑剔的</td><td>impeccable manners<br>无可挑剔的举止</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her impeccable manners impressed even the most demanding guests.  她无可挑剔的举止让最挑剔的客人都印象深刻。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 indefectible</del></td><td>/ˌɪndɪˈfektəbl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无瑕的；不会衰败的</td><td>indefectible virtue<br>完美无缺的美德</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old monk was praised for his indefectible devotion to the temple.  这位老僧因对寺院无瑕的虔诚而受到赞誉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 irreproachable</td><td>/ˌɪrɪˈproʊtʃəbl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无可指责的，无瑕的</td><td>irreproachable conduct<br>无可指责的行为</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His record as a judge was irreproachable for thirty long years.  他担任法官三十年的记录无可指责。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 perfect</td><td>/ˈpɜːrfɪkt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词、及物动词</td><td>完美的；使完善</td><td>a perfect score<br>满分成绩</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He spent years trying to perfect his technique on the cello.  他花了数年时间钻研以完善自己的大提琴技巧。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 seamless</td><td>/ˈsiːmləs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无缝的；无瑕的</td><td>a seamless transition<br>无缝的过渡</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The two departments managed a seamless merger within three months.  两个部门在三个月内完成了无缝合并。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unblemished</td><td>/ənˈblemɪʃt/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无瑕的，无污点的</td><td>an unblemished record<br>无污点的记录</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She left the company with an unblemished professional reputation.  她带着毫无瑕疵的职业声誉离开了公司。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 amiss</td><td>/əˈmɪs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词、副词</td><td>出错的，不对劲的</td><td>something is amiss<br>有些不对劲</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She sensed at once that something was seriously amiss at home.  她立刻感到家里出了严重问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 defective</td><td>/dɪˈfektɪv/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有缺陷的，有瑕疵的</td><td>a defective product<br>有瑕疵的产品</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company recalled thousands of defective batteries after the accident.  事故后公司召回了数千个有缺陷的电池。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 faulty</td><td>/ˈfɔːlti/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有缺陷的，有错误的</td><td>faulty wiring<br>有故障的线路</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The fire was traced to a faulty electrical connection in the basement.  火灾被查明源于地下室一处有缺陷的电线接头。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 flawed</td><td>/ˈflɔːd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有瑕疵的，有缺陷的</td><td>a deeply flawed plan<br>严重有缺陷的计划</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics argued that the study was flawed from the very beginning.  批评者认为这项研究从一开始就有缺陷。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 imperfect</td><td>/ɪmˈpɜːrfɪkt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不完美的，有缺陷的</td><td>an imperfect copy<br>不完美的副本</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Even an imperfect solution is better than doing nothing at all.  即便是不完美的方案也胜过什么都不做。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>immanent</b></td><td>/ˈɪmənənt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>内在的</td><td>immanent in nature<br>内在于自然之中</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He argued that order is immanent in nature rather than imposed on it.  他认为秩序内在于自然，而非外加于自然。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 constitutional</td><td>/ˌkɑːnstəˈtuːʃənəl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>体质上的，内在固有的</td><td>a constitutional weakness<br>体质上的虚弱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His shyness seems constitutional rather than a habit he learned.  他的害羞似乎是天性使然，而非后天习得的习惯。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 essential</td><td>/ɪˈsenʃəl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>本质的，必不可少的</td><td>an essential quality<br>本质的特性</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Patience is an essential part of her character.  耐心是她性格中本质的一部分。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 inborn</td><td>/ˈɪnˌbɔːrn/</td><td>★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>天生的，先天的</td><td>an inborn talent<br>天生的才能</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She seems to have an inborn sense of rhythm.  她似乎天生就有节奏感。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 inbred</td><td>/ˈɪnˌbred/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>天生的，根深蒂固的</td><td>an inbred sense of duty<br>天生的责任感</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He has an inbred distrust of large institutions.  他对大型机构有一种根深蒂固的不信任。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 indigenous</td><td>/ɪnˈdɪdʒənəs/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>土产的，本地的；与生俱来的，先天的</td><td>indigenous plants of the region<br>该地区的本土植物</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Many indigenous species of the island cannot survive anywhere else on the planet.  这个岛上许多本土物种无法在地球其他任何地方存活。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ingrained</td><td>/ɪnˈɡreɪnd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>本质的，根深蒂固的</td><td>deeply ingrained habits<br>根深蒂固的习惯</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Habits ingrained since childhood are extremely difficult to change in later life.  童年就已根深蒂固的习惯到了晚年极难改变。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 innate</td><td>/ɪˈneɪt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>天生的，固有的</td><td>an innate ability<br>天生的能力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Children have an innate curiosity about the world.  儿童对世界有一种天生的好奇心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 integral</td><td>/ˈɪntɪɡrəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不可缺少的；完整的</td><td>an integral part<br>不可缺少的部分</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Careful revision is an integral part of writing any serious research paper.  仔细修改是撰写任何严肃研究论文不可缺少的部分。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 intrinsic</td><td>/ɪnˈtrɪnsɪk/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>固有的，内在的</td><td>intrinsic value<br>内在价值</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The painting has an intrinsic beauty independent of its price.  这幅画有着与价格无关的内在美。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 natural</td><td>/ˈnætʃərəl/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>自然的，正常的</td><td>a natural reaction<br>自然的反应</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It is natural to feel nervous before a big exam.  大考前紧张是很自然的。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 adventitious</td><td>/ˌædvenˈtɪʃəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>外来的，后天的，非内在的</td><td>an adventitious circumstance<br>偶然的外部情况</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her success was not adventitious but the result of years of work.  她的成功不是偶然外来的，而是多年努力的结果。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 extrinsic</td><td>/eksˈtrɪnsɪk/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>外在的，非固有的</td><td>extrinsic motivation<br>外在的动机</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Money is an extrinsic reward that rarely sustains real interest.  金钱是一种外在奖励，很少能维持真正的兴趣。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 extraneous</td><td>/ɪkˈstreɪniəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无关的，不重要的</td><td>extraneous information<br>无关的信息</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Please delete all extraneous details from the summary.  请把摘要中所有无关的细节删掉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>immaterial</b></td><td>/ˌɪməˈtɪriəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无关的，不重要的</td><td>immaterial to the case<br>与本案无关</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Whether he liked the plan is immaterial to the legal question here.  他是否喜欢这个计划与这里的法律问题无关。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 extraneous</td><td>/ɪkˈstreɪniəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无关的，不重要的</td><td>extraneous information<br>无关的信息</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Please delete all extraneous details from the summary.  请把摘要中所有无关的细节删掉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 impertinent</td><td>/ɪmˈpɜːrtnənt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不相关的；无礼的，鲁莽的</td><td>an impertinent remark<br>无礼的言论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The lawyer objected that the question was impertinent to the charges.  律师反对说，这个问题与指控无关。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 inapplicable</td><td>/ˌɪnəˈplɪkəbəl/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不适用的，不相关的</td><td>inapplicable to this case<br>不适用于本案</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old rule is inapplicable to digital contracts.  旧规则不适用于电子合同。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 inapposite</del></td><td>/ɪnˈæpəzɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不恰当的，不相关的</td><td>an inapposite comparison<br>不恰当的比较</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The comparison to wartime rationing seems inapposite today.  把它比作战时配给在今天显得不恰当。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 irrelevant</td><td>/ˌɪˈreləvənt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无关的，不相干的</td><td>an irrelevant detail<br>无关的细节</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The lawyer objected because the question was completely irrelevant here.  律师提出反对，因为这个问题在此完全无关。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 applicable</td><td>/ˈæplɪkəbl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>可用的，可行的；相关的，有关的</td><td>applicable to all students<br>适用于所有学生</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 This rule is applicable to all students regardless of their year.  这条规定适用于所有学生，不论他们是哪个年级。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 apposite</td><td>/ˈæpəzɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>相关的，合适的</td><td>an apposite comment<br>恰当的评论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She made one apposite comment that settled the whole argument.  她一句恰当的评论就平息了整场争论。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 apropos</td><td>/ˌæprəˈpoʊ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词、介词</td><td>相关的；关于，有关</td><td>apropos of nothing<br>毫无来由地</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Apropos of nothing, he asked whether I had ever been to Rome.  他毫无来由地问我是否去过罗马。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 germane</td><td>/dʒɜːrˈmeɪn/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有关的，适当的</td><td>germane to the discussion<br>与讨论相关</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her question was germane to the discussion and changed our plan.  她的问题与讨论密切相关，改变了我们的计划。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 pertinent</td><td>/ˈpɜːrtnənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>相关的，恰当的</td><td>pertinent to the discussion<br>与讨论相关的</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Only evidence pertinent to the discussion should be presented today.  今天只应提出与讨论相关的证据。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 relevant</td><td>/ˈreləvənt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有关系的，重要的</td><td>relevant to the case<br>与本案有关</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The judge ruled that the old letters were relevant to the case.  法官裁定那些旧信件与本案有关。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>immature</b></td><td>/ˌɪməˈtʃʊr/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>（生理、心理）未完全发展的，未发育成熟的</td><td>emotionally immature<br>情绪上不成熟</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The fruit is still immature and much too sour to pick.  这些果子还未成熟，太酸，不能采摘。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 adolescent</td><td>/ˌædəˈlesənt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>青春期的；不成熟的</td><td>adolescent behavior<br>幼稚的举动</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His adolescent jokes annoyed the older members of the committee.  他幼稚的玩笑让委员会里年长的成员很不快。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 juvenile</td><td>/ˈdʒuːvənəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>少年的；幼稚的；青少年</td><td>juvenile behavior<br>幼稚的行为</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His juvenile jokes embarrassed everyone at the formal dinner.  他幼稚的玩笑让正式晚宴上的人都很尴尬。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 youngish</td><td>/ˈjʌŋɪʃ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>略年轻的</td><td>a youngish man<br>一个略显年轻的男子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A youngish woman in a grey coat asked us for directions.  一个穿灰外套的略显年轻的女子向我们问路。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 youthful</td><td>/ˈjuːθfəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>年轻的，有活力的</td><td>youthful enthusiasm<br>年轻人的热情</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 At sixty he still had a remarkably youthful energy.  六十岁时他依然有着惊人的年轻活力。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>immemorial</b></td><td>/ˌɪməˈmɔːriəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>远古的，久远的</td><td>from time immemorial<br>自远古以来</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Farmers here have shared the same well from time immemorial.  自远古以来，这里的农民一直共用同一口井。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 aged</td><td>/ˈeɪdʒɪd/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>年老的；陈年的</td><td>an aged farmer<br>一位年老的农民</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The aged oak still shades the whole courtyard in summer.  这棵老橡树夏天仍能荫蔽整个庭院。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 age-old</td><td>/ˌeɪdʒ ˈoʊld/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>古老的，年代久远的</td><td>an age-old tradition<br>一个古老的传统</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Making paper by hand is an age-old craft here.  手工造纸在这里是一门古老的技艺。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 antediluvian</td><td>/ˌæntidɪˈluːviən/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>非常古老的；过时的</td><td>antediluvian attitudes<br>过时的观念</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His antediluvian views on marriage shocked the younger guests.  他对婚姻的陈腐观念让年轻的客人们大为震惊。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 antique</td><td>/ænˈtiːk/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>古老的，古董的；古董</td><td>an antique wooden chair<br>一把古董木椅</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She collects antique clocks that were made in nineteenth-century France.  她收集在十九世纪法国制造的古董钟。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dateless</td><td>/ˈdeɪtləs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>古老得无从考证的</td><td>dateless folk songs<br>年代不明的民歌</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 These dateless legends were told long before anyone wrote them down.  这些年代不明的传说早在有人记录之前就流传着。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hoary</td><td>/ˈhɔːri/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>极老的</td><td>a hoary old joke<br>老掉牙的笑话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He told the same hoary anecdote at every family dinner.  他每次家庭聚餐都讲同一个老掉牙的轶事。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 old</td><td>/ˈoʊld/</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>古老的；年老的</td><td>an old tradition<br>古老的传统</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The village follows customs that are older than the kingdom itself.  这个村庄遵循着比王国本身更古老的习俗。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 venerable</td><td>/ˈvenərəbəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>古老而受尊敬的</td><td>a venerable institution<br>历史悠久的机构</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The venerable library has served scholars for over four centuries.  这座古老而受人尊敬的图书馆已为学者服务了四百多年。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 contemporary</td><td>/kənˈtempəˌreri/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>当代的；同时代的</td><td>contemporary art<br>当代艺术</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The gallery mixes ancient sculpture with contemporary photography.  这家画廊将古代雕塑与当代摄影混合展出。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 modern</td><td>/ˈmɑːdərn/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>现代的，新式的</td><td>modern architecture<br>现代建筑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Modern phones can do far more than early computers could.  现代手机的功能远超早期的计算机。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 8

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>immune</b></td><td>/ɪˈmjuːn/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不易被感染的，有免疫力的；不受影响的</td><td>immune to criticism<br>不受批评影响</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 No public figure is entirely immune to criticism these days.  如今没有哪个公众人物能完全不受批评的影响。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 resistant</td><td>/rɪˈzɪstənt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>抵抗的，抗拒的</td><td>resistant to change<br>抗拒变革的</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Older workers proved resistant to the new scheduling software.  年长员工对新排班软件表现出抗拒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 insusceptible</del></td><td>/ˌɪnsəˈseptəbl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不易受影响的</td><td>insusceptible to flattery<br>不为奉承所动</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old general seemed insusceptible to fear of any kind.  这位老将军似乎不受任何恐惧的影响。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unaffected</td><td>/ˌʌnəˈfektɪd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不受影响的</td><td>unaffected by the crisis<br>未受危机影响</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Northern farms remained largely unaffected by the drought.  北方的农场基本未受这场旱灾影响。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unresponsive</td><td>/ˌʌnrɪˈspɑːnsɪv/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>冷淡的，无反应的</td><td>an unresponsive audience<br>毫无反应的观众</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The comedian struggled before a cold, unresponsive audience.  这位喜剧演员面对冷淡无反应的观众很吃力。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 susceptible</td><td>/səˈseptəbl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>易受影响的；易感染的</td><td>susceptible to infection<br>易受感染</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Young children are especially susceptible to infection during the cold months.  幼儿在寒冷的月份里尤其容易受到感染。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 vulnerable</td><td>/ˈvʌlnərəbl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>易受攻击的，脆弱的</td><td>vulnerable to attack<br>易受攻击</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Wooden houses on the ridge are especially vulnerable to summer fires.  山脊上的木屋在夏季尤其容易受火灾侵袭。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 ductile</td><td>/ˈdʌktaɪl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有延展性的；易受影响的</td><td>ductile metal<br>有延展性的金属</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Young recruits are ductile and quickly absorb the habits of the older soldiers.  新兵易受影响，很快就学到老兵的习惯。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 pliable</td><td>/ˈplaɪəbl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>易弯曲的，柔软的；易受影响的，温顺的</td><td>pliable young minds<br>易受影响的年轻心智</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Young minds are pliable and easily shaped by the adults around them.  年轻的心智易受影响，很容易被身边的成年人塑造。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 pliant</td><td>/ˈplaɪənt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>易弯曲的；顺从的</td><td>a pliant assistant<br>顺从的助手</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He wanted a pliant assistant who would never question his decisions.  他想要一个顺从的助手，永远不会质疑他的决定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 yielding</td><td>/ˈjiːldɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>顺从的，易屈服的</td><td>a yielding personality<br>顺从的性格</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her yielding nature made her an easy target for pushy salesmen.  她顺从的性格使她很容易被强势的推销员盯上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del><b>immure</b></del></td><td>/ɪˈmjʊr/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>监禁，禁闭；使闭门不出</td><td>immure himself in study<br>闭门苦读</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The scholar immured himself in the archives for the whole winter.  这位学者整个冬天都闭门待在档案馆里。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 confine</td><td>/kənˈfaɪn/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>限制</td><td>confine the discussion<br>限定讨论范围</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Please confine the discussion to the budget for this year.  请把讨论限定在今年的预算上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 constrain</td><td>/kənˈstreɪn/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>限制</td><td>constrain his freedom<br>限制他的自由</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Strict rules constrain his freedom to travel outside the country.  严格的规定限制了他出国旅行的自由。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 incarcerate</td><td>/ɪnˈkɑːrsəreɪt/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>监禁，关押</td><td>incarcerate the offender<br>关押罪犯</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The state chose to incarcerate him rather than offer treatment.  州政府选择监禁他，而不是提供治疗。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 intern</td><td>/ɪnˈtɜːrn/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>拘禁，关押；实习生</td><td>intern the prisoners<br>拘禁囚犯</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The government interned thousands of civilians during the war.  政府在战争期间拘禁了数千名平民。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 jail</td><td>/ˈdʒeɪl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>监禁；监狱</td><td>jail the thief<br>把小偷关进监狱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He was jailed for six months after refusing to pay the fine.  他因拒缴罚款被监禁了六个月。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 imprison</td><td>/ˌɪmˈprɪzən/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>关押，监禁，禁闭</td><td>imprison the rebels<br>监禁那些叛乱者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The poet was imprisoned for three years without any trial.  这位诗人未经审判被监禁了三年。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 discharge</td><td>/dɪsˈtʃɑːrdʒ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>释放；解雇</td><td>discharge the prisoner<br>释放囚犯</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The court discharged the prisoner immediately after the new evidence appeared.  新证据出现后，法庭立即释放了那名囚犯。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 release</td><td>/riˈliːs/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>释放，解除束缚；发布</td><td>release the prisoners<br>释放囚犯</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The court ordered officials to release him from custody at once.  法庭命令官员立即将他从拘留中释放。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>immutable</b></td><td>/ɪˈmjuːtəbl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不可变的</td><td>an immutable law of nature<br>不可变的自然法则</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Gravity is an immutable law that no clever argument can suspend.  引力是不可变的法则，任何巧辩都无法使它失效。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 constant</td><td>/ˈkɑːnstənt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>不变的，恒定的；忠诚的</td><td>a constant friend<br>忠诚不变的朋友</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her support remained constant through every difficult year.  她的支持在每个艰难年头都不曾改变。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fixed</td><td>/ˈfɪkst/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>固定的，不变的</td><td>a fixed monthly rate<br>固定的月费率</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Tenants pay a fixed rent for the first three years.  租户前三年支付固定租金。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 inalterable</del></td><td>/ɪnˈɔːltərəbl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不可改变的</td><td>an inalterable fact<br>不可改变的事实</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Death is the one inalterable condition of every living thing.  死亡是一切生物唯一不可改变的境况。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 inflexible</td><td>/ˌɪnˈfleksəbəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不肯变通的，僵化的</td><td>an inflexible rule<br>不容变通的规则</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The manager was inflexible about the submission deadline.  经理在提交截止日期上毫不通融。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 invariable</td><td>/ɪnˈveriəbəl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不变的，恒定的</td><td>an invariable routine<br>一成不变的常规</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His morning routine has been invariable for twenty years.  他的晨间习惯二十年来一成不变。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unalterable</td><td>/ʌnˈɔːltərəbl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不可改变的</td><td>an unalterable decision<br>不可更改的决定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The terms of the contract are unalterable once both parties sign.  合同条款一经双方签署便不可更改。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unchangeable</td><td>/ʌnˈtʃeɪndʒəbl/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不可改变的</td><td>unchangeable natural laws<br>不变的自然法则</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He treated his own opinions as unchangeable truths.  他把自己的观点当作不可改变的真理。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 alterable</del></td><td>/ˈɔltərəbəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>可改变的，可变更的</td><td>an alterable schedule<br>可变更的日程</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The contract terms are alterable only with written consent.  合同条款只有经书面同意才可变更。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 changeable</td><td>/ˈtʃeɪndʒəbəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>易变的，可改变的</td><td>changeable weather<br>多变的天气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The weather in April is notoriously changeable here.  这里四月的天气以多变著称。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 elastic</td><td>/ɪˈlæstɪk/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有弹性的；灵活的</td><td>an elastic waistband<br>有弹性的腰带</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 These trousers have an elastic waistband, so they fit almost anyone.  这条裤子有松紧腰带，几乎任何人都能穿。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 flexible</td><td>/ˈfleksəbl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>灵活的，可变的；易受影响的</td><td>a flexible schedule<br>灵活的日程</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company offers a flexible schedule to parents of young children.  公司为有幼儿的员工提供灵活的日程。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 mutable</td><td>/ˈmjuːtəbəl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>易变的，可变的</td><td>mutable rules<br>可变的规则</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Fashion is mutable, but good tailoring never goes away.  时尚易变，但好裁剪永不过时。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 variable</td><td>/ˈveriəbəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>可变的，多变的</td><td>variable costs<br>可变成本</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Winds in the strait are variable and often dangerous.  海峡里的风多变且常常危险。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>impassive</b></td><td>/ɪmˈpæsɪv/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>冷漠的，无感情的</td><td>remain impassive under pressure<br>压力下仍无动于衷</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His face stayed impassive as the verdict was read aloud.  宣读判决时，他的脸上始终毫无表情。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 affectless</del></td><td>/ˈæfektləs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无情感的，冷漠的</td><td>an affectless voice<br>毫无感情的声音</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The witness described the crime in a flat, affectless tone.  证人用平淡无情感的语调描述这桩罪行。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 apathetic</td><td>/ˌæpəˈθetɪk/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>冷漠的，无兴趣的</td><td>apathetic voters<br>冷漠的选民</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Most students seemed apathetic about the election and did not vote.  大多数学生对这次选举漠不关心，也没有投票。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 cold-blooded</td><td>/ˌkoʊldˈblʌdɪd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>冷血的，无感情的</td><td>a cold-blooded killer<br>冷血的杀手</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The court described the crime as cold-blooded and carefully planned.  法庭形容这起罪行冷血且经过精心策划。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 deadpan</td><td>/ˈdedpæn/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>不带表情的，板着脸的</td><td>a deadpan expression<br>面无表情的神情</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He told the joke with a deadpan face and nobody laughed.  他面无表情地讲了那个笑话，没有人笑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 emotionless</td><td>/ɪˈmoʊʃənləs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无感情的，冷漠的</td><td>an emotionless voice<br>毫无感情的声音</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He read the verdict in a flat, emotionless tone.  他用平板毫无感情的语调宣读了判决。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 numb</td><td>/nʌm/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>麻木的，无感情的</td><td>numb with grief<br>因悲伤而麻木</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Numb with grief, she answered the reporters' questions in a flat voice.  她因悲伤而麻木，用平板的声音回答记者的提问。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 phlegmatic</td><td>/fleɡˈmætɪk/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>冷静的，无感情的，淡漠的</td><td>a phlegmatic temperament<br>冷静的性情</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her phlegmatic response to the crisis calmed the entire staff.  她对危机的冷静反应让全体员工镇定下来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 stoic</td><td>/ˈstoʊɪk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>隐忍的，冷静的</td><td>stoic acceptance of pain<br>隐忍地承受痛苦</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her stoic acceptance of pain impressed even the veteran nurses.  她隐忍地承受痛苦，连资深护士都为之动容。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 undemonstrative</del></td><td>/ˌʌndɪˈmɑːnstrətɪv/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不外露感情的</td><td>an undemonstrative father<br>不表露感情的父亲</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Undemonstrative by nature, he simply nodded at the good news.  他天性不外露感情，听到好消息只是点了点头。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 demonstrative</td><td>/dɪˈmɑːnstrətɪv/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>感情外露的</td><td>a demonstrative greeting<br>热情外露的问候</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her demonstrative hugs surprised colleagues used to formal handshakes.  她热情的拥抱让习惯正式握手的同事很惊讶。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 emotional</td><td>/ɪˈmoʊʃənəl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>感情丰富的；动情的</td><td>an emotional farewell speech<br>动情的告别演讲</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He became emotional when describing his father's last letter.  描述父亲最后一封信时他动了感情。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 fervent</td><td>/ˈfɜːrvənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>充满感情的，热情洋溢的</td><td>a fervent believer<br>热切的信徒</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She is a fervent believer in free public libraries for everyone.  她热切地相信应该为所有人提供免费的公共图书馆。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 fervid</td><td>/ˈfɜːrvɪd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>酷热的；热情的</td><td>a fervid supporter<br>热情的支持者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His fervid speech moved even the quietest people in the room.  他热情洋溢的演讲连房间里最安静的人都被感动了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 impassioned</td><td>/ɪmˈpæʃənd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>慷慨激昂的，充满激情的</td><td>an impassioned speech<br>慷慨激昂的演讲</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She gave an impassioned defense of public libraries.  她慷慨激昂地为公共图书馆辩护。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 passionate</td><td>/ˈpæʃənət/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>热情的，充满激情的</td><td>a passionate defense of art<br>对艺术的热情辩护</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She is passionate about protecting the city's old neighborhoods.  她对保护城市的老街区充满热情。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 vehement</td><td>/ˈviːəmənt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>（情感）强烈的，热情的</td><td>vehement opposition<br>强烈的反对</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She made a vehement protest against the new tax.  她对新税提出了强烈的抗议。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>impeccable</b></td><td>/ɪmˈpekəbl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无瑕的，无可挑剔的</td><td>impeccable manners<br>无可挑剔的举止</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her impeccable manners impressed even the most demanding guests.  她无可挑剔的举止让最挑剔的客人都印象深刻。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 absolute</td><td>/ˈæbsəluːt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>专制的；无限的；纯净不掺杂的；确凿的</td><td>absolute power<br>绝对的权力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The king held absolute power over his subjects.  国王对臣民拥有绝对权力。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 faultless</td><td>/ˈfɔːltləs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无瑕的，完美的</td><td>a faultless performance<br>无懈可击的表演</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her faultless pronunciation impressed everyone in the room.  她无瑕的发音让房间里的每个人都印象深刻。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 flawless</td><td>/ˈflɔːləs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无瑕的，完美无缺的</td><td>a flawless diamond<br>毫无瑕疵的钻石</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her flawless pronunciation surprised everyone in the language class.  她毫无瑕疵的发音令语言课上所有人惊讶。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 immaculate</td><td>/ɪˈmækjələt/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>洁净无瑕的；完美无缺的</td><td>an immaculate record<br>毫无瑕疵的记录</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 After thirty years of service his record remained absolutely immaculate.  服务三十年后，他的记录依然毫无瑕疵。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 indefectible</del></td><td>/ˌɪndɪˈfektəbl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无瑕的；不会衰败的</td><td>indefectible virtue<br>完美无缺的美德</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old monk was praised for his indefectible devotion to the temple.  这位老僧因对寺院无瑕的虔诚而受到赞誉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 irreproachable</td><td>/ˌɪrɪˈproʊtʃəbl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无可指责的，无瑕的</td><td>irreproachable conduct<br>无可指责的行为</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His record as a judge was irreproachable for thirty long years.  他担任法官三十年的记录无可指责。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 perfect</td><td>/ˈpɜːrfɪkt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词、及物动词</td><td>完美的；使完善</td><td>a perfect score<br>满分成绩</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He spent years trying to perfect his technique on the cello.  他花了数年时间钻研以完善自己的大提琴技巧。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 seamless</td><td>/ˈsiːmləs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无缝的；无瑕的</td><td>a seamless transition<br>无缝的过渡</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The two departments managed a seamless merger within three months.  两个部门在三个月内完成了无缝合并。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unblemished</td><td>/ənˈblemɪʃt/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无瑕的，无污点的</td><td>an unblemished record<br>无污点的记录</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She left the company with an unblemished professional reputation.  她带着毫无瑕疵的职业声誉离开了公司。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 amiss</td><td>/əˈmɪs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词、副词</td><td>出错的，不对劲的</td><td>something is amiss<br>有些不对劲</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She sensed at once that something was seriously amiss at home.  她立刻感到家里出了严重问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 defective</td><td>/dɪˈfektɪv/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有缺陷的，有瑕疵的</td><td>a defective product<br>有瑕疵的产品</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company recalled thousands of defective batteries after the accident.  事故后公司召回了数千个有缺陷的电池。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 faulty</td><td>/ˈfɔːlti/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有缺陷的，有错误的</td><td>faulty wiring<br>有故障的线路</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The fire was traced to a faulty electrical connection in the basement.  火灾被查明源于地下室一处有缺陷的电线接头。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 flawed</td><td>/ˈflɔːd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有瑕疵的，有缺陷的</td><td>a deeply flawed plan<br>严重有缺陷的计划</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics argued that the study was flawed from the very beginning.  批评者认为这项研究从一开始就有缺陷。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 imperfect</td><td>/ɪmˈpɜːrfɪkt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不完美的，有缺陷的</td><td>an imperfect copy<br>不完美的副本</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Even an imperfect solution is better than doing nothing at all.  即便是不完美的方案也胜过什么都不做。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>impecunious</b></td><td>/ˌɪmpɪˈkjuːniəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>贫穷的，没钱的</td><td>an impecunious student<br>身无分文的学生</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 As an impecunious young painter, he lived on bread and tea.  作为一个穷困的年轻画家，他靠面包和茶为生。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 beggared</td><td>/ˈbeɡərd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>一无所有的，贫穷的</td><td>a beggared family<br>赤贫的家庭</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The failed harvest left half the village beggared that winter.  歉收使那年冬天村里一半人家一无所有。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 destitute</td><td>/ˈdestəˌtuːt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>贫困的，穷困潦倒的</td><td>left destitute by war<br>因战争而穷困</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Thousands of refugees arrived destitute and without documents.  数千名难民抵达时穷困潦倒且没有证件。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 impoverished</td><td>/ɪmˈpɑːvərɪʃt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>贫困的；贫瘠的</td><td>an impoverished region<br>贫困地区</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 War left the once-prosperous city utterly impoverished for many years.  战争让这座曾繁荣的城市多年极度贫困。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 indigent</td><td>/ˈɪndɪdʒənt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>贫穷的，贫困的</td><td>indigent defendants<br>贫困的被告</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The clinic provides free care to indigent patients.  这家诊所为贫困病人提供免费医疗。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 necessitous</del></td><td>/nəˈsɛsɪtəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>贫困的；紧急的</td><td>necessitous families<br>贫困的家庭</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The charity was founded to help necessitous widows and orphans.  该慈善机构成立时旨在帮助贫困的寡妇和孤儿。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 needy</td><td>/ˈniːdi/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>贫困的；情感上过度依赖的</td><td>needy families<br>贫困家庭</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The charity distributes winter coats to needy children.  这家慈善机构向贫困儿童发放冬衣。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 penniless</td><td>/ˈpeniləs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>身无分文的，贫穷的</td><td>a penniless student<br>身无分文的学生</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He arrived in the city penniless and slept in the station.  他身无分文地来到这座城市，睡在车站里。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 penurious</del></td><td>/pəˈnʊriəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>贫困的；吝啬的</td><td>penurious circumstances<br>贫困的境况</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He spent a penurious youth in a crowded tenement.  他在拥挤的廉租房里度过了贫困的青年时期。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 threadbare</td><td>/ˈθredber/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>陈腐的；破旧的</td><td>a threadbare excuse<br>老掉牙的借口</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He offered the same threadbare excuse for arriving late again.  他又用同一个老掉牙的借口解释迟到。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 affluent</td><td>/ˈæfluənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>富裕的，丰富的</td><td>an affluent suburb<br>富裕的郊区</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They moved to a more affluent neighborhood after the promotion.  升职后他们搬到了更富裕的街区。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 flush</td><td>/flʌʃ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词、不及物动词</td><td>充裕的，有钱的；冲洗</td><td>flush with money<br>钱很充裕</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 After the bonus he was flush with money for months.  拿到奖金后他有好几个月钱都很充裕。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 opulent</td><td>/ˈɑːpjələnt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>富裕的；华丽的</td><td>an opulent hotel lobby<br>豪华的酒店大厅</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The opulent ballroom was lined with gold mirrors and velvet chairs.  这间华丽的舞厅摆满了金框镜子和天鹅绒椅。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 rich</td><td>/ˈrɪtʃ/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>肥沃的，丰富的；富有的</td><td>rich farmland<br>肥沃的农田</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The delta has rich soil ideal for growing rice.  三角洲土壤肥沃，非常适合种稻。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 wealthy</td><td>/ˈwelθi/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>富有的，富裕的</td><td>a wealthy merchant<br>富有的商人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Only the wealthy families could afford such an education.  只有富有的家庭才负担得起这样的教育。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>impede</b></td><td>/ɪmˈpiːd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>妨碍，阻碍</td><td>impede progress<br>阻碍进展</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Thick fog impeded the rescue team's progress.  浓雾阻碍了救援队的前进。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 clog</td><td>/klɑːɡ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>阻碍物</td><td>a clog on progress<br>进步的障碍</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Old regulations act as a clog on economic progress.  陈旧的法规成了经济进步的阻碍。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 embarrass</td><td>/ɪmˈbærəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使尴尬</td><td>embarrass him in public<br>在公众面前使他尴尬</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He tried to embarrass her in public and only embarrassed himself.  他想在公众面前让她尴尬，结果只是让自己出丑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 encumber</td><td>/ɪnˈkʌmbər/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>阻碍；妨碍</td><td>encumber the project<br>妨碍这个项目</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Too many rules encumber small businesses in this country.  太多规定阻碍了这个国家的小企业。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fetter</td><td>/ˈfetər/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>束缚</td><td>the fetters of tradition<br>传统的束缚</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Heavy family obligations fettered her ambition for many long years.  沉重的家庭责任多年来束缚了她的雄心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hinder</td><td>/ˈhɪndər/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>阻碍，妨碍</td><td>hinder progress<br>妨碍进展</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Poor lighting hindered the search for survivors.  照明不足妨碍了对生还者的搜寻。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 inhibit</td><td>/ɪnˈhɪbɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>抑制，阻碍</td><td>inhibit the growth of bacteria<br>抑制细菌生长</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Cold weather inhibits the growth of these tropical plants.  寒冷天气抑制了这些热带植物的生长。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 obstruct</td><td>/əbˈstrʌkt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>妨碍，阻塞</td><td>obstruct justice<br>妨碍司法公正</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A fallen tree obstructed the only road out of town.  一棵倒下的树堵住了唯一的出城道路。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 retard</td><td>/rɪˈtɑːrd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>减速，延迟</td><td>retard the growth<br>延缓生长</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Language barriers retarded the progress of the peace negotiations.  语言障碍阻碍了和平谈判的进展。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 shackle</td><td>/ˈʃækl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>束缚；限制，阻碍</td><td>shackle free trade<br>束缚自由贸易</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 New tariffs would shackle trade between the two neighboring countries.  新关税会束缚这两个邻国之间的贸易。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 stymie</td><td>/ˈstaɪmi/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>阻碍，妨碍</td><td>stymie an investigation<br>阻碍调查</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Missing records stymied the investigation for months.  档案缺失使调查停滞了数月。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 trammel</td><td>/ˈtræməl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>束缚，限制</td><td>the trammels of custom<br>习俗的束缚</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Old rules trammel the creativity of young designers here.  旧规矩束缚了这里年轻设计师的创造力。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 aid</td><td>/ˈeɪd/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>帮助，援助</td><td>come to someone's aid<br>前来帮助某人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Neighbors came to her aid within minutes of the fire.  火灾发生几分钟内邻居们就来帮忙。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 assist</td><td>/əˈsɪst/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>帮助，协助</td><td>assist with the work<br>协助工作</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Two nurses assisted the surgeon during the operation.  两名护士在手术中协助医生。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 facilitate</td><td>/fəˈsɪlɪteɪt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使变容易，促进</td><td>facilitate the discussion<br>促进讨论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new airport will facilitate tourism throughout the whole region.  新机场将促进整个地区的旅游业。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 help</td><td>/ˈhelp/</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>帮助，助益</td><td>a great help<br>很大的帮助</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Your advice was a great help during the difficult week.  在那困难的一周里你的建议帮了大忙。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>impending</b></td><td>/ɪmˈpendɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>即将发生的；逼近的</td><td>an impending disaster<br>即将来临的灾难</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Dark clouds warned the villagers of an impending storm that evening.  乌云警示村民当晚有一场暴风雨即将来临。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 approaching</td><td>/əˈproʊtʃɪŋ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>即将到来的，临近的</td><td>the approaching deadline<br>临近的截止期限</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Everyone worked late because of the approaching inspection.  由于即将到来的检查，所有人都加班到很晚。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 coming</td><td>/ˈkʌmɪŋ/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>即将到来的</td><td>in the coming weeks<br>在未来几周内</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The coming months will decide whether the project survives.  即将到来的几个月将决定该项目能否存活。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 imminent</td><td>/ˈɪmənənt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>即将发生的，逼近的</td><td>imminent danger<br>逼近的危险</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Forecasters warned that a violent storm was imminent.  预报员警告说一场猛烈的风暴即将来临。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 nearing</td><td>/ˈnɪrɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>接近的，临近的</td><td>nearing completion<br>接近完工</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The bridge is nearing completion after four years of work.  经过四年施工，这座桥已接近完工。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pending</td><td>/ˈpendɪŋ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词、介词</td><td>即将发生的；未决的</td><td>a pending decision<br>尚未做出的决定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Several lawsuits are still pending against the company.  针对该公司的几起诉讼仍未判决。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 proximate</td><td>/ˈprɑːksəmət/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>最接近的；即将到来的</td><td>the proximate cause<br>最直接的原因</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Investigators identified the proximate cause of the collapse.  调查人员查明了这次倒塌的最直接原因。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 upcoming</td><td>/ˈʌpˌkʌmɪŋ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>即将到来的</td><td>the upcoming election<br>即将举行的选举</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She is preparing hard for the upcoming championship match.  她正为即将到来的锦标赛全力备战。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 around the corner</td><td>/əˈraʊnd ðə ˈkɔːrnər/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>副词</td><td>即将发生；在附近</td><td>just around the corner<br>近在眼前</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Spring is around the corner, and the plum trees are budding.  春天即将来临，梅树已经开始发芽。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 late</td><td>/ˈleɪt/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词、副词</td><td>晚的，迟的；已故的</td><td>late in the season<br>在季节的后期</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The rain came too late to save the withered crops.  雨来得太晚，救不了枯萎的庄稼。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 recent</td><td>/ˈriːsənt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>最近的，近来的</td><td>in recent years<br>近几年来</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Recent studies suggest that short naps improve memory.  最近的研究表明小睡能改善记忆。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>impenetrable</b></td><td>/ɪmˈpenɪtrəbl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>难以理解的</td><td>impenetrable jargon<br>晦涩难懂的行话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The contract was written in impenetrable jargon that confused everyone.  合同用晦涩难懂的行话写成，让所有人都困惑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 impassable</td><td>/ɪmˈpæsəbəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不能通行的</td><td>an impassable road<br>无法通行的道路</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Heavy snow made the mountain pass impassable for a week.  大雪使山口一周内无法通行。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 impermeable</td><td>/ɪmˈpɜːrmiəbl/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不可渗透的</td><td>an impermeable membrane<br>不透水的薄膜</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Clay forms an impermeable layer beneath the reservoir.  黏土在水库下方形成了一层不透水的岩层。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 impervious</td><td>/ɪmˈpɜːrviəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不可渗透的，不可穿透的；不能被破坏的；不为所动的</td><td>impervious to water<br>不透水的</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The coating makes the fabric impervious to rain and light oil.  这层涂层使织物不透雨水和轻质油。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 impregnable</td><td>/ɪmˈpreɡnəbəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>坚不可摧的，无法攻克的</td><td>an impregnable fortress<br>坚不可摧的堡垒</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The cliffside castle was long considered impregnable by invaders.  这座悬崖上的城堡长期被入侵者视为坚不可摧。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 arcane</td><td>/ɑːrˈkeɪn/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>深奥的，难以理解的</td><td>arcane rules<br>晦涩难懂的规则</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The tax code is full of arcane rules nobody understands.  税法充满无人理解的晦涩规定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 cryptic</td><td>/ˈkrɪptɪk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>秘密的；难以理解的</td><td>a cryptic remark<br>费解的话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His cryptic note gave us no hint about where he had gone.  他那张费解的便条没有透露他去了哪里。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 enigmatic</td><td>/ˌenɪɡˈmætɪk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>神秘的，难以理解的</td><td>an enigmatic smile<br>神秘的微笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her enigmatic answer left the reporters guessing for several days.  她神秘的回答让记者们猜了好几天。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 incomprehensible</td><td>/ˌɪnkɑːmprɪˈhensəbəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无法理解的，难懂的</td><td>utterly incomprehensible instructions<br>完全无法理解的说明</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The instructions were incomprehensible to a first-time user.  这些说明对第一次使用的人来说无法理解。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 inscrutable</td><td>/ɪnˈskruːtəbəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>高深莫测的，难以理解的</td><td>an inscrutable expression<br>高深莫测的表情</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His inscrutable smile gave nothing away about his plans.  他那高深莫测的笑容丝毫没有透露他的计划。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unfathomable</td><td>/ənˈfæðəməbəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>难以理解的，深不可测的</td><td>an unfathomable mystery<br>难以理解的谜团</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His motives remained unfathomable even to his closest friends.  连最亲近的朋友也无法理解他的动机。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 ungraspable</del></td><td>/ʌnˈɡræspəbl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无法理解的；抓不住的</td><td>an ungraspable concept<br>无法领会的概念</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 To a child, infinity is an ungraspable idea.  对孩子来说，无穷是一个无法领会的概念。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unintelligible</td><td>/ˌʌnɪnˈtelədʒəbəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>难以理解的，费解的</td><td>unintelligible mumbling<br>含混不清的嘟囔</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The recording was so noisy that the speech became unintelligible.  录音噪声太大，讲话内容变得无法听懂。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 passable</td><td>/ˈpæsəbəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>可通行的；尚可的</td><td>a passable mountain road<br>可通行的山路</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The road is barely passable after the spring floods.  春季洪水过后，这条路勉强可以通行。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 penetrable</td><td>/ˈpenɪtrəbl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>可穿透的，可渗透的</td><td>easily penetrable armor<br>易被穿透的护甲</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Thin plywood is penetrable by an ordinary nail.  普通钉子就能穿透薄胶合板。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 permeable</td><td>/ˈpɜːrmiəbl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>可渗透的</td><td>a permeable rock layer<br>可渗水的岩层</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Water collects here because the rock layer below is highly permeable.  水在这里积聚，因为下面的岩层极易渗水。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 pervious</td><td>/ˈpɜːrviəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>可渗透的</td><td>pervious concrete surface<br>透水的混凝土路面</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Engineers laid a pervious pavement to reduce street flooding.  工程师铺设了透水路面以减少街道积水。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 fathomable</del></td><td>/ˈfæðəməbəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>可理解的，可探明的</td><td>hardly fathomable motives<br>难以捉摸的动机</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her reasons for leaving were not easily fathomable to us.  她离开的原因我们不容易理解。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 intelligible</td><td>/ɪnˈtelɪdʒəbl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>可理解的</td><td>barely intelligible speech<br>几乎听不懂的话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Speak slowly so your questions are intelligible to the audience.  慢点说，好让你的问题让听众听得懂。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 understandable</td><td>/ˌʌndərˈstændəbəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>可理解的；情有可原的</td><td>perfectly understandable<br>完全可以理解</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His anger was understandable given how badly he was treated.  考虑到他受到的糟糕对待，他的愤怒可以理解。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del><b>impenitent</b></del></td><td>/ɪmˈpenɪtənt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不悔悟的，死不认错的</td><td>an impenitent offender<br>毫无悔意的罪犯</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The thief remained impenitent even after hearing the victims speak.  即便听了受害者的陈述，窃贼仍毫无悔意。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 remorseless</td><td>/rɪˈmɔːrsləs/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>毫无悔意的，冷酷无情的</td><td>remorseless cruelty<br>冷酷无情的残忍</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The killer remained remorseless throughout the long public trial.  凶手在漫长的公开审判中始终毫无悔意。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 regretless</del></td><td>/rɪˈɡretləs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无悔的，不后悔的</td><td>a regretless decision<br>不后悔的决定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She left the job regretless and never looked back.  她毫无悔意地辞去工作，从未回头。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 shameless</td><td>/ˈʃeɪmləs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无羞耻心的，无耻的</td><td>shameless self-promotion<br>无耻的自我宣传</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The politician told shameless lies on national television.  这位政客在全国电视上无耻地撒谎。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unashamed</td><td>/ˌʌnəˈʃeɪmd/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不害羞的，不知羞愧的</td><td>unashamed of his past<br>不为过去羞愧</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He was unashamed about taking credit for other people's work.  他毫不羞愧地把别人的功劳记在自己名下。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unrepentant</td><td>/ˌʌnrɪˈpentənt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不悔悟的，毫无悔意的</td><td>remain unrepentant<br>仍毫无悔意</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Even after the verdict he remained completely unrepentant about the fraud.  即使判决之后，他对这场欺诈仍毫无悔意。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 contrite</td><td>/kənˈtraɪt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>（因为有罪孽或过错而感到）后悔悲痛的</td><td>a contrite apology<br>悔痛的道歉</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He looked genuinely contrite as he apologized for breaking the window.  他为打破窗户道歉时显得真心悔痛。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 regretful</td><td>/rɪˈɡretfəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>后悔的，遗憾的</td><td>a regretful glance back<br>懊悔的回望</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She sounded regretful when she declined the invitation.  她拒绝邀请时语气中带着遗憾。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 remorseful</td><td>/rɪˈmɔːrsfəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>悔恨的，自责的</td><td>look deeply remorseful<br>显得深深悔恨</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The young thief seemed genuinely remorseful in front of the judge.  这个年轻小偷在法官面前显得真心悔恨。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 rueful</td><td>/ˈruːfəl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>悔恨的，懊悔的</td><td>a rueful smile<br>懊悔的苦笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He admitted his error with a rueful smile and a shrug.  他带着懊悔的苦笑和一个耸肩承认了错误。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 penitent</td><td>/ˈpenɪtənt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>悔过的</td><td>a penitent sinner<br>悔过的罪人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The penitent thief returned the money and apologized to the shopkeeper.  那个悔过的小偷退还了钱，并向店主道歉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 9

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>imperative</b></td><td>/ɪmˈperətɪv/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>命令的，强制性的；迫切的</td><td>an imperative need<br>迫切的需要</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It is imperative that we repair the dam before the rains arrive.  在雨季来临前修好大坝是迫切必需的。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 compulsory</td><td>/kəmˈpʌlsəri/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>强制的，必修的</td><td>compulsory education<br>义务教育</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Wearing a helmet is compulsory on every construction site.  在每个建筑工地戴安全帽都是强制的。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 forced</td><td>/ˈfɔːrst/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>被迫的，强迫的</td><td>a forced confession<br>被迫的供词</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The court threw out the forced confession as unreliable.  法庭以不可靠为由驳回了这份被迫的供词。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 involuntary</td><td>/ɪnˈvɑːlənteri/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>非自愿的；无意识的</td><td>an involuntary reaction<br>不自觉的反应</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Blinking is an involuntary response to sudden light.  眨眼是对突然强光的不自觉反应。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 obligatory</td><td>/əˈblɪɡətɔːri/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>强制性的</td><td>obligatory military service<br>强制的兵役</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 In that country obligatory military service lasts for eighteen months.  在那个国家，强制的兵役要服十八个月。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 peremptory</td><td>/pəˈremptəri/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不容置疑的；专横傲慢的</td><td>a peremptory order<br>不容置疑的命令</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His peremptory tone left no room for questions or discussion.  他傲慢的语气不给任何提问或讨论的余地。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 required</td><td>/rɪˈkwaɪərd/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>必需的，规定的</td><td>required reading<br>必读材料</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A signed form is required before the trip can begin.  出行前必须提交一份签好的表格。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 clamant</del></td><td>/ˈkleɪmənt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>迫切的；喧闹的</td><td>a clamant need<br>迫切的需要</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 There is a clamant demand for clean drinking water here.  这里对清洁饮用水有迫切的需求。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 compelling</td><td>/kəmˈpelɪŋ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>极具说服力的</td><td>a compelling reason<br>令人信服的理由</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He gave a compelling reason for leaving the project early.  他给出了提前离开项目的极具说服力的理由。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 critical</td><td>/ˈkrɪtɪkəl/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>关键的；批评的</td><td>a critical moment<br>关键时刻</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Water supply is critical to the survival of this town.  供水对这座小镇的存续至关重要。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 crying</td><td>/ˈkraɪɪŋ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>迫切的，急需的；哭喊的</td><td>a crying need<br>迫切的需要</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 There is a crying need for more rural doctors.  乡村医生的需求十分迫切。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 emergent</td><td>/ɪˈmɜːrdʒənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>紧急的，突发的；新兴的</td><td>an emergent crisis<br>突发的危机</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Doctors must act fast in an emergent situation like this one.  在这样的紧急情况下医生必须迅速行动。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 exigent</td><td>/ˈeksɪdʒənt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>紧急的；苛求的</td><td>exigent circumstances<br>紧急情况</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The exigent shortage of water forced the city to ration supplies.  紧急的缺水迫使该市实行配给。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 imperious</td><td>/ɪmˈpɪriəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>傲慢的，专横的</td><td>an imperious tone<br>专横的语气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her imperious manner made junior colleagues afraid to disagree.  她专横的态度让年轻同事不敢有异议。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 importunate</td><td>/ɪmˈpɔrtʃənət/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>迫切的；胡缠的，纠缠不休的</td><td>importunate demands<br>纠缠不休的要求</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The importunate beggar followed us down the whole street.  那个纠缠不休的乞丐跟着我们走了一整条街。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pressing</td><td>/ˈpresɪŋ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>紧急的，迫切的</td><td>a pressing problem<br>紧迫的问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Housing remains the most pressing issue for the new mayor.  住房仍是新市长最紧迫的问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 urgent</td><td>/ˈɜːrdʒənt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>紧急的，急迫的</td><td>an urgent request<br>紧急的请求</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She sent an urgent message asking for immediate help.  她发了一条紧急消息请求立刻援助。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 optional</td><td>/ˈɑːpʃənəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>可选择的，非强制的</td><td>an optional extra course<br>一门选修的额外课程</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Attendance at the evening seminar is entirely optional.  参加晚间研讨会完全是自愿的。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 noncritical</del></td><td>/ˌnɑnˈkrɪtɪkəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不关键的，不危急的</td><td>noncritical patients<br>病情不危急的病人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Noncritical systems can be shut down during the upgrade.  非关键系统可以在升级期间关闭。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unimportant</td><td>/ˌʌnɪmˈpɔːrtənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不重要的，无关紧要的</td><td>an unimportant detail<br>无关紧要的细节</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He dismissed the objection as entirely unimportant.  他把这条反对意见视为完全不重要。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>imperial</b></td><td>/ɪmˈpɪriəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>巨大的，令人惊叹的</td><td>imperial grandeur<br>帝王般的宏伟</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The hall was decorated on an imperial scale that dazzled every visitor.  大厅以帝王般宏大的规模装饰，令每位来客惊叹。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 august</td><td>/ɔːˈɡʌst/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>威严的，庄重的</td><td>an august assembly<br>庄严的集会</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The august assembly of scholars rose when the old president entered.  当老校长进场时，这个威严的学者集会全体起立。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 epic</td><td>/ˈepɪk/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>宏大的，超凡脱俗的</td><td>an epic journey<br>宏大的旅程</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their epic journey across the desert took nearly four months on foot.  他们徒步穿越沙漠的宏大旅程花了近四个月。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 glorious</td><td>/ˈɡlɔːriəs/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>辉煌的，壮丽的</td><td>a glorious summer sunset<br>壮丽的夏日落日</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The mountains looked glorious in the late afternoon light.  傍晚的光线下群山显得壮丽辉煌。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 grand</td><td>/ˈɡrænd/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>宏大的，壮丽的</td><td>a grand staircase<br>宏伟的楼梯</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They held the ceremony in a grand marble hall.  他们在宏伟的大理石厅里举行仪式。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 imposing</td><td>/ɪmˈpoʊzɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>宏伟壮丽的</td><td>an imposing building<br>宏伟的建筑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 An imposing stone building dominates the square at the city center.  一座气势宏伟的石砌建筑主宰着市中心的广场。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 magnificent</td><td>/mæɡˈnɪfɪsnt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>壮丽的</td><td>a magnificent view<br>壮丽的景色</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 From the summit we enjoyed a magnificent view of the whole valley.  从山顶我们欣赏到整个山谷的壮丽景色。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 monumental</td><td>/ˌmɑːnjəˈmentəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>意义重大的；巨大的</td><td>a monumental achievement<br>意义重大的成就</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Building the cathedral was a monumental task that took two centuries.  建造这座大教堂是耗时两个世纪的浩大工程。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 noble</td><td>/ˈnoʊbəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>高贵的，贵族的</td><td>a noble bearing<br>高贵的气派</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The horse moved with a noble grace across the field.  那匹马以高贵的优雅姿态穿过田野。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 splendid</td><td>/ˈsplendɪd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>壮丽的；极好的</td><td>a splendid marble hall<br>壮丽的大理石厅堂</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 From the roof we had a splendid view of the harbor.  从屋顶望去，港口的景色极为壮丽。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 common</td><td>/ˈkɑːmən/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>普遍的，常见的；共有的</td><td>a common mistake<br>常见的错误</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Spelling errors are a common problem in student essays.  拼写错误是学生作文中常见的问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 humble</td><td>/ˈhʌmbl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>卑微的，地位低下的；谦逊的</td><td>humble origins<br>卑微的出身</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He rose from humble origins to lead the company.  他出身卑微，最终领导了这家公司。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 inferior</td><td>/ɪnˈfɪriər/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>低等的，次等的</td><td>inferior in quality<br>质量较差</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Customers complained that the new fabric was clearly inferior.  顾客抱怨新面料明显质量较差。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 low</td><td>/ˈloʊ/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>形容词、副词</td><td>低落的，沮丧的；低的</td><td>feel a bit low<br>感觉有点低落</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She has been feeling low since her closest friend moved away.  自从最好的朋友搬走，她一直情绪低落。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>imperious</b></td><td>/ɪmˈpɪriəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>傲慢的，专横的</td><td>an imperious tone<br>专横的语气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her imperious manner made junior colleagues afraid to disagree.  她专横的态度让年轻同事不敢有异议。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 arrogant</td><td>/ˈærəɡənt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>傲慢的，自大的</td><td>an arrogant manner<br>傲慢的举止</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His arrogant remarks cost him the respect of his colleagues.  他傲慢的言论使他失去了同事的尊重。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bumptious</td><td>/ˈbʌmpʃəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>专横傲慢的，自以为是的</td><td>a bumptious young man<br>自以为是的年轻人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her bumptious manner annoyed the older members of the committee.  她自以为是的态度让委员会里年长的成员很反感。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 haughty</td><td>/ˈhɔːti/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>傲慢的，目中无人的</td><td>a haughty manner<br>傲慢的举止</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She gave the waiter a haughty look and said nothing.  她傲慢地看了服务员一眼，什么也没说。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
