<tr><td>↳近 lofty</td><td>/ˈlɔːfti/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>崇高的；自大的</td><td>a lofty tone<br>自大的语气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She dismissed our idea with a lofty wave of her hand.  她自大地一挥手就否定了我们的想法。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 lordly</td><td>/ˈlɔːrdli/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>傲慢的；高贵的</td><td>a lordly manner<br>傲慢的举止</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He waved the waiter away with a lordly gesture.  他以傲慢的手势把侍者挥退。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 peremptory</td><td>/pəˈremptəri/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不容置疑的；专横傲慢的</td><td>a peremptory order<br>不容置疑的命令</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His peremptory tone left no room for questions or discussion.  他傲慢的语气不给任何提问或讨论的余地。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pompous</td><td>/ˈpɑːmpəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>浮夸的；傲慢的</td><td>a pompous speech<br>浮夸的演讲</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The pompous speech bored everyone in the crowded town hall.  那场浮夸的演讲让拥挤的市政厅里每个人都厌烦。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 presumptuous</td><td>/prɪˈzʌmptʃuəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>冒失的，放肆的</td><td>presumptuous of you<br>你太放肆了</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It seems presumptuous to criticize a book you have not read.  批评一本没读过的书，似乎太放肆了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 supercilious</td><td>/ˌsuːpərˈsɪliəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>高傲的，傲慢的</td><td>a supercilious smile<br>傲慢的微笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A supercilious smile crossed his face when he heard my question.  听到我的问题，他脸上掠过傲慢的微笑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 superior</td><td>/suːˈpɪriər/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>更好的，占优势的；上级</td><td>superior to the rest<br>优于其余各方</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 This model is clearly superior to the older version.  这个型号明显优于旧版。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 humble</td><td>/ˈhʌmbl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>卑微的，地位低下的；谦逊的</td><td>humble origins<br>卑微的出身</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He rose from humble origins to lead the company.  他出身卑微，最终领导了这家公司。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 lowly</td><td>/ˈloʊli/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>地位低的，卑微的</td><td>a lowly clerk<br>一个卑微的职员</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He began as a lowly assistant and rose to direct the firm.  他从卑微的助理做起，后来掌管了公司。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 modest</td><td>/ˈmɑːdəst/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>谦虚的；不大的，适度的</td><td>modest about her success<br>对成功很谦虚</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He stayed modest about his award and thanked his team.  他对自己的奖项很谦虚，还感谢了团队。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>impertinent</b></td><td>/ɪmˈpɜːrtnənt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不相关的；无礼的，鲁莽的</td><td>an impertinent remark<br>无礼的言论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The lawyer objected that the question was impertinent to the charges.  律师反对说，这个问题与指控无关。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 extraneous</td><td>/ɪkˈstreɪniəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无关的，不重要的</td><td>extraneous information<br>无关的信息</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Please delete all extraneous details from the summary.  请把摘要中所有无关的细节删掉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 immaterial</td><td>/ˌɪməˈtɪriəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无关的，不重要的</td><td>immaterial to the case<br>与本案无关</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Whether he liked the plan is immaterial to the legal question here.  他是否喜欢这个计划与这里的法律问题无关。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 inapplicable</td><td>/ˌɪnəˈplɪkəbəl/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不适用的，不相关的</td><td>inapplicable to this case<br>不适用于本案</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old rule is inapplicable to digital contracts.  旧规则不适用于电子合同。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 inapposite</del></td><td>/ɪnˈæpəzɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不恰当的，不相关的</td><td>an inapposite comparison<br>不恰当的比较</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The comparison to wartime rationing seems inapposite today.  把它比作战时配给在今天显得不恰当。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 irrelevant</td><td>/ˌɪˈreləvənt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无关的，不相干的</td><td>an irrelevant detail<br>无关的细节</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The lawyer objected because the question was completely irrelevant here.  律师提出反对，因为这个问题在此完全无关。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 audacious</td><td>/ɔːˈdeɪʃəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>大胆的，（但往往）愚勇的；令人吃惊的</td><td>an audacious plan<br>大胆的计划</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The general approved an audacious plan to cross the river at night.  将军批准了一个夜间渡河的大胆计划。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bold</td><td>/ˈboʊld/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>大胆的，勇敢的；醒目的</td><td>a bold move<br>大胆的举动</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It was a bold decision to leave a secure job.  辞去稳定的工作是个大胆的决定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 brash</td><td>/bræʃ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>愚勇的，鲁莽的</td><td>a brash young executive<br>鲁莽的年轻主管</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His brash remarks offended nearly everyone at the dinner table.  他鲁莽的言论几乎冒犯了餐桌上的每个人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 brassbound</del></td><td>/ˈbræsˌbaʊnd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>顽固守旧的</td><td>a brassbound tradition<br>僵化的传统</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The brassbound committee refused to consider any new idea.  这个顽固守旧的委员会拒绝考虑任何新想法。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 brassy</td><td>/ˈbræsɪ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>厚脸皮的，不知羞耻的</td><td>a brassy manner<br>厚脸皮的态度</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her brassy confidence annoyed the older members of the committee.  她那种不知羞耻的自信惹恼了委员会的老成员。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 brazen</td><td>/ˈbreɪzn/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词、动词</td><td>蛮横大胆的，厚颜无耻的；大胆自信地去面对或从事</td><td>a brazen lie<br>无耻的谎言</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She told a brazen lie without even lowering her eyes.  她说了个无耻的谎言，连眼睛都没垂下。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 impudent</td><td>/ˈɪmpjədənt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>放肆大胆的，无礼的</td><td>an impudent remark<br>一句无礼的话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The impudent student argued with the professor in class.  这个无礼的学生在课上与教授争辩。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 insolent</td><td>/ˈɪnsələnt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>粗野的，无礼的</td><td>an insolent tone<br>无礼的语气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The student's insolent reply to the teacher shocked the entire classroom.  这名学生对老师无礼的回答震惊了整个教室。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 germane</td><td>/dʒɜːrˈmeɪn/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有关的，适当的</td><td>germane to the discussion<br>与讨论相关</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her question was germane to the discussion and changed our plan.  她的问题与讨论密切相关，改变了我们的计划。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 pertinent</td><td>/ˈpɜːrtnənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>相关的，恰当的</td><td>pertinent to the discussion<br>与讨论相关的</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Only evidence pertinent to the discussion should be presented today.  今天只应提出与讨论相关的证据。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 relevant</td><td>/ˈreləvənt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有关系的，重要的</td><td>relevant to the case<br>与本案有关</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The judge ruled that the old letters were relevant to the case.  法官裁定那些旧信件与本案有关。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 meek</td><td>/miːk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>顺从的</td><td>a meek and quiet manner<br>温顺安静的举止</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Too meek to complain, he accepted the extra work without saying a word.  他太顺从了，不敢抱怨，一句话没说就接下了额外的工作。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 mousy</td><td>/ˈmaʊsi/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>胆小羞怯的；鼠灰色的</td><td>a mousy little woman<br>一个怯生生的小个子女人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The mousy student never raised her hand in class.  那个怯生生的学生在课上从不举手。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 retiring</td><td>/rɪˈtaɪrɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>腼腆的，不爱交际的</td><td>a shy and retiring man<br>一个害羞内敛的人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The author was a retiring man who avoided interviews.  这位作家性格内敛，从不接受采访。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 shy</td><td>/ˈʃaɪ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>害羞的，胆怯的</td><td>too shy to speak<br>害羞得不敢开口</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He was too shy to introduce himself to the visitors.  他太害羞，不敢向来访者自我介绍。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 timid</td><td>/ˈtɪmɪd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>胆怯的，羞怯的</td><td>a timid smile<br>怯生生的微笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She gave a timid smile and slipped into the back row.  她怯生生地一笑，溜进后排座位。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>imperturbable</b></td><td>/ˌɪmpərˈtɜːrbəbl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>冷静的；不慌不忙的</td><td>imperturbable calm<br>泰然自若的镇定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The pilot stayed imperturbable while the engine warning sounded.  发动机警报响起时，飞行员依然沉着冷静。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 collected</td><td>/kəˈlektəd/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>镇定的，冷静的</td><td>calm and collected<br>冷静镇定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She stayed cool and collected throughout the tense negotiation.  整个紧张的谈判过程中她都保持冷静镇定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 composed</td><td>/kəmˈpoʊzd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>镇静的，沉着的</td><td>remain composed<br>保持镇静</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She stayed composed while answering the reporters' hostile questions.  她在回答记者敌意的提问时始终保持镇静。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 cool</td><td>/ˈkuːl/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>凉爽的；冷淡的</td><td>a cool reception<br>冷淡的接待</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His proposal met a cool response from the committee.  他的提案得到委员会冷淡的回应。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 disimpassioned</del></td><td>/ˌdɪsɪmˈpæʃənd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>冷静的，不激动的</td><td>a disimpassioned observer<br>冷静的观察者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She gave a disimpassioned account of the violent quarrel.  她冷静地叙述了那场激烈的争吵。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 nonchalant</td><td>/ˌnɑːnʃəˈlɑːnt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>漫不经心的，淡然的</td><td>a nonchalant shrug<br>漫不经心地一耸肩</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He answered the accusation with a nonchalant shrug and changed the subject.  他冷淡地一耸肩回应指控，然后换了话题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unflappable</td><td>/ˌʌnˈflæpəbl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>镇定的，从容不迫的</td><td>an unflappable leader<br>沉着的领导者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Even during the emergency landing the pilot remained completely unflappable.  即使在紧急降落时，飞行员依然十分镇定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unruffled</td><td>/ənˈrʌfəld/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>镇定的，平静的</td><td>unruffled by the noise<br>不为噪音所扰</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He remained unruffled despite the angry shouting around him.  尽管周围怒吼声不断，他依然镇定自若。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 choleric</td><td>/ˈkɑːlərɪk/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>易怒的，暴躁的</td><td>a choleric temper<br>暴躁的脾气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His choleric temper frightened every new employee in the office.  他暴躁的脾气让办公室里每个新员工都害怕。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 touchy</td><td>/ˈtʌtʃi/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>易怒的，敏感的</td><td>a touchy subject<br>敏感的话题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Money is a touchy subject in that family, so avoid it.  钱在那个家里是敏感话题，别提。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>impervious</b></td><td>/ɪmˈpɜːrviəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不可渗透的，不可穿透的；不能被破坏的；不为所动的</td><td>impervious to water<br>不透水的</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The coating makes the fabric impervious to rain and light oil.  这层涂层使织物不透雨水和轻质油。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 impassable</td><td>/ɪmˈpæsəbəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不能通行的</td><td>an impassable road<br>无法通行的道路</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Heavy snow made the mountain pass impassable for a week.  大雪使山口一周内无法通行。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 impenetrable</td><td>/ɪmˈpenɪtrəbl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>难以理解的</td><td>impenetrable jargon<br>晦涩难懂的行话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The contract was written in impenetrable jargon that confused everyone.  合同用晦涩难懂的行话写成，让所有人都困惑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 impermeable</td><td>/ɪmˈpɜːrmiəbl/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不可渗透的</td><td>an impermeable membrane<br>不透水的薄膜</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Clay forms an impermeable layer beneath the reservoir.  黏土在水库下方形成了一层不透水的岩层。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 impregnable</td><td>/ɪmˈpreɡnəbəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>坚不可摧的，无法攻克的</td><td>an impregnable fortress<br>坚不可摧的堡垒</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The cliffside castle was long considered impregnable by invaders.  这座悬崖上的城堡长期被入侵者视为坚不可摧。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bulletproof</td><td>/ˈbʊlətˌpruːf/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>防弹的；无懈可击的</td><td>a bulletproof vest<br>防弹背心</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The lawyers built a bulletproof case against the company.  律师们为起诉该公司建立了无懈可击的案子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 imperishable</td><td>/ɪmˈperɪʃəbl/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不朽的，永恒的</td><td>imperishable glory<br>不朽的荣耀</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The soldiers won imperishable glory defending the bridge for three days.  士兵们守桥三天赢得了不朽的荣耀。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 indestructible</td><td>/ˌɪndəsˈtrʌktɪbəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不可摧毁的</td><td>an indestructible alloy<br>坚不可毁的合金</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their friendship proved indestructible despite years of separation.  尽管分离多年，他们的友谊依然牢不可破。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 inextinguishable</del></td><td>/ˌɪnɪkˈstɪŋɡwɪʃəbl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不可熄灭的，无法消除的</td><td>an inextinguishable hope<br>无法熄灭的希望</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She had an inextinguishable curiosity about distant places.  她对远方怀有无法熄灭的好奇心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 invulnerable</td><td>/ɪnˈvʌlnərəbəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不会受伤的，无懈可击的</td><td>invulnerable to attack<br>不受攻击伤害</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 No fortress is truly invulnerable to a long siege.  没有哪座堡垒真能经受长期围攻而不受损。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 immune</td><td>/ɪˈmjuːn/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不易被感染的，有免疫力的；不受影响的</td><td>immune to criticism<br>不受批评影响</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 No public figure is entirely immune to criticism these days.  如今没有哪个公众人物能完全不受批评的影响。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 insusceptible</del></td><td>/ˌɪnsəˈseptəbl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不易受影响的</td><td>insusceptible to flattery<br>不为奉承所动</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old general seemed insusceptible to fear of any kind.  这位老将军似乎不受任何恐惧的影响。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unaffected</td><td>/ˌʌnəˈfektɪd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不受影响的</td><td>unaffected by the crisis<br>未受危机影响</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Northern farms remained largely unaffected by the drought.  北方的农场基本未受这场旱灾影响。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unresponsive</td><td>/ˌʌnrɪˈspɑːnsɪv/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>冷淡的，无反应的</td><td>an unresponsive audience<br>毫无反应的观众</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The comedian struggled before a cold, unresponsive audience.  这位喜剧演员面对冷淡无反应的观众很吃力。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 passable</td><td>/ˈpæsəbəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>可通行的；尚可的</td><td>a passable mountain road<br>可通行的山路</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The road is barely passable after the spring floods.  春季洪水过后，这条路勉强可以通行。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 penetrable</td><td>/ˈpenɪtrəbl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>可穿透的，可渗透的</td><td>easily penetrable armor<br>易被穿透的护甲</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Thin plywood is penetrable by an ordinary nail.  普通钉子就能穿透薄胶合板。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 permeable</td><td>/ˈpɜːrmiəbl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>可渗透的</td><td>a permeable rock layer<br>可渗水的岩层</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Water collects here because the rock layer below is highly permeable.  水在这里积聚，因为下面的岩层极易渗水。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 pervious</td><td>/ˈpɜːrviəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>可渗透的</td><td>pervious concrete surface<br>透水的混凝土路面</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Engineers laid a pervious pavement to reduce street flooding.  工程师铺设了透水路面以减少街道积水。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 delicate</td><td>/ˈdeləkət/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>微妙的，精细的；脆弱的</td><td>a delicate distinction<br>微妙的区别</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She has a delicate ear for the subtle differences between dialects.  她对方言之间的细微差别有很灵敏的耳朵。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 sensitive</td><td>/ˈsensətɪv/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>敏感的，灵敏的</td><td>sensitive to light<br>对光敏感</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 This instrument is sensitive enough to detect the faintest vibration.  这台仪器灵敏到能测出最微弱的震动。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 vulnerable</td><td>/ˈvʌlnərəbl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>易受攻击的，脆弱的</td><td>vulnerable to attack<br>易受攻击</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Wooden houses on the ridge are especially vulnerable to summer fires.  山脊上的木屋在夏季尤其容易受火灾侵袭。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 ductile</td><td>/ˈdʌktaɪl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有延展性的；易受影响的</td><td>ductile metal<br>有延展性的金属</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Young recruits are ductile and quickly absorb the habits of the older soldiers.  新兵易受影响，很快就学到老兵的习惯。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 pliable</td><td>/ˈplaɪəbl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>易弯曲的，柔软的；易受影响的，温顺的</td><td>pliable young minds<br>易受影响的年轻心智</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Young minds are pliable and easily shaped by the adults around them.  年轻的心智易受影响，很容易被身边的成年人塑造。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 pliant</td><td>/ˈplaɪənt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>易弯曲的；顺从的</td><td>a pliant assistant<br>顺从的助手</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He wanted a pliant assistant who would never question his decisions.  他想要一个顺从的助手，永远不会质疑他的决定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 yielding</td><td>/ˈjiːldɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>顺从的，易屈服的</td><td>a yielding personality<br>顺从的性格</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her yielding nature made her an easy target for pushy salesmen.  她顺从的性格使她很容易被强势的推销员盯上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>impetuous</b></td><td>/ɪmˈpetʃuəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>冲动的，性急的，轻率的</td><td>an impetuous decision<br>轻率的决定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 One impetuous remark at the meeting cost him a valuable client.  会上一句冲动的话让他失去了一位重要客户。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ardent</td><td>/ˈɑːrdənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>热情的，热切的</td><td>an ardent supporter<br>热情的支持者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He remained an ardent fan of the team for fifty years.  他五十年来一直是这支球队的狂热球迷。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hasty</td><td>/ˈheɪsti/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>匆忙的，仓促的；轻率的</td><td>a hasty judgment<br>轻率的判断</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A hasty decision made in anger often costs far more later.  盛怒之下作出的轻率决定往往日后代价更大。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 headlong</td><td>/ˈhedlɔːŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词、副词</td><td>鲁莽的，草率的</td><td>rush headlong into<br>贸然冲进…</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their headlong rush to publish left several errors in the final report.  他们急于发表的草率做法使最终报告留下了几处错误。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 impassioned</td><td>/ɪmˈpæʃənd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>慷慨激昂的，充满激情的</td><td>an impassioned speech<br>慷慨激昂的演讲</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She gave an impassioned defense of public libraries.  她慷慨激昂地为公共图书馆辩护。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 impulsive</td><td>/ɪmˈpʌlsɪv/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>冲动的，鲁莽的</td><td>an impulsive decision<br>冲动的决定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Buying that car was an impulsive act he later regretted.  买那辆车是他后来后悔的冲动之举。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 passionate</td><td>/ˈpæʃənət/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>热情的，充满激情的</td><td>a passionate defense of art<br>对艺术的热情辩护</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She is passionate about protecting the city's old neighborhoods.  她对保护城市的老街区充满热情。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rash</td><td>/ræʃ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>草率的，仓促的</td><td>a rash decision<br>草率的决定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He regretted his rash decision to sell the house so quickly.  他后悔自己如此草率地决定迅速卖掉房子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 vehement</td><td>/ˈviːəmənt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>（情感）强烈的，热情的</td><td>vehement opposition<br>强烈的反对</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She made a vehement protest against the new tax.  她对新税提出了强烈的抗议。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 cautious</td><td>/ˈkɔːʃəs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>谨慎的，小心的</td><td>a cautious approach<br>谨慎的做法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Investors remained cautious until the election results were announced.  在选举结果公布前投资者一直很谨慎。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 circumspect</td><td>/ˈsɜːrkəmspekt/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>谨慎的，小心的</td><td>remain circumspect in public<br>在公开场合保持谨慎</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Diplomats stay circumspect in public and blunt behind closed doors.  外交官在公开场合谨慎，关起门来则直言不讳。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 wary</td><td>/ˈweri/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>警惕的，谨慎的</td><td>wary of strangers<br>对陌生人警惕</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Children are taught to be wary of strangers offering free rides.  孩子们被教导要警惕主动提供搭车的陌生人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>impious</b></td><td>/ɪmˈpaɪəs/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不敬神的</td><td>impious remarks<br>不敬神的言论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The villagers considered his joke about the shrine deeply impious.  村民认为他拿神龛开的玩笑极为不敬神。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 blasphemous</td><td>/ˈblæsfəməs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>亵渎神明的</td><td>a blasphemous joke<br>亵渎神明的玩笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The book was banned as blasphemous by church authorities.  这本书被教会当局以亵渎神明为由查禁。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 irreverent</td><td>/ɪˈrevərənt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不恭敬的，无礼的</td><td>an irreverent tone<br>不恭敬的语气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His irreverent humor delighted students and annoyed professors.  他不恭敬的幽默让学生开心却让教授不快。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 profane</td><td>/prəˈfeɪn/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词、形容词</td><td>亵渎</td><td>profane a sacred place<br>亵渎圣地</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Vandals profaned the temple by scrawling slogans on its walls.  破坏者在庙墙上乱涂标语，亵渎了圣殿。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sacrilegious</td><td>/ˌsækrəˈlɪdʒəs/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>亵渎圣物的</td><td>a sacrilegious act<br>亵渎神圣的行为</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Smoking inside the temple was considered sacrilegious by the villagers.  在寺内吸烟被村民视为亵渎神圣的行为。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 pious</td><td>/ˈpaɪəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>（信仰上）虔诚的</td><td>a pious old woman<br>虔诚的老妇人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The pious old woman attended the early service every single morning.  那位虔诚的老妇人每天早上都参加早祷。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>implacable</b></td><td>/ɪmˈplækəbl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>固执的；无法平息的</td><td>an implacable enemy<br>不可和解的敌人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She faced an implacable opponent who refused every reasonable compromise.  她面对一个拒绝一切合理妥协的固执对手。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 adamant</td><td>/ˈædəmənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>固执的，不可动摇的</td><td>adamant about the rules<br>对规则毫不通融</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The coach was adamant that no player would skip practice.  教练坚决要求没有球员可以缺席训练。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dogged</td><td>/ˈdɔːɡɪd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>顽强的；固执的</td><td>dogged determination<br>顽强的决心</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her dogged determination carried the project through two years of setbacks.  她顽强的决心让这个项目度过了两年的挫折。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 headstrong</td><td>/ˈhedˌstrɔːŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>任性的，固执的</td><td>a headstrong child<br>任性的孩子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The headstrong young man ignored every warning from his parents.  这个任性的年轻人对父母的每句劝告都置之不理。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 intransigent</td><td>/ɪnˈtrænzɪdʒənt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不妥协的，固执的</td><td>an intransigent attitude<br>不妥协的态度</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Both sides remained intransigent, so the negotiations collapsed.  双方都毫不妥协，谈判因此破裂。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 mulish</del></td><td>/ˈmjuːlɪʃ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>固执的</td><td>mulish refusal to listen<br>固执地不肯听</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His mulish refusal to listen frustrated everyone on the committee.  他固执地拒绝倾听，让委员会所有人都很沮丧。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 obdurate</td><td>/ˈɑːbdərət/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>固执的；冷酷无情的</td><td>remain obdurate<br>仍然固执不改</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The landlord was obdurate and refused to lower the rent.  房东顽固不化，拒绝降低房租。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 pertinacious</del></td><td>/ˌpɜːrtnˈeɪʃəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>顽固的，固执地坚持的</td><td>pertinacious in her demands<br>固执地坚持要求</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The pertinacious reporter kept calling until she got an answer.  这位执着的记者不停打电话，直到得到答复。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 stubborn</td><td>/ˈstʌbərn/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>固执的，顽固的</td><td>as stubborn as a mule<br>像骡子一样固执</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 My grandfather was too stubborn to see a doctor.  我祖父太固执，不肯去看医生。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unyielding</td><td>/ʌnˈjiːldɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不屈的，不让步的</td><td>unyielding opposition<br>毫不让步的反对</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The union remained unyielding on the question of wages.  工会在工资问题上依然毫不让步。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 determined</td><td>/dɪˈtɜːrmənd/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>坚定的，下决心的</td><td>determined to succeed<br>决心成功</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She was determined to finish the race despite her injury.  尽管受伤，她仍决心跑完比赛。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 grim</td><td>/ˈɡrɪm/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>冷酷的，不肯让步的；严峻的</td><td>grim determination<br>冷酷的决心</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He fought on with grim persistence until the very end.  他以冷酷的坚韧一直战斗到最后。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 relentless</td><td>/rɪˈlentləs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>残酷的，无情的；固执的，不肯妥协的</td><td>relentless pressure<br>无休止的压力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Relentless pressure from creditors finally forced the small firm to close.  债权人无情的压力最终迫使这家小公司关门。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 unappeasable</del></td><td>/ˌʌnəˈpiːzəbl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无法平息的</td><td>unappeasable rage<br>无法平息的怒火</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His unappeasable resentment poisoned every family gathering.  他无法平息的怨恨破坏了每次家庭聚会。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 acquiescent</td><td>/ˌækwiˈesnt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>顺从的，默许的</td><td>an acquiescent audience<br>顺从的听众</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Too acquiescent to protest, the staff accepted the new rules.  员工太顺从，没有抗议就接受了新规。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 placable</del></td><td>/ˈplækəbl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>易平息的，宽容的</td><td>a placable temper<br>容易平息的脾气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Fortunately his father was placable and forgave the mistake.  幸好他父亲脾气容易平息，原谅了这个错误。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>implement</b></td><td>/ˈɪmplɪment/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>执行，实施</td><td>implement the policy<br>执行政策</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The city will implement the new recycling policy in early spring.  市政府将在初春执行新的回收政策。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 administer</td><td>/ədˈmɪnəstər/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>执行，实施；管理</td><td>administer the law<br>执行法律</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A local committee administers the housing program.  一个地方委员会负责实施这项住房计划。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 apply</td><td>/əˈplaɪ/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>运用，应用</td><td>apply a rule<br>运用规则</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Try to apply what you learned to a real problem.  试着把学到的东西用到实际问题上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 effect</td><td>/ɪˈfekt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>影响，效果；实现</td><td>have a lasting effect<br>产生持久影响</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new rule had little effect on daily traffic.  新规定对日常交通几乎没有影响。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 enforce</td><td>/enˈfɔːrs/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>强制执行，实施</td><td>enforce the rules<br>强制执行规定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Police were sent to enforce the new parking regulations.  警察被派去强制执行新的停车规定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 execute</td><td>/ˈeksɪˌkjuːt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>执行，实施</td><td>execute the plan<br>执行计划</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The team executed the rescue plan with remarkable precision.  团队极为精确地执行了救援计划。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 invoke</td><td>/ɪnˈvoʊk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>援引；引起，造成</td><td>invoke widespread anger<br>引发广泛的愤怒</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The clumsy apology only invoked more anger among the injured workers.  拙劣的道歉只是在受伤的工人中引发了更多愤怒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 perform</td><td>/pərˈfɔːrm/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>表演；执行，履行</td><td>perform on stage<br>在舞台上表演</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The young pianist will perform on stage for the first time.  这位年轻钢琴家将首次登台表演。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 cancel</td><td>/ˈkænsəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>取消，撤销；抵消</td><td>cancel the meeting<br>取消会议</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They canceled the outdoor concert because of the heavy rain.  因为大雨，他们取消了露天音乐会。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 repeal</td><td>/rɪˈpiːl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>撤销，废除（法律等）</td><td>repeal the old law<br>废除旧法律</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Lawmakers voted to repeal the outdated statute that had confused courts for decades.  议员们投票废除了这部困扰法院数十年的过时法规。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 rescind</td><td>/rɪˈsɪnd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>废除，取消</td><td>rescind an offer<br>撤回报价</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company rescinded the job offer without explanation.  公司未加解释便撤回了聘用通知。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 revoke</td><td>/rɪˈvoʊk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>撤销，废除；吊销</td><td>revoke a license<br>吊销执照</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The court revoked his driving license after the second offense.  法院在他第二次违法后吊销了他的驾照。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 10

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>implode</b></td><td>/ɪmˈploʊd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>（使）剧烈收缩，（使）坍缩，（使）内爆</td><td>implode under pressure<br>在压力下坍缩</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The glass flask began to implode under the sudden drop in pressure.  玻璃烧瓶在压力骤降时开始内爆。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 buckle</td><td>/ˈbʌkəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>弯曲，塌陷；扣紧</td><td>buckle under the weight<br>在重压下弯曲</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The steel beams buckled when the roof collapsed inward.  屋顶向内塌陷时钢梁弯折了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 founder</td><td>/ˈfaʊndər/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词、不及物动词</td><td>创立者；彻底失败，沉没</td><td>the founder of the school<br>这所学校的创办者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The peace talks foundered on the difficult question of wages.  和谈在棘手的工资问题上完全失败。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 tumble</td><td>/ˈtʌmbəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>混乱，杂乱一堆</td><td>a tumble of clothes<br>一堆乱衣服</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The room was a tumble of boxes, books and old toys.  屋里是一堆乱七八糟的箱子、书和旧玩具。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 yield</td><td>/ˈjiːld/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>产生，出产；屈服，让出</td><td>yield results<br>产生结果</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Months of patient testing finally yielded a usable vaccine.  数月耐心的试验终于产出可用的疫苗。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 explode</td><td>/ɪksˈploʊd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>爆炸，爆发</td><td>explode with a roar<br>轰然爆炸</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old boiler exploded, shattering every window nearby.  那台旧锅炉爆炸了，附近的窗户全被震碎。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del><b>importune</b></del></td><td>/ˌɪmpɔːrˈtuːn/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>恳求，迫切请求</td><td>importune him for money<br>向他苦苦求钱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Beggars importuned travelers for coins outside the station all evening.  乞丐整晚在车站外向旅客苦苦讨要零钱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 appeal</td><td>/əˈpiːl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>上诉；恳求，呼吁</td><td>file an appeal<br>提出上诉</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her lawyers will file an appeal against the verdict next week.  她的律师下周将针对判决提出上诉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 beseech</td><td>/bɪˈsiːtʃ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>恳求，哀求</td><td>beseech him for mercy<br>恳求他宽恕</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The prisoners beseeched the governor for mercy in a long letter.  囚犯们在一封长信中恳求州长开恩。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 besiege</td><td>/bɪˈsiːdʒ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>围攻；不断烦扰</td><td>besiege the city<br>围攻城市</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Reporters besieged the minister with questions about the scandal.  记者们就丑闻不断向部长发问。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 conjure</td><td>/kənˈdʒʊr/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>恳求，央求；使浮现于脑海</td><td>conjure him to stay<br>恳求他留下</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She conjured him to stay until the doctor finally arrived.  她恳求他留下，直到医生终于赶来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 entreat</td><td>/ɪnˈtriːt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>恳求</td><td>entreat her to return<br>恳求她回来</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She entreated him to return before their mother learned of the quarrel.  她恳求他在母亲得知这场争吵之前回来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 impetrate</del></td><td>/ˈɪmpɪtreɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>恳求，祈求得到</td><td>impetrate divine mercy<br>祈求神的宽恕</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The villagers gathered to impetrate rain from the heavens.  村民聚集起来向上天祈求降雨。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 implore</td><td>/ˌɪmˈplɔːr/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>恳求，哀求</td><td>implore for help<br>苦苦求助</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She implored the judge to give her brother another chance.  她恳求法官再给她弟弟一次机会。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 petition</td><td>/pəˈtɪʃn/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>请愿书；请求</td><td>sign a petition<br>在请愿书上签名</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Thousands signed the petition to save the old public library.  数千人签署请愿书，要求保住这座旧的公共图书馆。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 plead</td><td>/ˈpliːd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>恳求；辩护，申辩</td><td>plead for mercy<br>恳求宽恕</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The prisoner pleaded for mercy in a trembling, exhausted voice.  囚犯用颤抖疲惫的声音恳求宽恕。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pray</td><td>/ˈpreɪ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>祈求，恳求</td><td>pray for mercy<br>祈求宽恕</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They prayed for the safe return of the fishermen.  他们祈求渔民平安归来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 solicit</td><td>/səˈlɪsɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>请求，征求；招揽</td><td>solicit donations<br>募集捐款</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The charity solicited funds from local businesses all winter.  该慈善机构整个冬天向本地企业募捐。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 supplicate</td><td>/ˈsʌplɪkeɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>恳求，乞求</td><td>supplicate for forgiveness<br>乞求宽恕</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The prisoner knelt to supplicate for forgiveness before the military tribunal.  囚犯跪下，在军事法庭前乞求宽恕。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 demand</td><td>/dɪˈmænd/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>强烈要求；需求</td><td>demand an apology<br>要求道歉</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The angry customers demanded an apology from the store manager.  愤怒的顾客要求店长道歉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>imposing</b></td><td>/ɪmˈpoʊzɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>宏伟壮丽的</td><td>an imposing building<br>宏伟的建筑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 An imposing stone building dominates the square at the city center.  一座气势宏伟的石砌建筑主宰着市中心的广场。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 august</td><td>/ɔːˈɡʌst/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>威严的，庄重的</td><td>an august assembly<br>庄严的集会</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The august assembly of scholars rose when the old president entered.  当老校长进场时，这个威严的学者集会全体起立。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 epic</td><td>/ˈepɪk/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>宏大的，超凡脱俗的</td><td>an epic journey<br>宏大的旅程</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their epic journey across the desert took nearly four months on foot.  他们徒步穿越沙漠的宏大旅程花了近四个月。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 glorious</td><td>/ˈɡlɔːriəs/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>辉煌的，壮丽的</td><td>a glorious summer sunset<br>壮丽的夏日落日</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The mountains looked glorious in the late afternoon light.  傍晚的光线下群山显得壮丽辉煌。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 grand</td><td>/ˈɡrænd/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>宏大的，壮丽的</td><td>a grand staircase<br>宏伟的楼梯</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They held the ceremony in a grand marble hall.  他们在宏伟的大理石厅里举行仪式。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 imperial</td><td>/ɪmˈpɪriəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>巨大的，令人惊叹的</td><td>imperial grandeur<br>帝王般的宏伟</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The hall was decorated on an imperial scale that dazzled every visitor.  大厅以帝王般宏大的规模装饰，令每位来客惊叹。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 magnificent</td><td>/mæɡˈnɪfɪsnt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>壮丽的</td><td>a magnificent view<br>壮丽的景色</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 From the summit we enjoyed a magnificent view of the whole valley.  从山顶我们欣赏到整个山谷的壮丽景色。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 monumental</td><td>/ˌmɑːnjəˈmentəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>意义重大的；巨大的</td><td>a monumental achievement<br>意义重大的成就</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Building the cathedral was a monumental task that took two centuries.  建造这座大教堂是耗时两个世纪的浩大工程。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 noble</td><td>/ˈnoʊbəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>高贵的，贵族的</td><td>a noble bearing<br>高贵的气派</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The horse moved with a noble grace across the field.  那匹马以高贵的优雅姿态穿过田野。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 splendid</td><td>/ˈsplendɪd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>壮丽的；极好的</td><td>a splendid marble hall<br>壮丽的大理石厅堂</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 From the roof we had a splendid view of the harbor.  从屋顶望去，港口的景色极为壮丽。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 common</td><td>/ˈkɑːmən/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>普遍的，常见的；共有的</td><td>a common mistake<br>常见的错误</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Spelling errors are a common problem in student essays.  拼写错误是学生作文中常见的问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 humble</td><td>/ˈhʌmbl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>卑微的，地位低下的；谦逊的</td><td>humble origins<br>卑微的出身</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He rose from humble origins to lead the company.  他出身卑微，最终领导了这家公司。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 inferior</td><td>/ɪnˈfɪriər/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>低等的，次等的</td><td>inferior in quality<br>质量较差</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Customers complained that the new fabric was clearly inferior.  顾客抱怨新面料明显质量较差。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 low</td><td>/ˈloʊ/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>形容词、副词</td><td>低落的，沮丧的；低的</td><td>feel a bit low<br>感觉有点低落</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She has been feeling low since her closest friend moved away.  自从最好的朋友搬走，她一直情绪低落。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>impostor</b></td><td>/ɪmˈpɑːstər/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>冒充者，骗子</td><td>unmask an impostor<br>揭穿冒充者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The charming visitor turned out to be an impostor with forged papers.  那位迷人的来客原来是个持伪造证件的冒充者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 charlatan</td><td>/ˈʃɑːrlətən/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>骗子，装懂的人</td><td>expose a charlatan<br>揭穿骗子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The journalist exposed the charlatan selling fake medicine.  记者揭穿了那个卖假药的骗子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fake</td><td>/ˈfeɪk/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词、动词</td><td>假的，伪造的；伪造品</td><td>a fake diploma<br>假文凭</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The gallery unknowingly displayed a fake for almost ten years.  这家画廊不知情地展出了一件赝品近十年。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fraud</td><td>/ˈfrɔːd/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>骗子；欺诈行为</td><td>expose a fraud<br>揭露骗局</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The so-called healer turned out to be an outright fraud.  那位所谓的治疗师原来是个彻头彻尾的骗子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hoaxer</td><td>/ˈhoʊksər/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>骗子，行骗恶作剧者</td><td>an anonymous hoaxer<br>匿名的行骗者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A hoaxer sent fake fossils to museums for nearly a decade.  一个骗子近十年间向博物馆寄送假化石。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mountebank</td><td>/ˈmaʊntɪbæŋk/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>骗子，江湖郎中</td><td>a smooth-talking mountebank<br>口若悬河的骗子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The town square once drew mountebanks selling miracle cures.  镇中心广场曾吸引卖神奇药方的江湖骗子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 phony</td><td>/ˈfoʊni/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>假的，冒充的；骗子</td><td>a phony accent<br>假装的口音</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He used a phony name when renting the small apartment.  他租那间小公寓时用了假名。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pretender</td><td>/prɪˈtendər/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>冒充者，觊觎者</td><td>a pretender to the throne<br>觊觎王位者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Three pretenders claimed the crown within a single year.  一年之内有三个觊觎者宣称拥有王位。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 quack</td><td>/kwæk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>骗子医生，江湖郎中</td><td>a medical quack<br>冒牌医生</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The medical quack sold colored water as a cure for every illness.  那个江湖郎中把有色的水当作万病灵药出售。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sham</td><td>/ʃæm/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词、形容词</td><td>掩饰，假装；虚假的</td><td>a sham marriage<br>假婚姻</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The couple admitted their marriage was a sham arranged for the visa.  这对夫妻承认他们的婚姻是为签证安排的假婚姻。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>impotent</b></td><td>/ˈɪmpətənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无力的，无能的</td><td>impotent rage<br>无能的愤怒</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Without funding the committee was impotent to change anything at all.  没有资金，委员会无力改变任何事情。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hamstrung</td><td>/ˈhæmstrʌŋ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>受制约的，无力施展的</td><td>hamstrung by regulations<br>受规章束缚</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The agency was hamstrung by a lack of funding.  该机构因缺乏资金而无力施展。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 handcuffed</td><td>/ˈhændˌkʌft/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>被束缚的，无能为力的</td><td>handcuffed by the budget<br>受预算限制</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Teachers felt handcuffed by the rigid new curriculum.  教师们觉得被僵化的新课程束缚住了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 helpless</td><td>/ˈhelpləs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无助的，无能力的</td><td>feel utterly helpless<br>感到彻底无助</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The villagers stood helpless as the river swallowed their fields.  河水淹没农田时，村民们无助地站着。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 impuissant</del></td><td>/ɪmˈpjuːɪsənt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无力的，无能的</td><td>an impuissant government<br>软弱无力的政府</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The council proved impuissant against such powerful landowners.  面对如此强大的地主，议会显得软弱无力。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 paralyzed</td><td>/ˈperəˌlaɪzd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无力的，瘫痪的</td><td>paralyzed by fear<br>因恐惧而动不了</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The whole office was paralyzed by the sudden power failure.  整个办公室因突然停电而陷入瘫痪。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 weak</td><td>/ˈwiːk/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>脆弱的，虚弱的</td><td>a weak structure<br>脆弱的结构</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The bridge has a weak structure and cannot bear trucks.  这座桥结构脆弱，无法承载卡车。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 mighty</td><td>/ˈmaɪti/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>强大的，有力的</td><td>a mighty river<br>浩荡的大河</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A mighty oak had stood there for three centuries.  一棵巨大的橡树在那里矗立了三个世纪。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 potent</td><td>/ˈpoʊtənt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>强有力的，有效力的</td><td>a potent argument<br>有力的论据</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Fear of shame is a potent motive in small communities.  在小社群中，怕丢脸是一种强有力的动机。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 powerful</td><td>/ˈpaʊərfəl/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>强大的，有力的</td><td>a powerful engine<br>强劲的发动机</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The telescope is powerful enough to show distant galaxies.  这台望远镜足够强大，能显示遥远的星系。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 puissant</td><td>/ˈpjuːɪsənt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>强大的，有权势的</td><td>a puissant ruler<br>强大的统治者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Once a puissant empire, the kingdom now controlled two provinces.  这个王国曾是强大的帝国，如今只控制两个省。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 strong</td><td>/strɔːŋ/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>强壮的，坚固的</td><td>strong arms<br>强壮的臂膀</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The bridge is strong enough to carry heavy trucks.  这座桥足够坚固，能承载重型卡车。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>imprecise</b></td><td>/ˌɪmprɪˈsaɪs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不精确的</td><td>an imprecise estimate<br>不精确的估计</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His imprecise measurements ruined the entire batch of expensive parts.  他不精确的测量毁了整批昂贵的零件。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 approximate</td><td>/əˈprɑːksəmət/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>大约的，近似的</td><td>an approximate figure<br>一个大概的数字</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Give me an approximate cost before we sign anything.  在我们签任何东西之前，先给我一个大概的费用。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 inaccurate</td><td>/ɪnˈækjərət/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不准确的，有错误的</td><td>an inaccurate report<br>不准确的报道</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The map turned out to be badly inaccurate near the coast.  这张地图在靠近海岸处相当不准确。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 loose</td><td>/ˈluːs/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、形容词</td><td>释放，放开；松的</td><td>loose the dogs<br>放开猎狗</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The keeper loosed the hawk and watched it climb into the sky.  饲养员放开那只鹰，看它飞向天空。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 squishy</td><td>/ˈskwɪʃi/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>含糊不清的；软湿的</td><td>squishy definitions<br>含糊的定义</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The report relies on squishy terms like "soon" and "substantial".  报告依赖“很快”“大量”这类含糊的说法。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 accurate</td><td>/ˈækjərət/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>精确的，准确的</td><td>an accurate measurement<br>精确的测量</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We need accurate figures before making the final decision.  在做最终决定前我们需要准确的数字。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 exact</td><td>/ɪɡˈzækt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、形容词</td><td>强索，征收；精确的</td><td>exact a heavy tax<br>征收重税</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The conquerors exacted tribute from every village in the valley.  征服者向谷中每个村庄强征贡赋。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 precise</td><td>/prɪˈsaɪs/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>精确的，明确的</td><td>precise measurements<br>精确的测量</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We need precise figures before we can approve the budget.  批准预算前我们需要精确的数字。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 veracious</del></td><td>/vəˈreɪʃəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>真实准确的；诚实的</td><td>a veracious witness<br>诚实的证人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The court relied on one veracious witness to settle the case.  法庭依靠一位诚实的证人来了结此案。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>impromptu</b></td><td>/ɪmˈprɑːmptuː/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>即席的表演；即席的，即兴的</td><td>an impromptu performance<br>即兴表演</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The band gave an impromptu concert in the town square.  乐队在镇广场上举行了一场即兴音乐会。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 improvisation</td><td>/ˌɪmprɑvɪˈzeɪʃən/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>即兴创作，即席之作</td><td>jazz improvisation<br>爵士即兴演奏</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His solo was a brilliant improvisation on an old melody.  他的独奏是对一段古老旋律的精彩即兴演绎。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 extemporization</del></td><td>/ɪkˌstempərəˈzeɪʃən/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>即兴创作，即席发挥</td><td>brilliant extemporization<br>精彩的即兴发挥</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The lecture was pure extemporization, yet perfectly organized.  这场讲座纯属即兴发挥，却条理分明。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ad-lib</td><td>/ˌædˈlɪb/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词、动词</td><td>即兴的；即兴表演</td><td>an ad-lib remark<br>即兴的评论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The host had to ad-lib for ten minutes when the guest was late.  嘉宾迟到时主持人只好即兴撑了十分钟。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 extemporary</del></td><td>/ɪkˈstɛmpəˌrɛri/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>即席的，无准备的</td><td>an extemporary speech<br>即席演讲</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She gave an extemporary talk that impressed the whole audience.  她做了一场即兴演讲，令全场听众印象深刻。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 improvised</td><td>/ˈɪmprəˌvaɪzd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>即兴的，临时凑成的</td><td>an improvised speech<br>即兴的演讲</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They slept in an improvised shelter made of branches.  他们睡在用树枝临时搭成的棚子里。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 offhand</td><td>/ˌɔːfˈhænd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词、副词</td><td>即兴的，没有准备的</td><td>an offhand remark<br>随口的评论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 An offhand remark about the budget caused three days of angry headlines.  一句关于预算的随口评论引来三天的愤怒头条。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unplanned</td><td>/ənˈplænd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无计划的，意外的</td><td>an unplanned detour<br>计划外的绕行</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 An unplanned stop in the village became the trip's highlight.  在村里的一次临时停留成了旅行的亮点。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unpremeditated</td><td>/ˌʌnpriˈmedɪteɪtɪd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>非预谋的，未事先计划的</td><td>an unpremeditated attack<br>非预谋的袭击</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The court decided the killing was sudden and unpremeditated.  法庭认定这起杀人是突发且非预谋的。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unprepared</td><td>/ˌʌnpriˈperd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>没有准备的</td><td>caught unprepared<br>措手不及</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The city was completely unprepared for such heavy snowfall.  这座城市对如此大的降雪毫无准备。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unrehearsed</td><td>/ˌʌnrɪˈhɜːrst/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>未经排练的，即兴的</td><td>an unrehearsed performance<br>未经排练的表演</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her unrehearsed answer was more convincing than any script.  她未经准备的回答比任何稿子都更有说服力。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 considered</td><td>/kənˈsɪdərd/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>经过深思的，慎重的</td><td>a considered opinion<br>深思后的意见</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 That is my considered judgment after reading the whole report.  这是我读完整份报告后的慎重判断。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 planned</td><td>/ˈplænd/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>计划好的，有计划的</td><td>a carefully planned trip<br>精心策划的旅行</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The whole ceremony was planned down to the last detail.  整个典礼被计划到了最后一个细节。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 premeditated</td><td>/priˈmedəˌteɪtɪd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>预谋的，事先策划的</td><td>premeditated murder<br>预谋杀人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Prosecutors argued that the robbery was carefully premeditated.  检察官认为这起抢劫是精心预谋的。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 prepared</td><td>/priˈperd/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>准备好的</td><td>be well prepared<br>做好充分准备</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Come prepared with questions for tomorrow's interview.  为明天的面试准备好问题再来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 rehearsed</td><td>/rɪˈhɜːrst/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>排练过的，事先准备好的</td><td>a carefully rehearsed answer<br>精心准备的回答</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her apology sounded rehearsed rather than sincere.  她的道歉听起来像是排练过的，而不真诚。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>improvise</b></td><td>/ˈɪmprəvaɪz/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>即兴而作</td><td>improvise a solution<br>临时想出办法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The pianist improvised for ten minutes when the singer arrived late.  歌手迟到时，钢琴家即兴演奏了十分钟。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ad-lib</td><td>/ˌædˈlɪb/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词、动词</td><td>即兴的；即兴表演</td><td>an ad-lib remark<br>即兴的评论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The host had to ad-lib for ten minutes when the guest was late.  嘉宾迟到时主持人只好即兴撑了十分钟。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 extemporize</del></td><td>/ɪkˈstempəraɪz/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>即兴发言，即兴创作</td><td>extemporize a speech<br>即兴发表演讲</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Asked to speak, the mayor extemporized a short but moving speech.  被请上台后，市长即兴发表了一篇简短而动人的演讲。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 plan</td><td>/ˈplæn/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>计划，筹划；方案</td><td>plan every detail<br>计划好每个细节</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They planned the whole ceremony months in advance.  他们提前几个月就筹划好了整个典礼。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 premeditate</del></td><td>/prɪˈmedɪteɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>预先考虑</td><td>premeditate the crime<br>预谋犯罪</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The lawyer argued that his client did not premeditate the attack.  律师主张他的委托人并未预先谋划这场袭击。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>imprudent</b></td><td>/ɪmˈpruːdnt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不明智的</td><td>an imprudent investment<br>不明智的投资</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Borrowing so much money at that rate was clearly imprudent.  以那样的利率借这么多钱显然是不明智的。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 impolitic</td><td>/ɪmˈpɑːlətɪk/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不明智的，不策略的</td><td>an impolitic question<br>不策略的提问</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It was impolitic to mention his failure at the party.  在聚会上提他的失败很不明智。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 inadvisable</td><td>/ˌɪnədˈvaɪzəbəl/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不明智的，不可取的</td><td>inadvisable at this stage<br>现阶段不可取</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Doctors said surgery was inadvisable for a patient so weak.  医生说对如此虚弱的病人手术并不可取。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 indelicate</td><td>/ɪnˈdelɪkət/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不雅的，粗俗的</td><td>an indelicate question<br>不雅的问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Asking about her salary was an indelicate question at the party.  在聚会上问她的薪水是个不得体的问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 injudicious</td><td>/ˌɪndʒuˈdɪʃəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不明智的，欠考虑的</td><td>an injudicious remark<br>欠考虑的言论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 One injudicious comment cost him the whole negotiation.  一句欠考虑的话让他输掉了整场谈判。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 tactless</td><td>/ˈtæktləs/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不机智的，笨拙的</td><td>a tactless comment<br>不得体的评论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 One tactless comment about her age spoiled the whole evening.  一句关于她年龄的不得体评论毁了整个晚上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 undiplomatic</td><td>/ˌʌndɪpləˈmætɪk/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不圆通的，不得体的</td><td>an undiplomatic refusal<br>不得体的拒绝</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His undiplomatic email offended half the department at once.  他不得体的邮件一下惹恼了半个部门。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unwise</td><td>/ʌnˈwaɪz/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不明智的，愚蠢的</td><td>an unwise decision<br>不明智的决定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Borrowing more money now would be deeply unwise.  现在再借钱是很不明智的。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 advisable</td><td>/ədˈvaɪzəbəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>明智的，可取的</td><td>it seems advisable<br>这似乎是明智的</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It is advisable to book your tickets several weeks early.  提前几周订票是明智的做法。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 politic</td><td>/ˈpɑːlətɪk/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>合时宜的，明智的，高情商的</td><td>a politic silence<br>明智的沉默</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It would not be politic to mention his failure at the dinner.  在晚宴上提他的失败并不明智。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 prudent</td><td>/ˈpruːdnt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>明智的；小心谨慎的，审慎的</td><td>a prudent investment<br>一笔审慎的投资</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It would be prudent to save part of every paycheck.  每份工资都存下一部分才是明智之举。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 tactical</td><td>/ˈtæktɪkəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>策略上明智的；战术的</td><td>a tactical retreat<br>策略性的撤退</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Staying silent was a tactical choice, not weakness.  保持沉默是策略上的选择，而非软弱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 wise</td><td>/ˈwaɪz/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>明智的，有智慧的</td><td>a wise decision<br>明智的决定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It was wise of her to save money before quitting the job.  她辞职前先存钱，很明智。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>impudent</b></td><td>/ˈɪmpjədənt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>放肆大胆的，无礼的</td><td>an impudent remark<br>一句无礼的话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The impudent student argued with the professor in class.  这个无礼的学生在课上与教授争辩。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 audacious</td><td>/ɔːˈdeɪʃəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>大胆的，（但往往）愚勇的；令人吃惊的</td><td>an audacious plan<br>大胆的计划</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The general approved an audacious plan to cross the river at night.  将军批准了一个夜间渡河的大胆计划。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 barefaced</td><td>/ˈberfeɪst/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>公然的，厚颜无耻的</td><td>a barefaced lie<br>明目张胆的谎言</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He told a barefaced lie to the committee without blinking.  他毫不眨眼地对委员会撒了个明目张胆的谎。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bold</td><td>/ˈboʊld/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>大胆的，勇敢的；醒目的</td><td>a bold move<br>大胆的举动</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It was a bold decision to leave a secure job.  辞去稳定的工作是个大胆的决定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 brash</td><td>/bræʃ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>愚勇的，鲁莽的</td><td>a brash young executive<br>鲁莽的年轻主管</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His brash remarks offended nearly everyone at the dinner table.  他鲁莽的言论几乎冒犯了餐桌上的每个人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 brazen</td><td>/ˈbreɪzn/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词、动词</td><td>蛮横大胆的，厚颜无耻的；大胆自信地去面对或从事</td><td>a brazen lie<br>无耻的谎言</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She told a brazen lie without even lowering her eyes.  她说了个无耻的谎言，连眼睛都没垂下。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 impertinent</td><td>/ɪmˈpɜːrtnənt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不相关的；无礼的，鲁莽的</td><td>an impertinent remark<br>无礼的言论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The lawyer objected that the question was impertinent to the charges.  律师反对说，这个问题与指控无关。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 insolent</td><td>/ˈɪnsələnt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>粗野的，无礼的</td><td>an insolent tone<br>无礼的语气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The student's insolent reply to the teacher shocked the entire classroom.  这名学生对老师无礼的回答震惊了整个教室。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 shameless</td><td>/ˈʃeɪmləs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无羞耻心的，无耻的</td><td>shameless self-promotion<br>无耻的自我宣传</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The politician told shameless lies on national television.  这位政客在全国电视上无耻地撒谎。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 courteous</td><td>/ˈkɜːrtiəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>礼貌的，谦恭有礼的</td><td>a courteous reply<br>礼貌的答复</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Even when customers shouted, the young clerk remained calm and courteous.  即使顾客大声嚷嚷，那位年轻店员仍然平静而有礼。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 genteel</td><td>/dʒenˈtiːl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有教养的，不粗俗的</td><td>genteel manners<br>有教养的举止</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her genteel accent betrayed an expensive and very traditional upbringing.  她文雅的口音透露出昂贵而传统的成长背景。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 mannerly</td><td>/ˈmænərli/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有礼貌的，举止得体的</td><td>a mannerly child<br>有礼貌的孩子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The mannerly boy thanked everyone before he left the table.  这个有礼貌的男孩离席前向每个人道谢。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 polite</td><td>/pəˈlaɪt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有礼貌的，客气的</td><td>make polite conversation<br>客套地寒暄</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It is polite to greet your neighbors in the morning.  早上向邻居问好是有礼貌的做法。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 proper</td><td>/ˈprɑːpər/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>适当的，正确的</td><td>the proper procedure<br>正确的程序</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Please store the samples in the proper containers.  请把样本存放在合适的容器里。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
